

######## ANGLE: med_benchmarks ########
angle: Hallucination benchmarks and evaluations for medical vision-language models, centred on chest X-ray QA and report generation: what each measures, image source and labels, question format, metrics, size, access terms, use of edited/synthetic images, models and sizes tested, and whether abstention or confidence was scored; plus the nearest prior work, gaps, and reviewer criticisms for a three-source (real / edited / generated X-ray) object-existence benchmark with abstention scoring.

[1] HalluCXR: Benchmarking and Mitigating Hallucinations in Medical VLMs for Chest Radiograph Interpretation (nearest prior work) (2026; arXiv preprint cs.CV, submitted 19 May 2026) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20469
    what: 856 MIMIC-CXR-JPG radiographs stratified by CheXpert labels (normal 153 / single 212 / 2-3 findings 241 / >=4 findings 250), queried three ways: open description, targeted yes/no ('Is there evidence of [finding]? Answer YES or NO first'), and differential diagnosis; 15,408 evaluations over six VLMs (BiomedCLIP, Gemini 3 Flash, GPT-5.1, InternVL2, LLaVA-NeXT-Mistral-7B, Qwen2-VL-7B). Eight-category taxonomy; ground truth = 12 CheXpert NLP labels plus reports, with 250 human annotations by two co-authors with clinical-informatics training (not radiologists). Reports 61.9-82.3% hallucinated outputs, explicit yes-bias (Gemini and LLaVA default YES, Qwen2-VL defaults NO; balanced accuracy reported), self-reported 0-100 confidence that is badly miscalibrated (Qwen2-VL says 100% on 89% of outputs while hallucinating on 80.2%), and an ECE-weighted ensemble cutting fabrication by up to 84.8%. No edited or synthetic images; no abstention option.
    why: This is what a reviewer will cite to say 'yes/no finding-presence hallucination on MIMIC-CXR with 7B open models is done'. It also exhibits the two flaws the student's design fixes: NLP-label ground truth and no abstention. Position against it explicitly: device-level objects, by-construction labels in the edited arm, matched triplets, abstention-aware scoring, <=4B models.
    code/data: Code and data 'will be released upon acceptance'; images are MIMIC-CXR-JPG under PhysioNet credentialed access (not redistributable).

[2] MedVH: Medical Visual Hallucination Test (plus the ICLR-2024 Tiny-Paper precedent by Wu, Kim and Wu) (2024 (PhysioNet v1.0.1 Dec 2025); arXiv Jul 2024; later Advanced Intelligent Systems; dataset on PhysioNet (credentialed)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.02730
    what: Six tasks: abnormality detection, wrongful image, none-of-the-above, clinically incorrect (false-premise) questions (500 multi-choice items each), false-confidence justification (500) and report generation (200 MIMIC-CXR images). Images drawn from VQA-RAD, SLAKE, PMC-VQA, MIMIC-Diff-VQA, PathVQA and MIMIC-CXR. Models: GPT-4V, MiniGPT-v2, LLaVA-1.5, LLaVA-Med-1.5, Med-Flamingo, CheXagent, LLM-CXR. Metrics: hallucination vs baseline accuracy, a harmonic 'characterization score', CHAIR for reports. No edited/synthetic images; 'abstention' exists only as a NOTA option. Precedent: Wu et al. 'Hallucination Benchmark in Medical VQA' (arXiv 2401.05827, ICLR 2024 Tiny Papers) with FAKE 542 / NOTA 1,000 / SWAP 817 MCQs from PMC-VQA, PathVQA, VQA-RAD on LLaVA-7B/13B, LLaVA-Med and GPT-4-turbo-vision (best 55.44%).
    why: Canonical medical 'hallucination-inducing question' benchmark; the wrongful-image and NOTA tasks are the medical analogue of absent-object probes. Cite both as the medical precedents for false-premise/absent-object questions and note that neither edits pixels nor scores calibrated abstention.
    code/data: https://github.com/dongzizhu/MedVH (question files); https://physionet.org/content/medvh/ (PhysioNet Credentialed Health Data License 1.5.0: CITI course + DUA). Wu et al. code: https://github.com/knowlab/halt-medvqa

[3] MedHEval: Benchmarking Hallucinations and Mitigation Strategies in Medical LVLMs (2025; arXiv Mar 2025 (Penn State / GE HealthCare), under review) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02157
    what: Splits hallucination by cause: visual misinterpretation (9,197 closed + 490 open QA from SLAKE, VQA-RAD, IU-Xray, MIMIC-CXR), knowledge deficiency (1,972 closed + 2,317 open from MIMIC-CXR) and context misalignment (2,000 closed from MIMIC-CXR/IV). Questions built with GPT-4; human check on only 50 samples by two annotators. Metrics: accuracy, CHAIR, CheXbert, RadGraph, RaTEScore, Claude-3.5-Sonnet 0-5 judging. Eleven models (GPT-4o, LLaVA-NeXT 7B/13B, MiniGPT-4, LLaVA-Med, LLaVA-Med-1.5, LLM-CXR, Med-Flamingo, RadFM, CheXagent, XrayGPT) and seven training-free mitigations (VCD, OPERA, DoLa, AVISC, M3ID, DAMRO, PAI). No abstention/confidence scoring; no edited images.
    why: Only medical benchmark that already runs VCD and six other decoding-time mitigations on CXR VQA, so it is the baseline table your mitigation section must be compared with. Its finding that decoding fixes help little for knowledge/context errors supports your choice of grounding/retrieval as the mitigation family.
    code/data: https://github.com/Aofei-Chang/MedHEval (annotation/split files, eval scripts, mitigation code; M3ID and DAMRO re-implemented); images must be fetched separately (MIMIC-CXR via PhysioNet; SLAKE, VQA-RAD, IU-Xray open).

[4] CARES: A Comprehensive Benchmark of Trustworthiness in Medical VLMs (2024; NeurIPS 2024 Datasets and Benchmarks Track) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06007
    what: About 41K closed and open QA over 16 modalities from seven sources (MIMIC-CXR, IU-Xray, Harvard-FairVLMed, PMC-OA, HAM10000, OL3I, OmniMedVQA); closed questions come from 10-30 GPT-4 templates per format. Trustfulness = factuality + uncertainty: after answering, the model is asked 'are you sure you accurately answered the question?' and scored with uncertainty-based accuracy, an over-confidence ratio and an abstention rate. Six 7B models (LLaVA-Med, Med-Flamingo, MedVInT, RadFM, Qwen-VL-Chat, LLaVA-1.6); factuality accuracy below 50% on most sets (LLaVA-Med 40.39% average). No edited images.
    why: The only widely cited medical VLM benchmark that scores abstention/over-confidence, so your Kalai-style confidence-target scoring must be contrasted with CARES's post-hoc 'are you sure' prompt (which is unpenalized and binary). Reuse its over-confidence ratio as a secondary metric for comparability.
    code/data: https://github.com/richard-peng-xia/CARES (CC BY 4.0 code and eval scripts incl. abstention-rate calculation); images obtained per source dataset (MIMIC-CXR credentialed).

[5] Med-HallMark / MediHall Score (and the related MedHallTune, MedHallu, MedHalu names) (2024 (rev. Apr 2026); arXiv (Fudan / Tencent Youtu)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10185
    what: 7,341 samples (Conventional 2,871; Confidence-weakening 1,350; Counterfactual 1,320; Image depiction 1,800) built on SLAKE, VQA-RAD, MIMIC and OpenI, with five severity levels (catastrophic, critical, attribute, prompt-induced, minor) combined into the MediHall Score; three doctors adjudicated labels. Models: BLIP2, InstructBLIP-7B/13B, LLaVA-1.5-7B/13B, mPLUG-Owl2, XrayGPT, MiniGPT4, RadFM, LLaVA-Med. No synthetic images; no abstention scoring. Do not confuse with: MedHallTune (arXiv 2502.20780; >100K PubMed-OA images, ~509K non-hallucinated + 518K GPT-4o-generated hallucinated instructions incl. 'Nonexistent Medical Object Introduction', GPT-4o-judged on clinical accuracy/relevance/detail/risk; models incl. Qwen-VL-Chat, InternVL, LLaVA-OneVision, LLaVA-Med-1.5-7B, HuatuoGPT-Vision-7B); MedHallu (arXiv 2502.14302) and MedHalu (arXiv 2409.19492), which are text-only LLM benchmarks.
    why: Its 'confidence-weakening' and 'counterfactual' prompt sets are the closest medical analogue of the general-domain yes-bias/misleading-prompt controls; cite for severity-weighted scoring. MedHallTune's 'nonexistent object introduction' category is a text-side precedent for object hallucination; MedHallu/MedHalu belong in related work only.
    code/data: https://github.com/ydk122024/Med-HallMark (MIT; JSON question files only, images must be downloaded from source datasets). MedHallTune: https://github.com/russellyq/MedHallTune

[6] HALLUCINOGEN and MED-HALLUCINOGEN (implicit object-hallucination attacks; chest X-ray extension) (2024 (rev. Mar 2025); arXiv (Seth, Manocha, Agarwal)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.20622
    what: 6,000 visual-entity pairs: 3,000 salient objects from MS-COCO (POPE splits) and 3,000 latent X-ray/disease pairs from the NIH ChestX-ray14 test set; each image gets implicit attack prompts (identification, localization, visual-context, counterfactual) whose free-text answers are string-matched to yes/no; metric is accuracy (lower = more hallucination). Eleven LVLMs (7B-11B open models incl. LLaMA-3.2-Vision and DeepSeek-V2, plus Gemini); mitigations tested: post-prompt defense, chain-of-thought, LLaVA-RLHF, LURE. No abstention/confidence; no edited images. The GitHub release states MED-HALLUCINOGEN is 3,000 pairs x 10 prompts (5 identification, 5 localization) on NIH Chest X-rays from Kaggle.
    why: Direct bridge from POPE-style object hallucination to chest X-rays, and its images need no credentialing, which de-risks the real arm. Reuse its prompt families as the 'implicit' condition and compare your explicit existence questions against it.
    code/data: https://github.com/AikyamLab/hallucinogen (MIT; prompts, image lists, eval code). Images: NIH ChestX-ray14 (Box: https://nihcc.app.box.com/v/ChestXray-NIHCC or Kaggle mirror).

[7] HEAL-MedVQA: Localizing Before Answering (grounded hallucination evaluation with visual and textual shortcut tests) (2025; IJCAI 2025) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00744
    what: 67,904 QA pairs on about 34K chest X-rays from VinDr-CXR and MIMIC-CXR with doctor-annotated pathology masks; 45,331 closed questions of the form 'Does [anatomy] have [disease]?' split into positive and 'hallucinated' (absent-disease) questions, plus 22,573 open questions. Two robustness protocols: Textual Perturbation Test (swap the anatomy/disease term, count Yes->No flips) and Visual Perturbation Test (blend a segmented region from a different-diagnosis image onto the original). Models: GPT-4o, GPT-4V, Gemini 1.5, LLaVA-Med, CheXagent, BioMedGPT, Med-Flamingo, with VCD, Prompt Highlighter and CRG as mitigations; proposed LobA reaches F1 0.752 on MIMIC yes/no. No abstention/confidence.
    why: The closest existing 'edited-image' medical probe: it pastes a region from another image rather than diffusion-editing the same scene, so it changes the scene instead of removing an object. Use it to define your novelty precisely (same-scene removal with by-construction labels) and reuse its TPT as a text-only control.
    code/data: Paper is CC BY-NC-SA 4.0; no public data/code link found in the HTML version (treat availability as unverified); underlying images: VinDr-CXR and MIMIC-CXR on PhysioNet (credentialed).

[8] MC-CXR: Multi-Context Chest X-ray Benchmark for Context-Induced Disruption (2026; Findings of EMNLP 2026) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24118
    what: 240 MIMIC-CXR cases expanded to 2,522 paired instances with reliable vs misleading context (retrieved reports/preliminary notes; prior CXRs with overlays); constrained 12-option letter MCQ on the target finding; metrics are switch-to-wrong rate (45.6-78.1% text, 35.7-61.7% visual) and context-aligned error. Ten models: InternVL3-8B, Qwen3.5-9B, Gemma-3-12B-IT, Llama-3.2-11B-Vision, Phi-4-multimodal, MedGemma-1.5-4B, NV-Reason-CXR-3B, Claude Opus 4.7, GPT-5.5, Gemini 3.5 Flash. A board-certified radiologist set targets and overrode the CheXpert label in 26/240 cases (10.8%). No abstention allowed (invalid answers counted wrong).
    why: Shows (a) small open models incl. MedGemma-1.5-4B and a 3B CXR model are now standard rosters, (b) retrieved context can hurt (relevant to retrieval-based mitigation), and (c) a measured 10.8% NLP-label error rate you can cite. Its 'paired perturbation' statistics are a template for your matched-triplet analysis.
    code/data: Dataset to be hosted on PhysioNet (credentialed); code link not found.

[9] CheXpercept: Expert-Level Lesion Perception benchmark (2026 roster of small and large open VLMs) (2026; arXiv Jun 2026 (KAIST)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21020
    what: 10,400 items on 2,100 MIMIC-CXR-JPG images (MIMIC-ILS split), seven lesions, four stages: binary presence ('Is there any [lesion] visible in the image?'), mask-revision yes/no, contour-revision MCQ, attribute MCQ; semi-automated generation reviewed by six medical experts. Fourteen VLMs: GPT-5.4, GPT-5.4-nano, Gemini-3.1-pro/flash, Qwen3.6-27B, Qwen3.5-122B, GLM-4.6V, InternVL3.5-38B, Gemma4-31B, MedGemma-27B, MedGemma1.5-4B, HuatuoGPT-Vision-7B, Lingshu-32B, Hulu-Med-32B. Presence stage >90% for the best (Qwen3.6-27B 92.2%), later stages near chance, strong model-specific response bias documented; medical VLMs show no perceptual advantage. No abstention/confidence.
    why: Confirms that coarse yes/no presence questions are near-saturated for large models but response bias persists, so your contribution must come from the edited/generated arms, device-level objects and abstention scoring rather than raw presence accuracy. Also gives you the 2026 open-model landscape (Gemma 4, Qwen3.x, InternVL3.5, MedGemma 1.5) to pick your <=4B roster from.
    code/data: 'Code and dataset will be publicly available' (not yet verified); images are MIMIC-CXR-JPG (PhysioNet credentialed).

[10] RadFlag, ReXTrust and FactCheXcker (Rajpurkar lab: hallucination detection and tool-grounded correction in CXR report generation) (2024-2025; RadFlag: ML4H 2024 (PMLR 259); ReXTrust: AAAI-bridge AIMedHealth (PMLR 281); FactCheXcker: CVPR 2025) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.00299
    what: RadFlag samples reports at several temperatures and uses GPT-4 entailment to flag claims unsupported across samples; on a 508-study MIMIC-CXR test subset (300 calibration / 208 test) it flags hallucinations with 73% precision for MedVersa and 71% for RaDialog; clinicians found GPT-4 entailment labels 84% accurate. ReXTrust (arXiv 2412.15264) trains self-attention over MedVersa hidden states for finding-level risk: AUROC 0.8751 (0.8963 on clinically significant findings) vs RadFlag 0.7999 and entropy 0.7361 on 424 held-out studies with gpt-4o-derived labels. FactCheXcker (arXiv 2411.18672) is a query-code-update pipeline that recomputes endotracheal-tube measurements with specialised modules and rewrites reports from 11 generators on MIMIC-CXR, reducing measurement error substantially. None scores abstention; all are report-level, not yes/no VQA.
    why: These are the medical analogues of your LLM-Check-style selection signals (sampling consistency = black-box; hidden-state = white-box) and FactCheXcker is the precedent for detector/tool-grounded correction of device statements, which is the cheapest 'grounding-based' mitigation you can run. Cite ReXTrust when transferring a hidden-state score from real to edited images.
    code/data: RadFlag: https://github.com/rajpurkarlab/RadFlag; FactCheXcker: https://github.com/rajpurkarlab/FactCheXcker; ReXTrust: no code statement found. All need MIMIC-CXR (credentialed).

[11] ReXrank leaderboard, ReXVQA and ReXGradient-160K (open CXR data without PhysioNet credentialing) (2024-2025; ReXrank: arXiv 2411.15122 / PMLR 281; ReXVQA: arXiv Jun 2025 (PSB 2026 per search listing); ReXGradient-160K: arXiv 2505.00228) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04353
    what: ReXrank (rexrank.ai) ranks report generators on MIMIC-CXR, IU-Xray, CheXpert Plus and a private 10,000-study ReXGradient test set with 8 metrics; hallucination is not measured explicitly. ReXVQA is a 653,834-question benchmark (HF card; abstract says ~696K) over 160K studies covering presence assessment, location, negation detection, differential diagnosis and geometric reasoning; eight MLLMs evaluated, MedGemma-4B-it best at 83.84% vs 77.27% for three radiology residents on 200 cases; no abstention/confidence. ReXGradient-160K supplies 273,004 PNG chest X-rays (downsampled to 1/4) with reports from 109,487 patients at 79 US sites plus ~400 radiologist boxes.
    why: ReXGradient-160K is the largest CXR-with-reports set obtainable under a click-through non-commercial DUA with no stated CITI requirement, so it is the fallback real-image source if PhysioNet credentialing does not arrive in time. ReXVQA's 'negation detection' and 'presence assessment' splits give a ready comparison for your real arm and show MedGemma-4B is the expected medical baseline.
    code/data: https://huggingface.co/datasets/rajpurkarlab/ReXVQA and https://huggingface.co/datasets/rajpurkarlab/ReXGradient-160K (gated; ReXGradient-160K Non-Commercial Data Access and Use Agreement: no redistribution, research only); ReXrank: https://rexrank.ai

[12] MIMIC-CXR-VQA / EHRXQA, Chest ImaGenome and Medical-Diff-VQA (PhysioNet CXR VQA sets with device and presence questions) (2021-2025; EHRXQA: NeurIPS 2023 D&B (arXiv 2310.18652); Chest ImaGenome: PhysioNet 2021; Medical-Diff-VQA: KDD 2023 / PhysioNet v1.0.1 Feb 2025) verified=True
    url: https://physionet.org/content/mimic-ext-mimic-cxr-vqa/
    what: MIMIC-Ext-MIMIC-CXR-VQA v1.0.0 (Jul 2024): 377,391 QA from 48 seed templates (794 paraphrases) over seven content types incl. presence; yes/no 'verify' questions are 56.1% of training; attribute categories include anatomical findings, diseases, devices, tubes/lines and technical assessment; the 13,793-question test set on 500 images uses Chest ImaGenome gold labels from four radiologists. Chest ImaGenome v1.0.0: 242,072 scene graphs with 29 anatomical boxes and device annotations (endotracheal tubes, chest tubes, PICCs, pacemakers), NLP-derived, with a manually validated 500-patient gold subset. Medical-Diff-VQA: 700,703 QA on 164,324 MIMIC-CXR image pairs, 155,726 'presence' questions, rule-based from reports with 97.4% correctness on a 1,700-pair human check. All require PhysioNet credentialing (CITI course + DUA).
    why: Together these are the only public sources of radiologist-validated device/tube/line presence labels on MIMIC-CXR, i.e. the real arm's ground truth if credentialing lands. Use the ImaGenome gold subset or the MIMIC-CXR-VQA test split for real-image items to avoid the NLP-label criticism; use the 'verify' templates as your question wording.
    code/data: https://physionet.org/content/chest-imagenome/1.0.0/ ; https://physionet.org/content/medical-diff-vqa/ ; https://physionet.org/content/ehrxqa/ ; code https://github.com/baeseongsu/ehrxqa. PhysioNet Credentialed Health Data License 1.5.0 for all.

[13] VQA-RAD, SLAKE and PMC-VQA (canonical closed yes/no medical VQA sets used by every medical hallucination benchmark) (2018 / 2021 / 2023; VQA-RAD: Scientific Data 2018; SLAKE: ISBI 2021 (arXiv 2102.09542); PMC-VQA: arXiv 2305.10415) verified=True
    url: https://huggingface.co/datasets/flaviagiammarino/vqa-rad
    what: VQA-RAD: 314 MedPix radiology images (X-ray/CT/MRI, head/chest/abdomen) with 2,244 clinician-written QA (1,793 train / 451 test), about 58% closed yes/no. SLAKE: 642 CXR/CT/MRI images with 14,028 bilingual QA (HF mirror shows 14,000 rows), OPEN vs CLOSED labelled. PMC-VQA: 227K multiple-choice QA on 149K PubMed Central figures. All are small, mixed-modality, and re-used by MedVH, MedHEval, Med-HallMark, EVADE and DualRead; none contains edited images or abstention scoring.
    why: Cite as the standard closed-QA sets and use SLAKE/VQA-RAD chest subsets only as a sanity check, not as the main benchmark: their CXR coverage is tiny and their questions are not object-existence probes. Their permissive licences (VQA-RAD CC0 on the HF card; SLAKE CC BY on the HF card) make them safe for a public release of your pipeline tests.
    code/data: https://huggingface.co/datasets/BoKelvin/SLAKE ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/flaviagiammarino/vqa-rad ; PMC-VQA code (MIT) https://github.com/xiaoman-zhang/PMC-VQA

[14] Edited and generated chest X-ray sources: RadEdit (ECCV 2024), RoentMod, RadCF, RoentGen, CheXGenBench/SynthCheX-75K (2022-2026; RadEdit: ECCV 2024 (arXiv 2312.12865); RoentMod: npj Digital Medicine 2026 (arXiv 2509.08640); RadCF: arXiv Sep 2026; RoentGen: Nature BME 2024 (arXiv 2211.12737); CheXGenBench: arXiv 2505.10496) verified=True
    url: https://huggingface.co/microsoft/radedit
    what: RadEdit is a Stable-Diffusion-1.5-derived latent diffusion model (512x512) trained on 487,680 frontal CXRs (MIMIC-CXR, ChestX-ray8, CheXpert) with BioViL-T text encoding; it adds or removes abnormalities under an edit_mask/keep_mask and was used to stress-test classifiers and segmenters under acquisition, manifestation and population shift (not VLMs). RoentMod wraps RoentGen with image-to-image modification; radiologists rated outputs realistic in 93% of cases with the requested finding present in 89-99%, and it exposed 'any pathology' shortcuts in multi-task/foundation models. RadCF (GSK) builds counterfactuals by specialising a foundation image model with latent flow matching, code released. RoentGen weights are only available to MIMIC-credentialed researchers. SynthCheX-75K-v2 provides 75,649 ready-made 1024x1024 synthetic CXRs from Sana-0.6B conditioned on MIMIC-CXR-derived finding annotations (Apache 2.0), filtered with HealthGPT.
    why: No prior work evaluates VLM hallucination on diffusion-edited or generated CXRs; these tools make that arm feasible. RadEdit is the domain-specific replacement for LaMa (removal) and for the ControlNet twin; SynthCheX-75K gives a free, unpaired generated arm with known conditioning labels if on-device generation fails.
    code/data: RadEdit: U-Net weights under MSRLA (research-only), pipeline MIT, needs microsoft/BiomedVLP-BioViL-T and SDXL-VAE; RadCF: https://github.com/GSK-AI/RadCF (CC BY-SA 4.0); RoentGen: https://github.com/StanfordMIMI/RoentGen (weights by request, MIMIC credential required); SynthCheX-75K-v2: https://huggingface.co/datasets/raman07/SynthCheX-75K-v2 (Apache 2.0); RoentMod code availability not stated.

[15] Abstention and confidence in medical VQA: EVADE, CONRep and DualRead (2026) (2026; DualRead: arXiv Sep 2026; EVADE: arXiv 2608.18833 (Aug 2026); CONRep: arXiv 2602.03910 (Feb 2026)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06419
    what: DualRead freezes a GRPO-trained actor and learns a separate confidence head; backbones Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct and MedGemma1.5-4B; in-distribution SLAKE-test (1,061 q) and OOD VQA-RAD, PathVQA, PMC-VQA, OmniMedVQA, ReXVQA, VQA-Med-2019 (4,584 q); reports AUROC 0.952 on SLAKE, AURC for selective prediction, and a Counterfactual Confidence Grounding AUC that replaces the image with a real hard-negative and checks that confidence drops. EVADE lets Qwen2.5-VL-7B abstain ('escape') unless full-image and zoomed analyses agree, cutting ECE by up to 45% on VQA-RAD/SLAKE/PathVQA. CONRep applies conformal prediction to finding-level binary predictions and free-text impressions of CXR report drafters. CARES (see above) scores a post-hoc 'are you sure' abstention. None uses an explicit confidence-target instruction with a stated penalty, and none scores abstention on edited or synthetic images.
    why: These define the state of the art you must cite for 'abstention in medical VQA' and show that <=4B models (Qwen3-VL-2B, MedGemma1.5-4B) are already being calibrated. Your Kalai-style t=0.5/0.9 instruction plus Effective Reliability is distinct (explicit penalty, behavioural), and DualRead's real-image-substitution counterfactual is the nearest idea to your edited arm, so cite it and explain why same-scene edits are a stronger test.
    code/data: No code links found for DualRead or EVADE in the versions read; CONRep code not stated.

[16] Retrieval- and grounding-based mitigation for Med-LVLMs: RULE, MMed-RAG, V-RAG, VGS-Decoding, CoEV (2024-2026; RULE: EMNLP 2024 (arXiv 2407.05131); MMed-RAG: ICLR 2025; V-RAG: GenAI4Health @ AAAI 2025 (arXiv 2502.15040); VGS-Decoding: arXiv Mar 2026 (2603.20314); CoEV: MICCAI 2026 (arXiv 2606.18609)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13085
    what: RULE controls factuality risk by calibrating the number of retrieved contexts and preference-tuning on retrieval-induced errors (+47.4% factual accuracy over three datasets); MMed-RAG adds domain-aware retrieval and adaptive context selection (+43.8% over five radiology/ophthalmology/pathology sets, code released). V-RAG retrieves similar images plus text for MIMIC-CXR report generation and improves entity support and RadGraph-F1. VGS-Decoding is a training-free decoder that scores each token's visual dependency by comparing distributions on the original vs a degraded image (medical analogue of VCD; LLaVA-Med, CheXagent, MedGemma on MIMIC-Diff-VQA and VQA-RAD; up to +9.12%; 2x inference cost). CoEV is a training-free grounding verifier that checks each statement against its evidence region (+3.0 PR-AUC detection, up to -11.9% hallucination rate). Both RULE and MC-CXR show retrieval can hurt when the retrieved context is wrong.
    why: This is the mitigation family the umbrella project asks for. For one undergraduate, the executable subset is: (1) detector-grounded prompting (a lines/tubes detector's output injected as evidence, FactCheXcker-style), (2) VGS/VCD-style contrastive decoding (training-free), (3) a light image-retrieval RAG over an open CXR set with a RULE-style cap on k. Full MMed-RAG preference fine-tuning is out of budget.
    code/data: MMed-RAG: https://github.com/richard-peng-xia/MMed-RAG ; RULE: GitHub release stated; VGS-Decoding and CoEV: code 'upon acceptance'; V-RAG: none found.

[17] Device, tube, line and foreign-object sources: RANZCR CLiP, ianpan/cxr-lines-and-tubes, Object-CXR, PadChest, CheXpert, NIH ChestX-ray14, Rad-Phi4-Vision-CXR (2017-2026; RANZCR CLiP: Kaggle 2021; Object-CXR: MIDL 2020 challenge; PadChest: Medical Image Analysis 2020; CheXpert: AAAI 2019; ChestX-ray8/14: CVPR 2017; Rad-Phi4-Vision-CXR: ML4H 2025 (PMLR 297)) verified=True
    url: https://huggingface.co/ianpan/cxr-lines-and-tubes
    what: RANZCR CLiP labels about 30,083 radiographs (9,095 with device polylines) for ETT/NGT/CVC normal-borderline-abnormal and Swan-Ganz presence; the ianpan/cxr-lines-and-tubes model (EfficientNet-B1 U-Net segmentation of lines and tips, carina localisation, 5-fold presence classifier, mean AUROC 0.9684) runs via transformers with trust_remote_code, research-only weights, MIT code. Object-CXR offered 5,000 CXRs with manually annotated foreign objects plus 5,000 without, but its official site now returns 404. PadChest: >160K images with 174 findings including endotracheal tube, nasogastric tube, central venous catheter and pacemaker, 27% physician-labelled, registration and research agreement (1.02 TB; 167 MB Kaggle sample). CheXpert: 224,316 radiographs with 14 observations including 'Support Devices', 500-study test set by eight radiologists, registration. NIH ChestX-ray14: 112K images, NLP labels, open Box download (used by MED-HALLUCINOGEN). Rad-Phi4-Vision-CXR is a compact VLM with tubes/lines presence-and-placement VQA and device grounding.
    why: Devices are the ideal 'objects' for a CXR object-existence benchmark: discrete, high-contrast, removable by inpainting, radiologist-labelled in RANZCR/PadChest, and almost untouched by hallucination benchmarks. The ianpan detector doubles as (a) an automatic check that an edit really removed the device and (b) the grounding oracle for mitigation; RANZCR polylines give removal masks for free.
    code/data: RANZCR CLiP: https://www.kaggle.com/competitions/ranzcr-clip-catheter-line-classification (terms not readable in this pass); PadChest: https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest/ ; CheXpert: https://stanfordmlgroup.github.io/competitions/chexpert/ ; ChestX-ray14 paper https://arxiv.org/abs/1705.02315 ; Object-CXR challenge listing https://2020.midl.io/challenges ; Rad-Phi4-Vision-CXR https://proceedings.mlr.press/v297/ranjit26a.html (weights release not verified).

[18] Label-noise and expert-review evidence: Rafferty & Rajan (2025/26), Santomartino et al. (Radiology 2024), ReXVal, von der Stück et al. (Life 2026) (2023-2026; arXiv cs.LG (rev. Mar 2026); Radiology 2024; PhysioNet 2023 / Patterns 2023; Life (Basel) 2026) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15107
    what: Rafferty & Rajan had two experts review 240 images (120 MIMIC-CXR, 120 CheXpert, 20 per class): agreement with dataset labels was only 0.517/0.591 (MIMIC-CXR) and 0.493/0.500 (CheXpert), as low as 0.30/0.40 for pneumothorax, and in disagreements the experts matched the original report far more often than the label (61 of 71; 64 of 70); they call for clinician-validated subsets and report-aware relabelling. Santomartino et al. compared CheXpert, RadReportAnnotator, cTAKES and GPT-4 labelers on 3,665 IU and 692 MIMIC-CXR reports: 82.9-94.3% accuracy with error rates up to 15.8% +/- 5.0 in patients over 80. ReXVal: six board-certified radiologists graded 200 report pairs from 50 MIMIC-CXR studies into six error types with clinical significance. von der Stück et al. evaluated seven VLMs (GPT-4o, Gemini 2.0, Claude 3.7, Perplexity, Google Vision, LLaVA-1.6, LLaVA-Med-1.5) on 180 cases: 17.3% accuracy and 74.4% hallucination by two-radiologist consensus, and warn that curated exam-style benchmarks inflate performance and that context gains are not visual understanding.
    why: These are the exact criticisms reviewers level at medical hallucination benchmarks: NLP-derived labels, no expert review, exam-style inflation, clinical irrelevance. Your edited arm's by-construction labels, radiologist-labelled device sources, and a small clinician audit directly answer them; cite the numbers when justifying why CheXpert labels are not used as sole ground truth.
    code/data: Santomartino: https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11535863/ ; ReXVal: https://physionet.org/content/rexval-dataset/ (credentialed); Life 2026 study: https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12842777/

-- gaps_and_opportunities --
  * No medical benchmark pairs the same X-ray in real / edited / generated form with identical object-existence questions. RadEdit, RoentMod and RadCF edit CXRs but were only used to stress-test classifiers and segmenters; HEAL-MedVQA's visual perturbation blends a region from a different image; DualRead's counterfactual swaps in a different real image; MC-CXR perturbs context, not pixels. A matched-triplet design with by-construction labels in the edited arm is unoccupied.
  * Devices, tubes, lines and foreign objects are nearly absent from hallucination benchmarks: HalluCXR, CheXpercept, HEAL-MedVQA, MED-HALLUCINOGEN and MedHEval all ask about pathologies. Only MIMIC-CXR-VQA templates, Chest ImaGenome attributes, Rad-Phi4-Vision-CXR and FactCheXcker touch devices, and none measures hallucination of device presence after removal.
  * Abstention-aware scoring with an explicit confidence target and penalty has not been applied to CXR VQA. CARES uses a post-hoc 'are you sure' prompt; HalluCXR collects self-reported 0-100 confidence with no abstention option; MC-CXR forbids abstention; EVADE/DualRead/CONRep do selective prediction but not an instructed, penalised abstain. Effective Reliability on edited vs real X-rays would be new.
  * Ground-truth quality: most CXR hallucination benchmarks (HalluCXR, MedHEval, CARES) inherit CheXpert/NegBio NLP labels whose expert agreement is around 0.5 and which a radiologist overrode in 10.8% of MC-CXR cases. A removed-object edit has a certain answer, which turns the label-noise criticism into a methodological strength if the edit is verified (detector check + small human audit).
  * Threshold transfer of selection signals from real to edited/generated X-rays is untested: ReXTrust (hidden states) and RadFlag (sampling consistency) were evaluated only on real MIMIC-CXR report generation with one model (MedVersa). Showing whether a real-image threshold holds on synthetic X-rays is a small but publishable result.
  * Very small models (<=4B) are under-studied on CXR hallucination: HalluCXR used 7B-class open models; CheXpercept's smallest was MedGemma1.5-4B; only DualRead reports Qwen3-VL-2B. A systematic 2B-4B comparison on device existence with yes-bias controls fills a reporting gap.
  * Grounding vs retrieval as mitigation is not yet compared head-to-head on object existence in CXR: MMed-RAG/RULE report report-level factuality gains, VGS-Decoding reports VQA gains, FactCheXcker reports measurement gains; none isolates whether a detector-grounded prompt or retrieved reports reduce 'yes' on removed objects without increasing 'no' on present ones (the Mirage/yes-bias trade-off).

-- recommended_for_this_project --
  * Choose chest X-rays over education: the CXR side has a dense, directly comparable literature (HalluCXR, MedVH, MedHEval, CARES, HEAL-MedVQA, MED-HALLUCINOGEN, CheXpercept, MC-CXR), ready editing/generation tools (RadEdit, SynthCheX-75K), radiologist-labelled device sources, and a published roster of small open models, so the report can be positioned precisely and reviewers have known baselines.
  * Make the 'objects' devices and lines (endotracheal tube, nasogastric tube, central venous catheter, Swan-Ganz, pacemaker, chest tube/drain, foreign objects), not pathologies: they are discrete, removable by inpainting along a polyline mask, radiologist-labelled in RANZCR CLiP and PadChest, verifiable with the ianpan/cxr-lines-and-tubes detector, and almost untouched by hallucination benchmarks. Keep 1-2 coarse pathology items (e.g. pleural effusion) only as a bridge to HalluCXR/CheXpercept numbers.
  * Access plan, day one: apply for PhysioNet credentialing immediately (CITI 'Data or Specimens Only Research' course plus DUA; approval time is not documented on the pages I could open) and in parallel build the credential-free path: RANZCR CLiP (Kaggle) for device-labelled reals and masks, NIH ChestX-ray14 (open) for MED-HALLUCINOGEN-compatible items, ReXGradient-160K (click-through non-commercial DUA) as a reports-with-images fallback. If credentialing arrives by week 2, upgrade the real arm to the Chest ImaGenome gold subset / MIMIC-CXR-VQA test split; if not, the credential-free path still yields a complete study.
  * Edited arm: first try RadEdit (SD-1.5-derived, 512x512, mask-constrained removal) on the GTX 1060 in fp16 with attention slicing; if it does not fit 6 GB, fall back to LaMa inpainting over the RANZCR polyline mask dilated by a few pixels, which is the same removal operator as the general-domain plan. Verify every removal with the ianpan detector (device score must drop below the real-image threshold) and a 100-item human audit; report the audit pass rate as in RoentMod's reader study.
  * Generated arm: prefer a layout-matched twin via RadEdit full-image regeneration conditioned on the same report sentence if VRAM allows; otherwise use SynthCheX-75K-v2 (Apache 2.0, 1024x1024, known conditioning labels) as an unpaired generated stratum and analyse it separately rather than as a per-item triplet. Do not plan on RoentGen weights (MIMIC credential required) or RoentMod code (availability unverified).
  * Scoring: keep the Kalai confidence-target instruction (t = 0.5, 0.9) and Effective Reliability, and additionally report CARES's uncertainty-based accuracy / over-confidence ratio / abstention rate, HalluCXR-style balanced accuracy and yes-rate, and DualRead-style AURC so each result maps onto an existing medical table. State explicitly that no prior CXR benchmark allowed instructed abstention.
  * Mitigation family (grounding and retrieval, as the umbrella requires): (1) detector-grounded prompting: run ianpan/cxr-lines-and-tubes, inject 'A dedicated detector reports: no endotracheal tube' as evidence (FactCheXcker-style tool grounding); (2) VGS/VCD-style contrastive decoding as the training-free grounding control; (3) a light retrieval arm: BiomedCLIP/BioViL-T nearest-neighbour reports from the open training split with k capped as in RULE, to test whether retrieved text increases 'yes' on removed devices (MC-CXR predicts it can). Skip MMed-RAG preference fine-tuning (training, budget).
  * Model roster: Qwen3-VL-2B, InternVL3.5-2B, Gemma 4 E2B as in the plan, plus MedGemma-1.5-4B as the medical comparator (used by MC-CXR, CheXpercept, DualRead; verify its Hugging Face terms before download) and optionally NV-Reason-CXR-3B (seen in MC-CXR; not verified separately). Replace the SENTINEL aligned checkpoint with the medical comparator if compute is tight, since no CXR-specific SENTINEL exists.
  * Ground truth and audit: never use CheXpert/NegBio labels as sole truth for real items (expert agreement about 0.5 per Rafferty & Rajan; 10.8% overridden in MC-CXR). Use RANZCR radiologist labels, Chest ImaGenome gold, or CheXpert's radiologist-labelled test set, and recruit one clinician or radiology resident through the advisor for a 100-item audit; if none is available, say so and lean on by-construction labels.
  * Positioning sentence for the report: 'Unlike HalluCXR, MedVH and HEAL-MedVQA, which probe presence on unmodified images with NLP-derived or pasted-region labels and no abstention, we evaluate the same scene in real, device-removed and generated form, score instructed abstention, and test grounding- and retrieval-based mitigation on <=4B open models.' Cite Med-HallMark for severity weighting, CARES for abstention metrics, RadEdit/RoentMod for edit realism, ReXTrust/RadFlag for selection signals, Rafferty & Rajan and Santomartino for label noise.
  * Avoid: claiming 'first medical hallucination benchmark' (Med-HallMark 2024, MedVH 2024, HalluCXR 2026 precede); citing MedHallu/MedHalu as VLM benchmarks (text-only); using fp16 on Kaggle T4 for bf16-trained medical models; redistributing any MIMIC-derived image or question file (all PhysioNet-licensed benchmarks release only question JSONs).

-- cautions --
  * Web search budget was exhausted near the end; the following were not completed and remain unverified: RANZCR CLiP licence/terms (Kaggle page and the Scientific Data paper could not be read; image counts come from the ianpan model card), PhysioNet credentialing turnaround time (FAQ page 404), Rad-Phi4-Vision-CXR weight release and its tubes/lines F1 (PDF 404; the '0.91' figure appeared only in a search snippet), and the current download location of Object-CXR (official site 404).
  * HalluCXR, CheXpercept, MC-CXR, RadCF, DualRead, EVADE, CoEV and VGS-Decoding are 2026 preprints or just-accepted papers whose code/data are 'upon acceptance'; expect them to be unavailable during the project and cite them as positioning, not as reusable assets.
  * The paper excerpt for HalluCXR lists 'InternVL2 (6B)', which is not a standard InternVL2 size; check the paper before quoting model sizes.
  * RadEdit's fit on a 6 GB GTX 1060 (fp16, Pascal) was not verified; its U-Net weights are research-only (MSRLA) and outputs are described by Microsoft as non-photorealistic with VAE artefacts, so the human audit of edits is mandatory.
  * SynthCheX-75K is conditioned on pathology annotations; whether device/support-device conditioning exists in its captions was not verified, so it may not supply device-positive generated images.
  * CARES OpenReview reviews could not be read (verification wall); the reviewer-criticism evidence here comes from published critiques (Rafferty & Rajan; Santomartino et al.; Life 2026 study) and from MC-CXR's radiologist override rate rather than from NeurIPS review text.
  * The search-snippet claim that the CheXpert labeler scores '54.3% F1 on MIMIC-CXR' was not traced to a primary source and is not included in the items; use the verified Rafferty & Rajan agreement figures instead.
  * ReXVQA question counts differ between the abstract (~696K) and the Hugging Face card (653,834); SLAKE counts differ between the paper (642 images / 14,028 QA) and the HF mirror (586 unique images / 14,000 rows). Quote the primary paper and note the mirror.
  * All MIMIC-derived resources (MedVH, MIMIC-CXR-VQA, Chest ImaGenome, Medical-Diff-VQA, ReXVal, MC-CXR, HEAL-MedVQA's MIMIC part) require CITI training plus a DUA and forbid redistribution; an OSF pre-registration and public release must therefore contain only question files, edit masks and scripts, never images.
  * MedGemma-1.5-4B and NV-Reason-CXR-3B were seen only in other papers' model lists; their Hugging Face licences and gating were not checked in this pass.

-- confidence_notes -- Every item was checked against at least one primary page (arXiv abstract or HTML, PhysioNet or Hugging Face card, GitHub README, or conference proceedings page); numbers quoted come from those pages. Confidence is high for the benchmark descriptions, dataset sizes and access terms of MIMIC-CXR-VQA, Chest ImaGenome, Medical-Diff-VQA, MedVH, ReXVQA/ReXGradient, RadEdit, SynthCheX-75K and CARES; medium for merged sub-items where only the abstract was read (RoentMod, CONRep, V-RAG, RULE datasets); low for RANZCR terms, Rad-Phi4 numbers, Object-CXR availability and PhysioNet timing, which are flagged above. The gap analysis is inferred from the absence of matched real/edited/generated designs and instructed abstention in all papers read, not from an exhaustive search.


######## ANGLE: med_data ########
angle: Public chest X-ray (plus two other radiograph) datasets with object-level annotations that one undergraduate can obtain this week, assessed for building real / edited (object removed) / synthetic-object triplets with yes/no presence questions, under a GTX 1060 + Kaggle + under-$40 budget. Headline: the fastest non-expert-verifiable object sources are RANZCR CLiP (tubes and lines, Kaggle, instant) and Object-CXR (foreign objects, direct download); the fully redistributable base is NIH ChestX-ray14 + ChestX-Det + CheXmask; PadChest-GR is the best CXR device-box set but is request-gated; the MIMIC/PhysioNet family (VinDr-CXR, MIMIC-CXR-JPG, Chest ImaGenome, MS-CXR, MIMIC-Ext-CXR-QBA) needs CITI + credentialing (1-2 weeks typical) and forbids online-API use, so it is a gated extension; CANDID-PTX (chest tubes) is closed to non-NZ users since Feb 2024.

[1] RANZCR CLiP - Catheter and Line Position (Kaggle, 2021) verified=True
    url: https://www.kaggle.com/competitions/ranzcr-clip-catheter-line-classification (descriptor: https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8553862)
    access: Kaggle account, open the competition, accept the rules (late join), then `kaggle competitions download -c ranzcr-clip-catheter-line-classification`. No approval step; minutes plus download time. Kaggle pages are JS-rendered and could not be read here, so read the Rules tab yourself before relying on the terms.
    license_or_terms: The Scientific Data descriptor is CC BY 4.0 (verified), but that covers the article; the images are governed by the Kaggle competition rules (not readable here; the standard Kaggle clause permits non-commercial research and education and prohibits redistributing competition data). Treat edited derivatives as non-redistributable: release item ids, masks, scripts and checksums only.
    size: 30,083 chest radiographs as JPEG, 'upscaled to original resolution before conversion to JPEG' (descriptor), 50,612 image-level labels, 17,999 manual line annotations. Total download size not verified (community reports around 13 GB).
    object_level_annotations: 11 binary labels per image (ETT normal/borderline/abnormal; NGT normal/borderline/abnormal/incompletely imaged; CVC normal/borderline/abnormal; Swan-Ganz present) plus train_annotations.csv with 17,999 manually drawn polylines tracing ETT, NGT and CVC on a subset of the images; 43 labelers (radiologists, radiology trainees, hospital medical officers) at RANZCR sites; images previously de-identified.
    non_expert_verifiable_classes: Presence of an endotracheal tube, nasogastric tube or central venous catheter is verifiable by a trained non-expert with a one-page reference sheet (radiopaque lines with a characteristic course); faint or short CVCs and Swan-Ganz catheters are harder and should be flagged; the normal/borderline/abnormal position labels need clinical expertise and must not be used as 'objects'.
    prior_vlm_hallucination_use: No VLM-hallucination paper using RANZCR was found. The nearest device-hallucination work, FactCheXcker (CVPR 2025, https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.18672), studies ETT measurement hallucination on MIMIC-CXR, not RANZCR.
    fit_for_three_source_design: R: image with one polyline-annotated line type. E: LaMa fill under the polyline dilated 8-15 px at full resolution, so the line is 'absent by edit' while ECG leads and ICU context remain (a built-in prior-vs-trace probe). S: insert a line into a real negative by cut-paste of a polyline crop from another image guided by CheXmask anatomy (CPU) or by a RadEdit masked 'add' edit. Adversarial negatives come free from the 11 image-level labels (ask about line types labelled absent). Questions: 'Is there an endotracheal tube / nasogastric tube / central venous catheter / Swan-Ganz catheter in this radiograph?'

[2] Object-CXR - foreign objects on chest X-rays (JF Healthcare, 2020) verified=True
    url: https://github.com/hlk-1135/object-CXR (mirror of the original jfhealthcare/object-CXR README); torrent mirror listed at https://academictorrents.com/details/fdc91f11d7010f7259a05403fc9d00079a09f5d5
    access: README links a Google Drive folder for train + dev (link not tested here); the original jfhealthcare.github.io page is gone; an Academic Torrents mirror lists 13.64 GB (train.zip 12.13 GB, dev.zip 1.51 GB). No registration.
    license_or_terms: README: images are 'anonymousized and free to download for scientific research and non-commercial usage'. No explicit clause on derivatives or redistribution, and the provider cannot be reached, so do not redistribute images or edited images; release masks, ids and scripts.
    size: 10,000 frontal CXRs: train 4,000 with foreign objects + 4,000 without; dev 500 + 500; test 500 + 500 (test annotations were withheld for the challenge; current status unverified). JPEG; resolution not stated in the README.
    object_level_annotations: Every foreign object inside the lung field annotated as rectangle, ellipse or polygon; annotated by '12 medically-trained radiologists with 1 to 3 years of experience'; images from about 300 township hospitals in China.
    non_expert_verifiable_classes: Buttons, necklaces, pendants, underwires, zips, jewellery and similar are verifiable by anyone with a few examples; the README itself notes 'buttons are visually similar to nodules', which is exactly the confusion a hallucination probe wants. No pathology labels, so no expert classes.
    prior_vlm_hallucination_use: Included in CheXinstruct/CheXagent as the foreign-object detection source (https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.12208). The anatomy-guided shape-insertion paper (https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12022, Jan 2025) synthesises foreign objects into CXRs for segmentation training. No VLM-hallucination benchmark built on it was found.
    fit_for_three_source_design: R: positives with one compact object (0.3-5% of image area, not touching the lung border). E: LaMa fill of the dilated box/ellipse/polygon. S: a synthetic object inserted into one of the 5,000 real negatives (shape insertion or cut-paste; optionally RadEdit), giving the mirror image of E on the same anatomy. The 5,000 negatives are exact real negatives. Caveat: anything non-anatomical in the lung field counts as an 'object' (some are lines or implants), so curate by hand and label the object type.

[3] NIH ChestX-ray14 (ChestX-ray8/14) with BBox_List_2017 verified=True
    url: https://nihcc.app.box.com/v/ChestXray-NIHCC (Kaggle mirror: https://www.kaggle.com/datasets/nih-chest-xrays/data; HF mirror card: https://huggingface.co/datasets/alkzar90/NIH-Chest-X-ray-dataset)
    access: Open download from the NIH Box folder or Kaggle (account only); no approval.
    license_or_terms: NIH README as reproduced on the HF mirror card: 'There are no restrictions on the use of the NIH chest x-ray images', with attribution requirements (link to the NIH download site, cite Wang et al. CVPR 2017, acknowledge the NIH Clinical Center as data provider). Edited and generated derivatives can be redistributed with that attribution. The Box README itself was not readable here.
    size: 112,120 PNG frontal images, 1024x1024, 30,805 patients; about 42 GB (commonly cited, not re-measured).
    object_level_annotations: BBox_List_2017.csv: about 1,000 images with one radiologist-drawn box each for 8 findings (Atelectasis, Cardiomegaly, Effusion, Infiltrate, Mass, Nodule, Pneumonia, Pneumothorax); commonly summarised as 984 boxes on 880 images. No device or foreign-object annotations; the 14 image-level labels are NLP-mined and noisy.
    non_expert_verifiable_classes: None of the 8 box classes is safely verifiable without a radiologist; cardiomegaly can be approximated from the cardiothoracic ratio using CheXmask. Pacemakers, wires and lines are present in many images but unlabelled.
    prior_vlm_hallucination_use: MED-HALLUCINOGEN (3,000 NIH 'latent findings', in the project digest); cxr-grounding 'Do Medical VLMs Look at the X-ray?' (https://github.com/m871-akram/cxr-grounding; RadEdit edits of NIH images inside CheXmask contours, tested on MedGemma 4B/1.5); RadEdit's and SIIM/ChestX-Det's base images; CheXinstruct.
    fit_for_three_source_design: Open, redistributable base for the release track: CheXmask masks and ChestX-Det boxes cover it and RadEdit was trained on it. For devices the student would have to label pacemakers/sternotomy wires on NIH images (shortlist with a zero-shot classifier, then hand-check), which is a 1-2 day opportunity rather than a ready set.

[4] CheXmask (anatomical masks for 5 CXR datasets) verified=True
    url: https://physionet.org/content/chexmask-cxr-segmentation-data/1.0.0/
    access: Open access on PhysioNet ('Anyone can access the files'); per-file download so only the ChestX-ray8 CSV need be fetched.
    license_or_terms: Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 International. Contains only image ids and masks, no images.
    size: 657,566 masks as RLE in CSV; 37.3 GB uncompressed, 15.6 GB ZIP; covers ChestX-ray8, CheXpert, MIMIC-CXR-JPG, PadChest and VinDr-CXR.
    object_level_annotations: Left lung, right lung and heart masks from HybridGNet with a per-mask Dice-RCA quality score (documentation recommends keeping Dice RCA (Mean) >= 0.7); no human-verified subset.
    non_expert_verifiable_classes: Anatomy rather than findings; used for geometry (cardiothoracic ratio, lung-field crops, anatomy-guided placement of synthetic objects).
    prior_vlm_hallucination_use: cxr-grounding used CheXmask contours to constrain RadEdit edits and to check that nothing changed outside the mask.
    fit_for_three_source_design: Conditioning for the S arm (place synthetic ETTs along the tracheal midline, NGTs crossing the left hemidiaphragm, pacemakers in the upper chest, foreign objects inside the lung field), lung-field crop-and-reask as a grounding-based mitigation, and matched geometry covariates across arms.

[5] ChestX-Det / ChestX-Det10 (Deepwise) verified=True
    url: https://github.com/Deepwise-AILab/ChestX-Det-Dataset (HF mirror: https://huggingface.co/datasets/natealberti/ChestX-Det)
    access: Direct zip downloads from resource.deepwise.com (train_data.zip, test_data.zip) linked in the README; no registration.
    license_or_terms: Apache-2.0 per the GitHub repository; images are NIH ChestX-ray14 (no restrictions, attribution). Derivatives redistributable.
    size: 3,578 NIH images (about 3,025 train / 553 test per the HF mirror); PNG; zip sizes not verified.
    object_level_annotations: Boxes and polygons for 13 classes (Atelectasis, Calcification, Cardiomegaly, Consolidation, Diffuse Nodule, Effusion, Emphysema, Fibrosis, Fracture, Mass, Nodule, Pleural Thickening, Pneumothorax) by three board-certified radiologists.
    non_expert_verifiable_classes: None safely; Fracture and Calcification are partly visible to a trained lay rater but still need expert sign-off. No devices.
    prior_vlm_hallucination_use: None found; not in the CheXinstruct source list as read here.
    fit_for_three_source_design: Expert-class extension only: LaMa removal inside a polygon yields a 'healthy counterfactual' that needs radiologist verification; useful to contrast finding-level with device-level hallucination, and fully releasable.

[6] PadChest-GR (grounded reports, 2024) verified=True
    url: https://bimcv.cipf.es/bimcv-projects/padchest-gr/ (paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.05085)
    access: Request via the BIMCV Google Form ('Descargar Dataset', 46 GB) after reading the PADCHEST Dataset Research Use Agreement; approval time not stated. Submit on day one and treat as a gated extension.
    license_or_terms: The paper states CC BY 4.0, but the BIMCV page binds users to (1) not share the data and (2) not attempt re-identification; clarify with BIMCV before redistributing any derived images.
    size: 4,555 CXR studies (3,099 abnormal, 1,456 normal) from 2014-2017, PNG 'with a reduced grayscale range compared to DICOM'; 7,037 positive and 3,422 negative finding sentences in Spanish and English; 46 GB.
    object_level_annotations: 88.3% of the 7,037 positive findings have boxes (up to two independent reader sets); 14 radiologists (nine senior, five junior); device/hardware categories include electrical device (e.g. pacemaker), nasogastric tube, central venous catheter, endotracheal tube and tracheostomy tube, with 'rectangular annotations cover[ing] the full horizontal and vertical extent of the long tubes and pacemaker wires'; negative sentences give explicit absences.
    non_expert_verifiable_classes: The device categories are verifiable; most of the 24 finding categories (nodule, atelectasis, interstitial pattern, etc.) are not.
    prior_vlm_hallucination_use: Built for and used by MAIRA-2 grounded report generation (Microsoft; reported in search listings, not re-read here). No hallucination benchmark on it found.
    fit_for_three_source_design: Best CXR device-box source with explicit negatives if approval arrives in time. E: LaMa inside the box (boxes are loose for diagonal tubes, so refine the mask by thresholding the bright line inside the box or a scribble); S: insertion into a 'normal' study; sentences double as a retrieval corpus for RAG mitigation.

Kaggle: immediate after accepting competition rules (rules not readable here); resized PNG mirrors exist on Kaggle.

[8] MIMIC-CXR-JPG 2.1.0 + Chest ImaGenome 1.0.0 (PhysioNet, credentialed) verified=True
    url: https://physionet.org/content/mimic-cxr-jpg/2.1.0/ ; https://physionet.org/content/chest-imagenome/1.0.0/
    access: Credentialed access + DUA for each (same CITI course and credential); per-file download so a few hundred images can be pulled without the full archive. Apply on day one; expect 1-2 weeks, possibly longer.
    license_or_terms: PhysioNet Credentialed Health Data License 1.5.0 (no sharing, no re-identification, release code). PhysioNet's 24 Sept 2025 policy: the DUA 'prohibits sharing access to the data with third parties, including sending it through APIs or using it on online platforms'; locally deployed models are 'strongly recommended'. So no GPT/Claude judge on these images.
    size: 377,110 JPG images, 227,827 studies (MIMIC-CXR-JPG; total size not shown while logged out, commonly cited near 570 GB). ImaGenome: 242,072 frontal images with automatic scene graphs; gold set 500 patients / 1,000 images.
    object_level_annotations: ImaGenome: 29 anatomical-location boxes per image from an atlas pipeline (automatic) with report-derived attributes including a 'tubesandlines' category (endotracheal tube, enteric tube, chest tube, picc, ij line, subclavian line, swan-ganz catheter, cardiac pacer and wires, prosthetic valve, sternotomy wires, aortic graft) and a 'devices' category; MIMIC-CXR-JPG has 14 CheXpert labels including Support Devices. No device-specific boxes or masks.
    non_expert_verifiable_classes: Tube/line attributes are visually verifiable; findings are not. Device masks for editing would have to be drawn by hand.
    prior_vlm_hallucination_use: HalluCXR (May 2026, 856 MIMIC-CXR images, 6 VLMs, 61.9-82.3% of outputs hallucinated, https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20469); MedHEval (MIMIC-CXR, 7 mitigation methods: VCD, OPERA, DoLa, AVISC, M3ID, DAMRO, PAI; https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02157); CARES (https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06007); MedVH (MIMIC-CXR report generation and MIMIC-Diff-VQA; https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.02730); FactCheXcker (ETT measurement hallucination); MC-CXR (PhysioNet-hosted CXR data, Aug 2026).
    fit_for_three_source_design: Gated extension: R items from ImaGenome tube/line attributes with hand-drawn masks (50-100 images), and the free-text reports as the retrieval corpus for RAG-style mitigation and as a report-conditioned text-only prior. Not for the core plan because of credentialing time, size and the online-API prohibition.
