-- recommended_pipeline --
  * Day 0, 1 h: create a Kaggle account, join RANZCR CLiP (screenshot the Rules tab) and start `kaggle competitions download -c ranzcr-clip-catheter-line-classification` (size unverified, community reports ~13 GB, 1-3 h); in parallel pull Object-CXR train + dev from the README Google Drive link or the Academic Torrents mirror (13.64 GB listed, 1-3 h). Record SHA-256 of every archive.
  * Day 0, 1 h: file the slow requests so they run in the background: PhysioNet account, CITI 'Data or Specimens Only Research' via the MIT affiliate (2-3 h of coursework; upload the training report, not the certificate), credentialing with Dr. Behzad as reference and the kfupm.edu.sa email (FAQ: 1-2 weeks; 2022 surge notice: up to 45 days); PadChest-GR Google Form (46 GB); optional VinDr-SpineXR registration + DUA. None of these may be on the critical path.
  * Day 1, 2-3 h CPU: realised-yield table before any editing. RANZCR: join train.csv to train_annotations.csv, keep images whose polylines cover exactly one line type (ETT, NGT or CVC) and build the 11-label presence vector for adversarial negatives (a labelled-absent type). Object-CXR: keep positives with one compact object (0.3-5% of area, inside the lung field, not border-touching) and sample 300 negatives from the 5,000 no-object images. Target 150 + 150 base images (minimum 100 + 100); hash the list.
  * Day 1-2, 2 h: standardise once: decode, resize the longest side to one fixed value (1024 px, or the hero model's native budget), save PNG, identical for all arms; log pixel size and per-model visual-token count so arms are token-matched exactly as in the COCO plan.
  * Day 2, 3-4 h on the GTX 1060 (fp32): masks = polylines dilated 8-15 px at full resolution, boxes/ellipses/polygons dilated 10 px; IOPaint LaMa removal (~2-5 s per 1024 px image, 300 images < 1 h); also SD-1.5-inpaint and mid-grey variants for the remover ablation (2-3 h). Keep a residual-trace flag per E image.
  * Day 3, CPU 1 h + 1060 overnight: S arm = synthetic object on a real negative. (a) Cut-paste insertion: a polyline crop or foreign-object crop from another image placed along CheXmask-derived anatomy (tracheal midline for ETT, path across the left hemidiaphragm for NGT, lung field for foreign objects), intensity-matched and blended; minutes on CPU. (b) RadEdit masked 'add' edit on a 20-image pilot (0.4B UNet, 512x512, 100 steps; fp32 time on the 1060 unverified, estimate 1-3 min per image) to decide whether a diffusion-inserted sub-arm is viable; cxr-grounding found RadEdit adds findings but 'could not remove findings convincingly', so use it only for insertion.
  * Day 3-4, 3-4 h human: verification with a one-page reference sheet per class (ETT, NGT, CVC, Swan-Ganz, pacemaker, foreign object). Student checks every R label, every E image (object gone? trace?) and every S image (object visible? plausible?) at ~10-15 s per item (900 images x up to 4 items = 2-3 h); second rater on a 200-item blinded sample; Cohen's kappa; faint CVCs flagged 'ambiguous' and excluded from tests; ask the advisor for a radiology resident to spot-check 50 items if available.
  * Day 4, 2 h on Kaggle T4: pilot the hero model on 35 R/E/S triplets x 6 yes/no questions (object present, co-occurring device absent, foreign object, etc.), estimate discordance, fix N with the McNemar power formula, freeze PROTOCOL.md and the OSF registration with the CXR hypotheses.
  * Week 2: full runs on the RunPod bf16 pod with the existing P0/P1/P2 prompts; mitigation family re-fitted to the umbrella: retrieval = kNN exemplar retrieval from the labelled RANZCR/Object-CXR pool (BiomedCLIP or SigLIP embeddings) for few-shot prompting plus an open text corpus (IU X-ray reports or device-appearance notes); grounding = CheXmask lung-field crop-and-reask and 'give a box, then answer' prompting; keep the Mirage/Rethinking-CD bias controls.
  * Week 3 (gated): if PhysioNet approval has arrived, add 50-100 MIMIC-CXR items from Chest ImaGenome 'tubesandlines' attributes with hand-drawn masks and use the reports as a retrieval corpus, running only local models (PhysioNet's 2025 policy bars online APIs); if PadChest-GR arrives, add its device boxes with explicit negatives.
  * Release: NIH/ChestX-Det/CheXmask/GRAZPEDWRI-DX derivatives may be published as images with attribution; for Kaggle (RANZCR) and Object-CXR items publish ids, masks, edit scripts, seeds and checksums only, with a rebuild script. Expected wall-clock: downloads 3-6 h, selection 1 day, editing 2 nights, verification 1-2 days, so a verified 200-300 base-image triplet set within 5-6 working days.

-- ethics_and_terms --
  * All images are previously de-identified public research datasets (RANZCR CLiP: de-identified hospital archives; Object-CXR: 'anonymousized'; NIH: de-identified by the NIH Clinical Center; GRAZPEDWRI-DX: de-identified with burned-in text masked); the project adds no PHI, uses no clinical data, and will not attempt to re-identify any patient, institution or hospital.
  * State that the study is secondary use of de-identified public data with no human-subject interaction; confirm with the advisor whether KFUPM requires an IRB exemption letter, and keep the PhysioNet DUA, Kaggle rule acceptance and BIMCV agreement screenshots as records.
  * Redistribution: Kaggle competition data (RANZCR, VinBigData, SIIM) and Object-CXR images are not redistributed; edited or synthetic versions of them are treated as derivatives and are also withheld; the release contains item ids, masks, prompts, seeds, scripts and checksums. NIH images, ChestX-Det (Apache-2.0), CheXmask (CC BY 4.0) and GRAZPEDWRI-DX (CC BY 4.0) derivatives may be released with attribution (NIH requires a link to the Box site, the CVPR 2017 citation and acknowledgement of the NIH Clinical Center).
  * PhysioNet data (if used): research use only, no sharing, no re-identification, code released with the publication, and no transmission of images or reports to online LLM services or APIs (PhysioNet policy of 24 Sept 2025); any LLM-based judging must run on local open models, so the plan's capped GPT-nano judge is disallowed on PhysioNet-derived items.
  * Synthetic and edited radiographs must be labelled as such in filenames and metadata, marked 'not for clinical use', and never presented as genuine patient images; RadEdit weights are under the MSRLA research licence ('Any deployed use case of the model, commercial or otherwise, is out of scope') and RoentGen-v2 is gated behind MIMIC-CXR credentials.
  * Cite every dataset with its descriptor paper and version (VinDr-CXR Sci Data 2022; CLiP Sci Data 2021; Object-CXR challenge; ChestX-ray14 CVPR 2017; ChestX-Det; CheXmask Sci Data 2024; PadChest-GR 2024; MIMIC-CXR-JPG; Chest ImaGenome; GRAZPEDWRI-DX Sci Data 2022) and the PhysioNet platform paper where applicable.
  * Non-expert verification must be disclosed as such: presence of tubes, lines, pacemakers, implants and foreign objects was verified by trained non-radiologist raters with inter-rater statistics; no diagnostic claim is made about findings, and any finding-level items are labelled 'radiologist-unverified' unless an expert signed them off.

-- gaps_and_opportunities --
  * No open CXR dataset gives device boxes under a redistribution-friendly licence: RANZCR and Object-CXR are usable but non-redistributable, PadChest-GR is request-gated with conflicting licence statements, CANDID-PTX (the only chest-tube segmentation set) closed to non-NZ users in Feb 2024, and ChestX-Det/VinDr-CXR/MS-CXR box only findings. A small, hand-labelled, CC-licensed device-presence set on NIH images (pacemakers, sternotomy wires, lines) would itself be a contribution.
  * No medical hallucination benchmark pairs real, object-removed and synthetic-object versions of the same radiograph: HalluCXR, MedHEval, CARES, MedVH and MC-CXR evaluate on unmodified images; the nearest neighbour, cxr-grounding (RadEdit + CheXmask on NIH, MedGemma), edits findings in only and reports that 'edited films look sicker than real ones', i.e. an edit-trace confound; FactCheXcker covers ETT measurement, not presence. Device-level counterfactual triplets with a trace-vs-prior attribution are open.
  * Thin-structure removal is easier in CXR than in COCO: tubes and lines are 1-3 px wide radiopaque curves on smooth background, so LaMa fills leave few residues, while ICU context (ECG leads, other lines) remains, giving a cleaner co-occurrence-prior probe ('intubated-looking patient without an ETT') than the COCO version.
  * The mitigation family asked for by the umbrella (grounding and retrieval) maps onto available data: exemplar retrieval from the labelled pool and CheXmask-driven crop-and-reask need no credentialed data; report-level RAG (MMed-RAG/RULE style) needs MIMIC-CXR or IU X-ray reports, so the open IU reports are the credential-free corpus.
  * PhysioNet's 2026 additions (MIMIC-Ext-CXR-QBA with device yes/no questions and automatic boxes; LATTE-CXR) show the field moving to localized device VQA on MIMIC-CXR, which strengthens the case for a credentialed extension but also means the core must stand without it.
  * Other radiograph sets with open implant/foreign-body boxes (GRAZPEDWRI-DX CC BY 4.0; VinDr-SpineXR restricted-access with 'Surgical implant' and 'Foreign body') allow a cross-anatomy generalisation arm that no hallucination paper has reported.
  * Two-reader box sets (PadChest-GR) and three-reader box sets (VinDr-CXR train) provide reader-agreement filters analogous to RePOPE label cleaning; using agreement as an item-inclusion rule is unexploited in medical hallucination evaluation.
  * Search coverage gap: the web-search budget was exhausted before a last sweep for 2024-2026 device/tube datasets beyond PadChest-GR, LATTE-CXR and MIMIC-Ext-CXR-QBA, for Kaggle mirrors of Object-CXR, and for RSNA Pneumonia Detection terms; a follow-up search could close these.

-- cautions --
  * Kaggle competition pages (RANZCR CLiP, VinBigData, SIIM-ACR) render only a title to fetch tools, so their Rules text, dataset sizes and the train_annotations.csv image count were not verified; the CLiP numbers come from the Scientific Data descriptor, and the ~13 GB figure is a community report.
  * CANDID-PTX is confirmed unavailable: the figshare record states it 'is not available for access by users outside Health New Zealand' since Feb 2024; do not plan on chest-tube segmentations.
  * PhysioNet timing is uncertain: the FAQ says approval 'typically takes 1-2 weeks', a 2022 notice warned of up to 45 days, and a reference must answer email; anything MIMIC/VinDr-based must be a gated extension, and PhysioNet's 24 Sept 2025 policy forbids sending these data to online LLM APIs.
  * MIMIC-CXR-JPG total size (commonly ~570 GB) and the PhysioNet file panels for credentialed datasets were not visible while logged out, so sizes for VinDr-CXR, Chest ImaGenome and VinDr-SpineXR are unverified.
  * PadChest-GR's licence is inconsistent across sources (paper: CC BY 4.0; BIMCV page: no sharing, no re-identification); approval time is unstated; ask BIMCV before any derivative release.
  * Object-CXR's hosting is fragile: the original site is gone, the Google Drive link in the mirror README was not tested, and the Academic Torrents listing (13.64 GB) comes from a search summary; its licence is an informal 'scientific research and non-commercial usage' note with no derivative clause.
  * Open-i/IU X-ray's CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 licence and the NLM TB-set terms and counts are from secondary summaries or literature, not from pages read this session (the FAQ fetch returned empty).
  * NIH ChestX-ray14 terms were read from a Hugging Face mirror card reproducing the README ('no restrictions' plus attribution), not from the NIH Box README itself; the 984-box/880-image figure is the commonly cited count, not recounted here.
  * RadEdit on a GTX 1060: weights (0.4B UNet, SDXL-VAE, BioViL-T) should fit in 6 GB at fp32 but per-image time is unverified; RadEdit is research-only (MSRLA), was trained on 512x512 crops, 'does not achieve photorealism', and cxr-grounding found it unable to remove findings convincingly; RoentGen-v2 (MIT, SD 2.1-based, 0.9B) is gated behind a MIMIC-CXR credential.
  * Non-expert verification is credible for tubes, lines, pacemakers, implants and foreign objects but not for position correctness or any finding; faint CVCs and Swan-Ganz catheters are a known weak spot and should be flagged or excluded.
  * MIMIC-Ext-CXR-QBA shows two publication dates (page: 22 July 2025; PhysioNet index: 25 Aug 2026); cite the page version string. Prior-use claims marked 'reported' (MAIRA-2 on PadChest-GR, RadVLM on VinDr-CXR, MedDiME/Latent Drifting on CheXpert) were not confirmed from the papers' own text.
  * The WebSearch budget ran out mid-task, so a final sweep for additional 2024-2026 device/tube datasets, Kaggle mirrors of Object-CXR and VinBigData/SIIM rule text was not possible; the user can continue the search in a follow-up message.

-- confidence_notes -- Verified from primary pages this session: PhysioNet pages for VinDr-CXR, VinDr-SpineXR, CheXmask, REFLACX, MS-CXR, Chest ImaGenome, MIMIC-CXR-JPG, MIMIC-Ext-CXR-QBA, the Credentialed Health Data License 1.5.0, the FAQ (1-2 weeks) and the Sept 2025 LLM policy; GitHub READMEs for ChestX-Det (Apache-2.0) and the Object-CXR mirror; the CLiP descriptor full text (Europe PMC); PadChest and PadChest-GR BIMCV pages and the PadChest-GR arXiv HTML; CheXpert Plus arXiv HTML; figshare API records for CANDID-PTX and GRAZPEDWRI-DX; the RadEdit and RoentGen-v2 model cards; cxr-grounding README; arXiv abstracts or HTML of HalluCXR, FactCheXcker, MedHEval, MedVH, CARES, MediConfusion, MC-CXR, CoEV, RadVLM, GEMeX, CheXagent, the shape-insertion paper, RoentMod and MedDiME. Not verified (marked false or flagged): Kaggle rule text and sizes for RANZCR/VinBigData/SIIM, Open-i licence, NLM TB counts and terms, MIMIC-CXR-JPG size, NIH Box README wording (read via a mirror), Object-CXR link status, Academic Torrents listings. The pipeline time estimates for LaMa and RadEdit on a GTX 1060 are engineering estimates, not measurements.


######## ANGLE: med_synthetic ########
angle: Synthetic and edited chest X-ray generation/editing tools (text-to-image, mask-conditioned, counterfactual, inpainting) and prior work that used generated or edited X-rays to probe vision models; plus the cheapest credential-free recipe for real / edited-removed / generated-twin CXR arms at matched pixel size.

[1] RadEdit: stress-testing biomedical vision models via diffusion image editing (Microsoft Health Futures) (2024 (ECCV 2024); ECCV 2024; model on Hugging Face) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.12865
    what: A 0.4B-parameter latent diffusion UNet trained from scratch on 487,680 frontal CXRs (MIMIC-CXR impressions, NIH and CheXpert labels as prompts) at 512x512 with BioViL-T text encoder, SDXL-VAE and DDIM. Editing = DDPM inversion with classifier-free guidance (weight 15) applied only inside an edit mask, plus 'keep' masks that protect correlated structures; demonstrated edits: remove COVID features, remove pneumothorax while keeping chest drains (CANDID-PTX masks), add edema/consolidation/pacemakers. Edits are auto-filtered by a BioViL-T directional 'editing score' (<0.2 discarded) and validated by agreement of downstream classifiers/segmenters between real and edited test sets; the authors report sharp boundary artifacts at mask edges, inability to change sizes (cardiomegaly), and the model card states it 'does not achieve photorealism' and that SDXL-VAE reconstruction artifacts make outputs distinguishable from real images. Only classifiers and segmentation models were stress-tested, no VLM.
    why: The one medically trained editor whose weights you can obtain in minutes, at the 512-px size the plan already uses, with an edit-mask + keep-mask API that maps one-to-one onto the 'remove the object, keep everything else' arm. Its 0.4B UNet should run on the GTX 1060 in fp32 (estimate, ~2.5 GB weights) and its pacemaker insertion and chest-drain keep-mask are the closest precedents for device-level objects. Its documented boundary artifacts are exactly the residual-trace confound the plan's three-remover ablation tests.
    code/data: https://huggingface.co/microsoft/radedit — gated by an HF login + clinical-disclaimer click-through (no credentialing); UNet weights under the Microsoft Research License Agreement (research-only, 'out-of-scope: any deployed use'), editing code pipeline.py under MIT; also needs microsoft/BiomedVLP-BioViL-T (text encoder) and stabilityai/sdxl-vae.

[2] RoentGen (Stanford MIMI) and RoentGen-v2 (2022/2024 (Nature BME) and 2025 (v2); Nature Biomedical Engineering 2024; arXiv 2508.16783 (v2)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.12737
    what: Stable Diffusion adapted to report-to-CXR generation, with expert assessment of quality/alignment and a 5% classifier gain in v1. v2 adds conditioning on sex, age and race plus findings, generated a 565k demographically balanced set, and reports +6.5% classification with synthetic pretraining and a 19.3% reduction in underdiagnosis gaps across 137k external images. Text-only control: no masks, no editing API (RoentMod supplies that).
    why: The canonical generator everyone benchmarks against and must be cited, but both weight sets sit behind MIMIC-CXR credentialing (CITI training + PhysioNet approval), a timeline risk before the Nov 8 checkpoint; keep it off the critical path. SD 2.1 (0.9B) is tight on 6 GB in fp32 (estimate) and comfortable on a Kaggle T4 in fp16.
    code/data: https://github.com/StanfordMIMI/RoentGen (v1 weights only to MIMIC-CXR-credentialed researchers via a Google Form); https://huggingface.co/stanfordmimi/RoentGen-v2 (MIT licence but gated: must be MIMIC-CXR credentialed and may not share with non-credentialed people; Stable Diffusion 2.1 base, 0.9B; diffusers DiffusionPipeline); 565k synthetic images at stanfordmimi/RoentGen-v2-synthetic-dataset; v2 paper https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16783

[3] RoentMod: a synthetic chest X-ray modification model to identify and correct image interpretation model shortcuts (2025 (arXiv) / 2026 (npj Digital Medicine); npj Digital Medicine 2026; arXiv 2509.08640) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08640
    what: Counterfactual editing with zero training: RoentGen's text encoder + UNet dropped into the stock Stable Diffusion image-to-image pipeline. Two radiologists (4-yr resident, 8-yr attending) read 800 synthetic scans: 93% judged realistic and 89-99% correctly incorporated the requested finding for cardiomegaly, edema, pleural effusion, pneumonia, hernia and lung mass; emphysema (76%) and nodules (70%) were unreliable and excluded. Medical devices were not modification targets. Runtime <2 s/image on an RTX 4090 and <30 s/image on a desktop CPU; used to expose and fix shortcut learning in classifiers (+3-19% AUC).
    why: The reader-study numbers your report should quote as the realism/label-validity reference, and its img2img recipe is the cheapest 'twin generated from the real image' method. Its failure list (nodules, emphysema) tells you which 'objects' to avoid; its exclusion of tubes/lines/pacemakers shows device editing is an open spot.
    code/data: Full text https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC13087218/ ; code/weights stated 'available upon publication' and synthetic cohorts 'by request' — a public repository was not located in this session (unverified).

[4] XReal: Realistic Anatomy and Pathology-Aware X-ray Generation via Controllable Diffusion Model (MBZUAI) (2024; arXiv 2403.09240) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.09240
    what: The authors' own LDM trained on >120k AP MIMIC-CXR images at 256x256, plus two training-free controllers: an Anatomy Controller (a VAE that turns a lungs/heart/aorta mask from TorchXRayVision's segmenter into a base X-ray, applied for the first 50 of 100 steps) and a Pathology Controller that inpaints a disease (e.g. pneumonia, cardiomegaly) inside a user mask. Two radiologists rated anatomy 4.17 and pathology 4.13 (1-5) versus 3.63/3.56 for real images; Dice 0.838 between input mask and generated organs; MS-SSIM 0.701; FID 55 (authors note FID is weak for CXR). Stated limitation: generated images can drop acquisition artifacts and devices.
    why: The only credential-free, MIT, mask-conditioned CXR generator, i.e. the medical analogue of the plan's SD 1.5 + ControlNet layout twin. Two consequences: its 256-px native size forces an explicit matched-pixel-size decision (thin tubes almost vanish at 256), and because it tends not to render devices, device questions on twins are 'absent by construction', which is a clean negative set but not an insertion tool.
    code/data: https://github.com/BioMedIA-MBZUAI/XReal — MIT; pretrained weights via an open SharePoint link (no gating); needs taming-transformers and CLIP repos, Python 3.8; inference notebook provided; training data preprocessed from MIMIC-CXR-JPG.

[5] Cheff: Cascaded Latent Diffusion Models for High-Resolution Chest X-ray Synthesis (with inpainting/device removal demo) (2023 (PAKDD 2023); PAKDD 2023; arXiv 2303.11224) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.11224
    what: 256-px latent diffusion followed by a diffusion super-resolution stage to 1024x1024, trained on MaCheX (651,471 CXRs pooled from ChestX-ray14, CheXpert, MIMIC-CXR, PadChest, BRAX, VinDr-CXR); FID 11.58 vs 46.54 for a cascaded baseline; DDIM 150 steps; the paper demonstrates diffusion inpainting that removes medical devices and fills with 'a clear lung'. No radiologist study; VRAM and per-image time not stated (the 1024-px SR stage probably exceeds 6 GB, unverified).
    why: A second, independently trained, credential-free medical inpainter for the three-remover ablation (LaMa vs RadEdit vs Cheff) with an explicit device-removal precedent. Its MaCheX pooling (VinDr, PadChest inside) matters for the contamination flag if those sets supply your real arm.
    code/data: https://github.com/saiboxx/chexray-diffusion — MIT; five checkpoints on open LRZ Sync+Share links (autoencoder, unconditional LDM, report-conditioned LDM, super-resolution base and fine-tuned).

[6] LLM-CXR: Instruction-Finetuned LLM for CXR Image Understanding and Generation (2024 (ICLR 2024); ICLR 2024) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.11490
    what: A Dolly-based LLM instruction-tuned to both read and generate CXRs as VQ-GAN tokens, bidirectional VQA and generation at 256 px; one of the two CXR-native text-to-image baselines in CheXGenBench.
    why: Will not run on the 1060 (11 GB) but fits a Kaggle T4; mostly a citation and an optional token-based generator for a robustness check of the twin arm. Low priority.
    code/data: https://github.com/hyn2028/llm-cxr — Apache-2.0; LLM and VQ-GAN checkpoints on Google Drive (256-px, 'mimic-cxr-256'); README asks for a GPU with at least 11 GB for interactive inference; MIMIC-CXR-JPG credentials needed only to retrain.

[7] BiomedJourney (Microsoft) and its instruction-editing successors InstructX2X and ProgEmu (2023; 2026; 2025; arXiv 2310.10765; arXiv 2603.00423; MICCAI 2025 (arXiv 2503.23149)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.10765
    what: Instruction-learned counterfactual editing from longitudinal patient pairs (prior image + GPT-4 progression text -> new image) with a two-stage curriculum; claims to beat InstructPix2Pix and RoentGen. InstructX2X adds region-restricted edits with a guidance map, compares against RoentGen, LLM-CXR, BiomedJourney and RadEdit; ProgEmu unifies counterfactual image and textual explanation generation.
    why: Cite as the instruction-editing lineage your edited arm belongs to, but plan nothing on them: nothing is downloadable.
    code/data: BiomedJourney: none — https://microsoft.github.io/BiomedJourney/ still says 'we plan to release' code and models (checked today). InstructX2X (https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00423): InstructPix2Pix base at 512 px, MIMIC-EDIT-INSTRUCTION (21,957 add/remove/severity edits over 8 diseases), CC BY 4.0 paper, no code URL found. ProgEmu: project page progemu.github.io only.

[8] ChexGen: A Generative Foundation Model for Chest Radiography (text-, mask- and box-guided DiT) (2025; NEJM AI 2025; arXiv 2509.03903) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03903
    what: Latent diffusion transformer pretrained on 960k radiograph-report pairs with text-, segmentation-mask- and bounding-box-guided synthesis (T5 text embeddings), validated by experts and quantitative metrics; used for augmentation, supervised pretraining, detection/segmentation and fairness audits. VRAM not stated ('batch size 1 for best fidelity').
    why: The strongest openly described mask/box-controllable CXR generator, but credential-gated; a fallback for the generated-twin arm only if MIMIC access lands early. Mask control is limited to SIIM pneumothorax masks, not devices.
    code/data: https://github.com/era-ai-biomed/ChexGen — Apache-2.0 code; four checkpoints (pretrained_256, impression_512, demographic_impression_512, control_siim_512 for pneumothorax masks) released only after PhysioNet credentialing plus a model-access request form.

[9] Causal/counterfactual CXR generators: causal-gen (Imperial, ICML 2023), RadCF (GSK, 2026), RadiT (Imperial, 2026) (2023 / 2026 / 2026; ICML 2023; arXiv 2609.24879; arXiv 2606.19460) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24879
    what: causal-gen: deep structural causal models for high-fidelity counterfactuals (sex, age, finding) including MIMIC-CXR. RadCF: specialises a pretrained non-causal generator into a causal mechanism; interventions on view, sex, age, effusion, and on BRAX 'medical device presence'; evaluated with attribute judges across effectiveness, composition, realism, minimality and reversibility (no radiologist); shows counterfactuals detect and mitigate shortcut learning in a classifier. RadiT: 512-px generation/editing over a 19-variable flow-SCM covering demographics, view, CheXpert-14 findings and Support Devices, trained on 1.2M CXRs; three pulmonologists were at near-chance distinguishing 50 real from 50 synthetic images.
    why: Precedents for treating 'device present' as an intervenable variable and for axiomatic counterfactual metrics you can borrow for the validity appendix; RadiT's expert-indistinguishability is the counterpoint to RadEdit's 'not photorealistic' admission when you discuss radiologist-detectable synthetic cues. Only RadCF is runnable, and retraining is out of budget.
    code/data: causal-gen https://github.com/biomedia-mira/causal-gen (MIT; no MIMIC checkpoints shared). RadCF https://github.com/GSK-AI/RadCF (Apache-2.0; latent flow matching on REPA-E, LoRA 24M trainable; weight hosting not confirmed). RadiT https://radit-project.github.io/ (1.3B rectified-flow transformer; no code/weight release statement found in paper or project page).

[10] 2026 localized CXR editors with reader-validated rubrics (no weights): Spatially Gated Diffusion, Mask-Guided Attention Regulation, Med-Banana-80K (2026 (Oct), 2026 (Mar), 2025 (Nov); arXiv 2610.00805; arXiv 2603.04130; arXiv 2511.00801) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.00805
    what: SGD: SDXL-base LoRA (rank 32) at 1024 px with a dual-trajectory gate producing an image-space mask; 2,400 insert/remove requests over pleural effusion, cardiomegaly, edema, consolidation, atelectasis and pneumothorax; two radiologists blind-rated 240 requests on target completion, unintended change, quality and joint validity (93.3% joint validity, kappa 0.75-0.82) and protected-region MAE fell from 0.0190 to 0.0075. MGAR gates attention with organ masks for anatomically consistent edits. Med-Banana verifies CXR edits with MLLM judges on instruction compliance, pathological plausibility, anatomical consistency and imaging fidelity.
    why: They set the 2026 bar for validating edited CXRs: a two-reader blinded joint-validity rubric plus protected-region error. Adopt that rubric for the human audit and cite them as positioning; none can serve as a tool, and SDXL-based editors do not fit the 1060 anyway.
    code/data: Spatially Gated Diffusion: no code or weights released; MIMIC-CXR-JPG + MS-CXR (credentialed). Mask-Guided Attention Regulation: no code mentioned. Med-Banana-80K: CC BY 4.0, code/data at an anonymous review link (https://anonymous.4open.science/r/Med-Banana-anonymous-E1E7/), editors are LoRA-adapted Qwen-Image-Edit-2511 and FireRed-Image-Edit-1.1.

[11] Cite-only generators without usable weights: DiffChest, CXR-IRGen, MINIM (2023 / 2024 (WACV) / 2024 (Nature Medicine); arXiv 2309.17123; WACV 2024; Nature Medicine 2024) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.17123
    what: DiffChest: self-conditioned diffusion on 515,704 CXRs that generates patient-specific counterfactual explanations for 193 conditions and surfaces confounders (Fleiss kappa >= 0.8 across most findings). CXR-IRGen: SD/U-ViT vision module + BART report module producing paired image-report data. MINIM: unified image-text generator across OCT, fundus, CXR and CT with reported downstream gains (+15% for chest tasks).
    why: Required citations for the related-work map (counterfactual explanation, paired image-report synthesis, multi-modality foundation generation) but none is executable in this project; do not plan on them.
    code/data: DiffChest: no code found. CXR-IRGen https://github.com/junjie-shentu/CXR-IRGen (code, no checkpoints, no licence stated). MINIM https://github.com/WithStomach/MINIM (training code; only a Baidu-hosted Windows .exe bundle of a multi-modality model; no licence stated; 512-px custom U-Net, not Stable Diffusion).

[12] Which text-conditioned CXR diffusion checkpoints exist and how synthetic-CXR validity has been measured: CheXGenBench, IrohXu SD1.4-MIMIC, Prakash et al. (2025; 2023; 2024; arXiv 2505.10496 (CheXGenBench); Hugging Face; arXiv 2411.18602) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10496
    what: CheXGenBench fine-tunes 11 T2I models (RadEdit, LLM-CXR, SD 1.4/1.5/2/2.1/3.5-M, PixArt-Sigma, Sana, Lumina-2, FLUX.1-dev) on 237,388 MIMIC-CXR samples at 512 px and finds Sana 0.6B best on fidelity, that every model emits re-identifiable samples (10-25% above high-risk thresholds), and that adding synthetic data degrades report-generation fine-tuning. Prakash et al. (Stanford) use a proxy model plus radiologist feedback and show conditioning on single-disease labels or geometrically transformed segmentation masks gives +0.150 F1 and +0.146 Dice.
    why: Evidence base for picking a generator and for the claim that synthetic CXRs already hurt multimodal models, the direct motivation for a real/edited/generated comparison; the proxy-model validity check is a free label-validity gate (TorchXRayVision classifiers) you can run on every arm.
    code/data: CheXGenBench fine-tuned checkpoints stated released via the authors' GitHub/Hugging Face (links and licences not opened this session). https://huggingface.co/IrohXu/stable-diffusion-mimic-cxr-v0.1 (SD 1.4 fine-tuned on MIMIC-CXR; gated by a contact-info form; licence not stated; author warns it is only good for editing, not generation). Prakash et al. https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.18602 (no code statement).

[13] Inpainting precedents for removing devices from CXRs: Sogancioglu et al., Le et al., Cheff inpainting, MaskMedPaint; and the LaMa gap (2018; 2020; 2023; 2024; arXiv 1809.01471; arXiv 2008.06828; PAKDD 2023; ML4H 2024 (arXiv 2411.10686)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/1809.01471
    what: Sogancioglu et al. trained context encoders, semantic inpainting and contextual attention on 1.2M patches from 60k healthy CXRs (64x64 holes in 128x128 patches) and ran a 2AFC study in which an experienced radiologist had difficulty detecting inpainted regions. Le et al. detect, segment and inpaint foreign objects (pacemakers, tubes) then show downstream gains. MaskMedPaint inpaints outside the classification region of CXRs with text-to-image diffusion to break spurious correlations. No 2023-2026 peer-reviewed study of LaMa specifically for tube/line removal on CXRs was found.
    why: Supports the three-remover ablation (LaMa vs RadEdit vs Cheff) and supplies the 2AFC detectability protocol your human audit should copy at small scale; the absence of a LaMa-on-CXR device-removal paper is a small, honest novelty claim.
    code/data: Sogancioglu: no code found. Le et al.: no code found (uses CheXphoto). MaskMedPaint: no code link on arXiv page. LaMa https://github.com/advimman/lama (Apache-2.0, already in the plan; IOPaint wrapper runs it on CPU).

[14] Credential-free mask and label sources for tubes, lines, drains and anatomy: RANZCR CLiP, CANDID-PTX, CheXmask, TorchXRayVision (2021; 2021; 2025; 2020-2025; Scientific Data 2021 (CLiP); Radiology: AI 2021 (CANDID-PTX); PhysioNet (CheXmask); GitHub/MELBA (TorchXRayVision)) verified=True
    url: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41597-021-01066-8
    what: Together these give pixel masks for the 'objects' (ETT/NGT/CVC polylines, chest-tube and pneumothorax segmentations), lung/heart keep-masks, and a free Support-Devices/effusion classifier to act as a proxy judge, without MIMIC credentialing.
    why: Prerequisite for the removal arm (edit masks), for RadEdit keep-masks, and for XReal layout twins; the CLiP annotations are polylines, so the dilation width you rasterise them with becomes a documented, auditable parameter.
    code/data: RANZCR CLiP on Kaggle: 30,083 CXRs from 3,791 patients, 50,612 image-level labels and 17,999 manually segmented annotations stored as coordinate lists (polylines) for ETT, nasoenteric tubes and CVCs with normal/borderline/abnormal classes; images CC BY 4.0, metadata CC0, Kaggle registration required (competition rules page not opened). CANDID-PTX: 19,237 1024-px CXRs with pneumothorax, chest-tube and rib-fracture segmentations, DOI 10.17608/k6.auckland.14173982 (figshare returned 403; access terms unverified). CheXmask https://physionet.org/content/chexmask-cxr-segmentation-data/ (open, CC BY 4.0; 657,566 lung+heart RLE masks at 1024 for ChestX-ray8, CheXpert, MIMIC-CXR-JPG, PadChest, VinDr-CXR). TorchXRayVision https://github.com/mlmed/torchxrayvision (Apache-2.0; PSPNet segmenter of 14 structures at 512 px; CheXpert DenseNet classifier includes Support Devices).

[15] Image-sensitivity and hallucination audits of medical VLMs on CXRs (nearest precedents to a real-vs-edited comparison): ModaLens, MedVH, MC-CXR, HalluCXR (2026; 2024; 2026; 2026; arXiv 2609.15635; arXiv 2407.02730 + PhysioNet; Findings of EMNLP 2026 (arXiv 2608.24118); arXiv 2605.20469) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15635
    what: ModaLens swaps the radiograph for another study with question and report fixed on 3,199 MIMIC-CXR pairs: MedGemma-27B changes its answer 20.9% of the time without a report but only 4.3% with one. MedVH's 'Wrongful Image' task feeds mismatched or other-organ images and finds medical LVLMs hallucinate more than general ones. MC-CXR adds misleading text and visual overlays on prior CXRs to 10 VLMs (switch-to-wrong 35.7-61.7% for visual context). HalluCXR evaluates six VLMs on 856 real MIMIC-CXR images with an 8-category taxonomy (61.9-82.3% of outputs contain hallucinations; ensembles cut fabrication up to 84.8%).
    why: These are the numbers to position against, and none uses edited or generated images of the same study: no medical VLM paper was found that compares behaviour on real vs edited vs generated CXRs, so the matched-triplet design transfers intact. MedVH/MC-CXR are credential-gated; ModaLens's swap audit is a cheap extra control arm.
    code/data: ModaLens: GitHub with prompts and run records, CC BY 4.0. MedVH: https://physionet.org/content/medvh/1.0.1/ (PhysioNet Credentialed Health Data License 1.5.0). MC-CXR: dataset on PhysioNet. HalluCXR: release not stated in abstract.

[16] Grounding- and retrieval-based mitigation precedents for CXR VLMs: V-RAG, ARCD, FactCheXcker, MedHEval (2025; 2025; 2025 (CVPR); 2025; AAAI'25 GenAI4Health workshop (arXiv 2502.15040); arXiv 2512.17189; CVPR 2025 (arXiv 2411.18672); arXiv 2503.02157) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.17189
    what: V-RAG retrieves similar images plus text and uses entity probing to correct hallucinations in MIMIC-CXR report generation (higher RadGraph-F1). ARCD is a training-free, plug-and-play contrastive decoding scheme that uses anatomical masks to reweight tokens, attention and logits toward a region, reducing hallucinations on CXR, CT, MRI and ultrasound. FactCheXcker removes measurement hallucinations about endotracheal tube position via query-code-update, improving 10 of 11 report models. MedHEval benchmarks 7 mitigation methods on 11 Med-LVLMs and finds limited effectiveness, especially for knowledge and context errors.
    why: Defines the 'grounding and retrieval-based' family the umbrella asks for: ARCD is the medical cousin of the plan's VCD/prior-contrast arm and needs exactly the anatomical masks listed above; a kNN image+report retrieval over the real arm is the retrieval arm; FactCheXcker shows tubes/lines are already a recognised hallucination locus.
    code/data: V-RAG: no code link on arXiv page. ARCD: no code link found (CC BY 4.0 paper). FactCheXcker: code on GitHub per abstract. MedHEval: no code link on arXiv page.

-- gaps_and_opportunities --
  * No medical VLM paper found compares the same study as real / edited-removed / generated-twin CXR for object-level yes/no questions; RadEdit, RoentMod and RadCF stress-tested only classifiers and segmenters, and the VLM audits (ModaLens, MedVH, MC-CXR, HalluCXR) use real images only. The TriPOPE triplet design is unoccupied in the CXR domain.
  * Nobody reports whether a VLM 'sees' a removed tube/line at different rates under LaMa vs RadEdit vs Cheff removal; RadEdit's own paper documents sharp mask-edge artifacts, so the residual-trace ablation is both motivated and new in this domain.
  * Device objects (ETT, NGT, CVC, chest tube, pacemaker) are under-served by generators: RoentMod excluded them, XReal drops them, RadiT models them but is unreleased; only RadEdit's masked insertion/removal and classical inpainting touch them, which makes device-absent twins a clean negative set and device insertion the scarce resource.
  * A free automatic label-validity gate exists and has not been combined with VLM evaluation: RadEdit's BioViL-T editing score (threshold 0.2) plus TorchXRayVision Support-Devices/effusion probabilities before vs after editing, mirroring Prakash et al.'s proxy-model check.
  * CheXGenBench shows synthetic data degrades multimodal report models and that every CXR generator emits re-identifiable samples; a student-scale study of how open VLMs answer on those same synthetic images (hallucination rate, yes-bias, confidence) is the natural next question and only needs inference.
  * Radiologist-detectable synthetic cues are measurable with a 2AFC (Sogancioglu) or real-vs-synthetic reader test (RadiT, RoentMod); a 50+50 image version with the advisor or a radiology resident would be a publishable appendix.
  * The 2026 editing papers (Spatially Gated Diffusion, InstructX2X) validate edits with two-reader joint-validity rubrics but release no weights; reproducing their rubric on RadEdit outputs is a cheap, citable bridge.

-- recommended_for_this_project --
  * Decide for chest X-rays: the three-arm design is executable without any credentialing using RadEdit (HF click-through), XReal (MIT, SharePoint weights), Cheff (MIT) and LaMa for images, with RANZCR CLiP (CC BY 4.0) and CANDID-PTX masks for objects; no comparable editor/generator stack was surveyed for diagrams, so the education alternative would have to be built from general-domain tools.
  * Day one: request access to microsoft/radedit on Hugging Face; download XReal and Cheff checkpoints; pull RANZCR CLiP from Kaggle; apply for PhysioNet/MIMIC credentialing in parallel but put nothing credential-gated (RoentGen-v2, ChexGen, MedVH, MC-CXR, MS-CXR) on the critical path before Nov 8.
  * Choose objects in two classes: (a) devices that can only be removed, using masks (ETT/NGT/CVC polylines from CLiP, chest tubes from CANDID-PTX); (b) findings RadEdit and XReal can add or remove with high reader-validated rates (pleural effusion, consolidation/pneumonia, edema, pneumothorax). Avoid nodules and emphysema (RoentMod 70-76% validity) and cardiomegaly (RadEdit cannot change size).
  * Fix pixel size at 512x512 (RadEdit native; RoentMod and CheXGenBench also use 512): center-crop/resize the real images exactly as RadEdit did, run LaMa/RadEdit/Cheff removals at 512, and if XReal twins are used, upsample 256 to 512 with one documented resampler for every arm and add a 256-px sensitivity check; keep logging VLM image tokens per arm as the plan already does.
  * Recipe for the three arms: real = CLiP/CANDID image at 512; edited-removed = (i) LaMa with the dilated polyline mask, (ii) RadEdit with edit mask = dilated line, keep mask = lungs from CheXmask/TorchXRayVision, prompt 'no endotracheal tube', (iii) Cheff inpainting; generated twin = XReal from the real image's TorchXRayVision anatomy mask (true layout-matched generation, device absent by construction) with RadEdit img2img from the real image (RoentMod's recipe) as the 512-px alternative; only count a device as 'present' in a twin if a RadEdit insertion was applied and passed the validity gate.
  * Validity gates per item: RadEdit editing score >= 0.2; TorchXRayVision Support Devices / effusion probability shift before vs after; protected-region MAE outside the mask (Spatially Gated Diffusion); a 2AFC or 50+50 real-vs-synthetic reader test; and a two-reader joint-validity rubric (target completed, no unintended change, quality) borrowed from the 2026 editors.
  * Re-fit the mitigation family to the umbrella: grounding = ARCD-style anatomical-region contrastive decoding reimplemented from the paper with TorchXRayVision masks (keep VCD as the general-domain baseline); retrieval = kNN image+report retrieval over the real arm (V-RAG-lite, embeddings from BioViL-T which you already load for RadEdit); keep the Mirage/Rethinking-CD yes-bias controls and abstention scoring unchanged.
  * Compute plan: run RadEdit, XReal and Cheff on the GTX 1060 in fp32 overnight (per-image time unverified; budget 1-3 min per RadEdit edit as a planning estimate and measure 20 images first); on Kaggle T4 run RadEdit in fp16 but keep the SDXL-VAE in fp32 or swap in madebyollin/sdxl-vae-fp16-fix (MIT) to avoid NaN/black images; LLM-CXR only on the T4 (11 GB); SDXL/FLUX-based editors are out.
  * Cite: RadEdit, RoentGen/RoentGen-v2, RoentMod, XReal, Cheff, CheXGenBench, Prakash et al. 2024, Sogancioglu 2018, Spatially Gated Diffusion, RadCF/RadiT, ModaLens, MedVH, MC-CXR, HalluCXR, ARCD, V-RAG, FactCheXcker, and the general-domain AIGCs-Confuse-AI-Too result already in the digest. Avoid planning on BiomedJourney, DiffChest, MINIM, CXR-IRGen (no usable weights) and on anything requiring MIMIC credentials before it arrives.
  * Contamination flag: every CXR generator was trained on MIMIC/CheXpert/NIH and most medical VLMs on MIMIC-CXR; using RANZCR CLiP and CANDID-PTX as the real arm reduces (does not remove) overlap, and RadEdit's training set includes NIH and CheXpert, so report which public sets each model and generator saw.

-- cautions --
  * The per-turn web-search cap was reached before GTX 1060 and Kaggle T4 throughput for RadEdit/XReal/Cheff could be verified; all images-per-minute and VRAM-fit statements for those tools are estimates from parameter counts and must be measured on 20 images before scheduling.
  * CANDID-PTX access terms and licence are unverified (figshare and RSNA pages returned 403); RadEdit's paper used its chest-drain masks, so the dataset exists, but registration/DUA requirements must be checked by the student.
  * RANZCR CLiP is CC BY 4.0 per the Scientific Data paper, but the Kaggle competition rules page could not be opened; read the rules after login before redistributing derived masks.
  * RoentMod's code/weights are stated as 'available upon publication'; a public repository was not located this session, so treat it as citation-only until found. CheXGenBench's fine-tuned checkpoints are stated released on GitHub/Hugging Face but their links and licences were not opened; RadCF's weight hosting was not confirmed; XReal and Cheff VRAM are not documented.
  * RadEdit's UNet is under the Microsoft Research License Agreement (research-only; out-of-scope for any deployed use) and the model card warns outputs are not photorealistic and can carry SDXL-VAE reconstruction artifacts; label every edited/generated image as synthetic in any release and check MSRLA terms before redistributing edited images.
  * MIMIC-CXR credentialing (CITI training + PhysioNet approval) commonly takes weeks (duration unverified); RoentGen-v2, ChexGen, MedVH, MC-CXR and MS-CXR all depend on it.
  * Running the SDXL-VAE in fp16 on a T4 produces NaNs/black images (documented on the sdxl-vae-fp16-fix card); keep the VAE in fp32 or use the fix. fp16 on the Pascal GTX 1060 is a throughput trap, so use fp32 there.
  * Thin tubes and lines are a few pixels wide: at 256 px (XReal native) they may vanish, and any resampling applied unequally across arms can itself become a detectable 'trace'; apply identical preprocessing to all three arms.
  * No paper confirms that medical VLM behaviour on RadEdit-style edits mirrors behaviour on real distribution shifts; treat the edited arm as a controlled probe, not as a proxy for clinical performance, and keep the human audit.
  * MINIM and IrohXu checkpoints have no stated licence; CXR-IRGen has no checkpoints; BiomedJourney has no code; do not cite any of these as available tools.

-- confidence_notes -- High confidence on tool availability, licences, gating, resolutions and validation methods for RadEdit, RoentGen/v2, RoentMod, XReal, Cheff, LLM-CXR, ChexGen, RadCF, RadiT, the 2026 editors, CheXGenBench, CLiP, CheXmask and TorchXRayVision: each was read from its arXiv abstract/HTML, GitHub README, Hugging Face card, PhysioNet page or PMC full text today. Medium confidence on the 'no three-way real/edited/generated VLM comparison exists' claim: it rests on about 30 targeted searches plus the surveyed benchmarks, not an exhaustive citation trace. Low confidence (explicitly unverified) on GTX 1060/T4 runtimes and VRAM fit, CANDID-PTX access terms, RoentMod code location, CheXGenBench checkpoint links, and RadCF weight hosting; these are flagged in cautions and should be settled by the student in the first two days.


######## ANGLE: med_models ########
angle: Open-weight medical VLMs (CXR-specialist and general-medical) that fit a 6 GB GTX 1060 in NF4 or a 16 GB T4, plus 7B-class options for a rented 24 GB bf16 pod and small generalists as baselines, with licenses, gating, grounding support, reported CXR/medical-VQA numbers, harness support, and a five-run panel for a chest X-ray object-existence (findings/tubes/lines/devices) hallucination study with abstention scoring.

[1] MedGemma 1.5 4B (instruction-tuned) | google/medgemma-1.5-4b-it | 4.30B (safetensors metadata); Gemma 3 architecture with MedSigLIP encoder | HF repo created 2026-01-07; model version 1.5.0 dated 2026-01-13 (card); Google Research blog Jan 2026 verified=True
    license_and_access: Health AI Developer Foundations (HAI-DEF) terms, HF license 'other'; gated='auto' (click-through acceptance). Terms: Google claims no ownership of outputs, outputs usable in research; no clinical use / nothing that makes Google a device 'manufacturer'; redistributed derivatives must carry the terms and a notice file.
    domain_training: General-medical with explicit CXR data: MIMIC-CXR, Chest ImaGenome (anatomy boxes), plus dermatology (PAD-UFES-20, SCIN), ophthalmology (EyePACS), pathology (CAMELYON) and proprietary sets. New in 1.5: 3D CT/MRI, longitudinal CXR comparison, CXR anatomical localization.
    input_output: Images resized to 896x896 -> 256 tokens; Gemma chat template; 128K context. Boxes: official notebook uses a system prompt 'The format of bounding boxes coordinates is [y0, x0, y1, x1]... normalized to [0,1000]' and the model returns a JSON list [{"box_2d": [y0,x0,y1,x1], "label": ...}]; notebook pads CXRs to square first.
    reported_scores: HAI-DEF/HF model card (Google eval): MIMIC-CXR RadGraph F1 27.2; MIMIC-CXR macro-F1 top-5 89.5; CheXpert macro-F1 top-5 48.2; CXR14 macro-F1 (3 cond.) 48.4; SLAKE closed acc 82.8; VQA-RAD closed acc 70.2 (tokenized F1 48.1); Chest ImaGenome bbox IoU 38.0. Lotfinia et al. 2026 (arXiv 2606.17710): pooled CXR yes/no acc ~58.2; keeps 42.4% of correct answers with the image removed (Yes-rate 8.1); opposite-label flip rate 53.5. ReXrank 'MedGemma' MIMIC-CXR RadGraph 0.549 (version unspecified).
    fits_6gb_or_t4: NF4 weights ~2.8 GB (estimate) -> fits 6 GB; bf16 ~8.6 GB fits a T4 only in 8-bit/NF4 or (per Google's localization notebook) 'T4 with torch.bfloat16' (bf16 is emulated on T4, slow, unverified). fp16 is unsafe: Gemma 3 activations overflow fp16 (HF PR #37226, issue #39972). MedGemma issue #5 reports 8-bit bitsandbytes gibberish (closed Sep 2026 without fix) -> validate NF4 vs bf16 on ~20 items.
    harness_support: transformers >=4.50 (Gemma3ForConditionalGeneration). lmms-eval has gemma3.py (simple) and a generic huggingface chat wrapper, no medgemma-specific class; VLMEvalKit has Gemma3-4B/12B/27B classes but no MedGemma entry (loading MedGemma through the Gemma3 class is plausible, unverified).
    url: https://huggingface.co/google/medgemma-1.5-4b-it

-- generalist_baselines --
  * Qwen/Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct (Apache-2.0, Oct 2025; vendor SLAKE 61.1; relative 0-1000 'bbox_2d' grounding; lmms-eval + VLMEvalKit support) - keep as generalist A from the existing plan.
  * google/gemma-4-E2B-it (Apache-2.0, ungated, Apr 2026; MedXpertQA-MM 23.5; VLMEvalKit Gemma4 class) - keep as generalist B; its 26B sibling abstains on all presence questions without an image in Lotfinia et al. 2026, which makes the E2B abstention behaviour worth measuring.
  * Qwen/Qwen3.5-2B (Apache-2.0, Feb 2026; vendor SLAKE 74.4 thinking / 67.5 non-thinking; lmms-eval qwen3_5 backend) - swap-in if Qwen3-VL-2B is replaced; disable thinking for cost.
  * OpenGVLab/InternVL3_5-2B (Apache-2.0, Aug 2025) - third generalist / replacement if a Gemma-family fp16 problem blocks the T4 path.
  * LiquidAI/LFM2.5-VL-1.6B (LFM Open License v1.0, Jan 2026) - smallest edge baseline; no medical numbers published.
  * Matched-base controls (not generalist baselines per se): Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct for CheXOne/MedVLThinker-3B (Qwen Research License), google/gemma-3-4b-it for MedGemma (Gemma terms, gated manual, encoder confound), Qwen/Qwen2-VL-2B-Instruct for MedVLM-R1/Med-R1, Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-7B-Instruct for Lingshu-7B/HuatuoGPT-Vision-7B-Qwen2.5VL.

-- recommended_panel --
  * Run 1 - CXR specialist: StanfordAIMI/CheXOne in instruct mode (Qwen2.5-VL-3B architecture, 4.07B, cc-by-nc(-nd), ungated; VQA + box grounding; max_pixels=512*512). NF4 on the 1060 for triage, bf16 on the pod for scored runs. Reasoning mode is a cheap secondary condition (\boxed{} final answer).
  * Run 2 - general-medical with CXR supervision: google/medgemma-1.5-4b-it (4.3B, HAI-DEF gated auto; trained on MIMIC-CXR + Chest ImaGenome; returns [y0,x0,y1,x1] boxes on a 0-1000 scale) - also the engine for the grounding-based mitigation arm (ground-then-answer; abstain when no box or box-text inconsistency).
  * Run 3 - generalist A: Qwen/Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct (already in STRATEGY.md; relative 0-1000 boxes let the same ground-then-answer mitigation be applied to a generalist).
  * Run 4 - generalist B: google/gemma-4-E2B-it (Apache-2.0; NF4 on the 1060, bf16 on the pod; avoid fp16).
  * Run 5 - matched base that isolates CXR post-training: Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct, the exact base of CheXOne with identical vision encoder and chat format; the CheXOne-vs-base contrast is the cleanest 'does medical fine-tuning change device-existence hallucination and abstention' test available at <=4B. (The Gemma-3-4b-it vs MedGemma pair is confounded by the MedSigLIP encoder swap; use it only as an appendix pair.)
  * Optional pod-only tier (about 2-3 extra GPU-hours at $0.22-0.34/h): Lingshu-7B vs Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-7B-Instruct as a second medical-fine-tuning pair at 7B (both bf16 16.6 GB on a 24 GB card). Cheapest alternative pair if budget is tight: Qwen2-VL-2B-Instruct vs JZPeterPan/MedVLM-R1 (same base, Apache-2.0, 2B) with yes/no cast as two-option MCQ and the <think>/<answer> template.
  * Mitigation/tool models, not panel members: MAIRA-2 (gated MSRLA, grounded findings) or CheXOne grounding as a 'counter-evidence' verifier; retrieval over MIMIC-CXR / ReXGradient-160K reports (both access-controlled) or over the study's own real-image bank; do not put report-only models (MAIRA-2, LLaVA-Rad, SRRG, SLaVA-CXR) in the yes/no panel.

-- gaps_and_opportunities --
  * No 2026 paper evaluates small (<=4B) open medical VLMs on support-device/tube/line existence with abstention-rewarded scoring. HalluCXR (arXiv 2605.20469, May 2026) uses 856 real MIMIC-CXR images, three query types incl. targeted YES/NO, an 8-category taxonomy with no device category, ECE but no abstention option, six models (BiomedCLIP, Gemini 3 Flash, GPT-5.1, InternVL2-6B, LLaVA-NeXT-Mistral-7B, Qwen2-VL-7B), ensemble + length-based mitigation only, code 'upon acceptance'. Position TriPOPE-CXR as the device-level, counterfactual, abstention-aware complement.
  * Lotfinia et al., 'Vision-language models for chest radiography do not always need the image' (arXiv 2606.17710, v3 Oct 2026; MIT code at github.com/mahshadlotfinia/causal) is the direct precedent for the blind/no-image arm: 2,548 yes/no questions (MS-CXR, MIMIC-CXR incl. a 50/50 support-devices block, ReXErr-v1), prompt 'Is [finding] present in this chest X-ray? Answer with a single word: Yes or No.', image-swap/occlusion/removal/noise controls, MedGemma-1.5-4B and LLaVA-Med-7B among eight systems, non-answers excluded from denominators. It has no edited or synthetic images, no abstention reward, no mitigation and no small CXR-specialist (CheXOne/CheXagent). Reuse its prompt and swap/no-image controls, report its metrics (retention under image removal, opposite-label flip rate) alongside Effective Reliability, and cite its MedGemma-1.5-4B numbers (~58.2 pooled acc; 42.4% retention without image) as an external anchor.
  * Counterfactual device removal on real CXRs has no public benchmark: Chest ImaGenome, MS-CXR and VinDr-CXR boxes cover findings/anatomy, not tubes/lines; Object-CXR (foreign objects with boxes) is in CheXOne's training list but its current access terms are unverified. A LaMa-removed line/tube arm plus a layout-matched synthetic CXR is therefore novel and, because the edited and generated images are unseen by every model, the only contamination-free arm.
  * Training-set contamination is severe for the specialists: CheXOne lists MIMIC-CXR, CheXpert Plus, ReXGradient, VQA-RAD, SLAKE, MIMIC-CXR-VQA, ReXVQA, VinDr-CXR, MS-CXR, Object-CXR, PadChest, ChestXray14, RSNA, SIIM among 30 training sources (only official test splits held out); MedGemma 1.5 trained on MIMIC-CXR + Chest ImaGenome; Lingshu and CheXagent on MIMIC-CXR. Draw real items only from official test splits (MIMIC-CXR test, VinDr-CXR 3,000-image test) and flag contamination per model in the validity appendix.
  * Harness gap: lmms-eval (main, Oct 2026) ships slake (BoKelvin/SLAKE, exact match after alias normalisation, closed/open and X-ray breakdowns), medqa (text), pope, amber_g, hallusion_bench, refcoco_grounding, but no vqa_rad, pathvqa, pmc_vqa, omnimedvqa, medxpert or any medical yes/no task; VLMEvalKit has OmniMedVQA, MedXpertQA_MM_test, WorldMedQA-V, Medqbench, POPE/HallusionBench/AMBER YORN, and Gemma3/Gemma4/Qwen3-VL-2B/LFM2-VL classes but no MedGemma, CheXagent, CheXOne, Lingshu or LLaVA-Med entries. A released lmms-eval task yaml for a CXR device-existence POPE with abstention scoring is a small, citable artifact.
  * Grounding-based mitigation is now feasible on small models but unevaluated for devices: MedGemma 1.5 (Chest ImaGenome anatomy IoU 38.0) and CheXOne (MS-CXR phrase-grounding mIoU 0.608, VinDr abnormality mIoU 0.452) can be asked to box the queried object before answering; box-absence or box/answer inconsistency becomes an abstention trigger. The closest 2026 method, 'Hallucination Detection and Correction in Medical VLMs via Counter-Evidence Verification' (arXiv 2606.18609, +11.9% hallucination reduction on report generation), releases code only upon acceptance and does not target devices.
  * Retrieval-based mitigation has no small-model CXR precedent for existence questions; HeteroRAG (arXiv 2508.12778) and AMANDA (arXiv 2510.02328) are the nearest 2025 references but use general medical VQA sets. Report retrieval needs MIMIC-CXR reports (PhysioNet credentialed) or ReXGradient-160K (HF gated-auto, custom 'rexgradient' license); a retrieval arm restricted to the study's own labelled real-image bank avoids both.
  * Calibration/abstention literature in 2026 (arXiv 2604.02543: Qwen3-VL/InternVL3/LLaVA-NeXT 2B-38B overconfident on three medical VQA sets, HAC mitigation; 2606.27023: fine-tuning MedGemma 4B/Qwen2-VL-7B for verbalised confidence, >=60% calibration-error reduction; 2606.16583, EMNLP Findings: 8 UE methods x 12 VLMs, uncertainty barely moves under NOTA perturbations) never combines Kalai-style confidence-target instructions with Effective Reliability on CXR device existence, and none uses counterfactual images.
  * Data access for a student: MIMIC-CXR-JPG, MIMIC-CXR-VQA (377k QA incl. presence and devices/tubes/lines categories, 500-image test), MS-CXR, Chest ImaGenome, VinDr-CXR (18k DICOM, 22 local labels, 3,000-image test) and RadVLM all sit behind PhysioNet credentialing (CITI course + DUA) - start day one; ReXVQA/ReXGradient-160K are gated-auto on HF; GEMeX-VQA QA files are on HF (no license field) with MIMIC-sourced images. If credentialing is late, the edited/synthetic arms plus a small public non-PhysioNet real set keep the study alive.

-- cautions --
  * fp16 on a T4 is unsafe for the Gemma family: Gemma 3 activations exceed the fp16 range (HF transformers PR #37226, issue #39972), so MedGemma 1.0/1.5 and Gemma 3 4B need bf16 (pod), fp32, or NF4 with fp32 compute on a T4. Google's own CXR-localisation notebook states a Colab T4 with torch.bfloat16 (emulated, slow; speed unverified). Gemma 4 E2B fp16 behaviour is unverified.
  * Qwen2-VL fp16 is documented to produce gibberish (HF issues #33294, #35151): MedVLM-R1, Med-R1 and Qwen2-VL-2B must run in bf16 or fp32 on a T4. Qwen2.5-VL/Qwen3-VL fp16 stability is unverified; compare any quantised or fp16 run against bf16 on ~20 items before scoring.
  * MedGemma quantisation: Google-Health/medgemma issue #5 reports 8-bit bitsandbytes gibberish with the reference code (closed Sep 2026 without a fix); NF4 community reports exist but the Medium write-up was unreadable (403). Treat NF4 MedGemma on the 1060 as triage only.
  * Transformers version matrix forces one venv per family: CheXagent-2-3b pins transformers==4.40.0 with trust_remote_code; MAIRA-2 needs >=4.48,<4.52; Lingshu recommends 4.52.1; Qwen3-VL and LFM2-VL need >=4.57; CheXOne and Qwen3.5 need transformers from main; lmms-eval/VLMEvalKit pins may conflict with all of these.
  * Licenses that matter for the report: MedGemma under HAI-DEF (gated auto; outputs yours; no clinical use; derivatives carry the terms); Gemma 3 4B under Gemma terms (gated manual); CheXOne cc-by-nc-4.0 on HF but CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 in the paper (assume ND: no redistributed fine-tunes); Qwen2.5-VL-3B is Qwen Research License, not Apache; MAIRA-2 MSRLA gated research-only; LLaVA-Med/LLaVA-Rad research-only (MSR code license, LLaVA-Med data CC BY-NC 4.0); CheXagent-8b has no license field (GitHub 'Other'); Med-Flamingo has none; RadVLM needs PhysioNet credentialing (CITI + DUA), HF repo returns 401.
  * Download sizes: CheXagent-8b and MAIRA-2 are stored in F32 (33 GB and 27.5 GB), CheXagent-2-3b F32 12.6 GB; budget pod disk and transfer time accordingly.
  * Report-only models (MAIRA-2, LLaVA-Rad, CheXpert-Plus SRRG, SLaVA-CXR) do not follow yes/no instructions; excluding them from the panel is a design decision to state explicitly. MedVLM-R1/Med-R1 expect MCQ with <think>/<answer> tags, so a yes/no item must be recast as a two-option MCQ, which is not identical to plain yes/no prompting on the other models.
  * Matched-pair confounds: MedGemma vs Gemma 3 changes the vision encoder (MedSigLIP), CheXOne vs Qwen2.5-VL-3B fully fine-tunes the encoder, Lingshu vs Qwen2.5-VL-7B adds 12-modality data; name the confound when interpreting 'medical fine-tuning' effects.
  * Numbers from the ReXrank leaderboard (MIMIC-CXR 'RadGraph' column: CheXagent 0.474, CheXpertPlus-MIMIC 0.485, MAIRA-2 0.517, CheXOne-R1 0.519, MedGemma 0.549, MedVersa 0.55) are site columns with unspecified model versions and a composite ranking; cite only for orientation. Qwen3.5 and Gemma 4 medical rows are vendor-reported and partly thinking-mode.
  * Unverified because the shared web-search budget ran out at the end: Kaggle's VinDr-CXR mirror terms; LLaVA-Med's own fine-tuned VQA table (images); BiomedGPT score provenance (search snippet only); Hulu-Med-4B numbers; whether lmms-eval's gemma3/qwen2_5_vl wrappers load MedGemma/CheXOne unmodified; GMAI-MMBench support in VLMEvalKit (not found in the registry files read); Med-R1 Qwen2.5-VL-3B checkpoints on HF; Object-CXR access; exact RAD-DINO/MedSigLIP HF ids.

-- confidence_notes -- All HF ids, licenses, gating flags, parameter counts and creation dates were read from the HF API or model cards on 2026-10-12; paper claims come from arXiv abstract/HTML pages, GitHub READMEs, the HAI-DEF model card and PhysioNet pages, as cited per item. Memory fit figures are estimates from parameter counts (NF4 about 0.6-0.7 bytes/param including overhead) rather than measurements, and fp16 stability on a T4 is documented only for Gemma 3 and Qwen2-VL. The web-search budget was exhausted near the end; the items listed in the final caution were not re-verified and remaining fetches relied on direct URLs. High confidence in the panel recommendation and the gap statements about HalluCXR, Lotfinia et al., CheXOne and the harness task lists; medium confidence in the 6 GB NF4 quality of MedGemma and in harness loading of the medical models without wrapper edits.


######## ANGLE: med_mitigation ########
angle: Grounding- and retrieval-based hallucination mitigation for chest X-ray (radiology) VLMs, evaluated for what one undergraduate can run training-free on a GTX 1060 + Kaggle + under $40 of pod time, including whether the plan's general-domain VCD-style arms survive on medical images and which open, non-credentialed corpora and detectors make the X-ray version executable.

[1] RULE: Reliable Multimodal RAG for Factuality in Medical VLMs (2024 (EMNLP main); EMNLP 2024 (main); code https://github.com/richard-peng-xia/RULE (MIT)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.05131
    what: Retrieves training-set reports for a query X-ray with a contrastively trained ResNet-50 + BioClinicalBERT retriever (corpus 1,035 IU-Xray / 3,000 MIMIC-CXR pairs), calibrates how many contexts to show, and LoRA-tunes LLaVA-Med-1.5 (7B) on cases where retrieval caused over-reliance. VQA accuracy: IU-Xray 75.47 -> 87.84, MIMIC-CXR 75.79 -> 83.92; report-generation BLEU 9.64 -> 27.53 on IU-Xray.
    why: Its ablation is the key number for a training-free study: basic RAG alone moved MIMIC-CXR VQA from 75.79 to 67.35 (a loss) and IU-Xray only 75.47 -> 77.15; the calibrated context count recovered 76.54 / 78.62 and the rest of the gain needed fine-tuning. So a plug-in 'retrieve similar reports' arm on a 2-4B model should be pre-registered as likely null-to-negative, with the number of retrieved contexts as a swept factor. Corpora (MIMIC-CXR, IU-Xray, FairVLMed) all require access applications.
    code/data: https://github.com/richard-peng-xia/RULE (MIT); needs MIMIC-CXR (PhysioNet credentialed) and IU-Xray

[2] MMed-RAG: Versatile Multimodal RAG System for Medical VLMs (2025 (ICLR); ICLR 2025; code https://github.com/richard-peng-xia/MMed-RAG (MIT)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13085
    what: Domain-aware retrieval (per-domain ResNet-50/BioClinicalBERT retrievers over training-set reports, BiomedCLIP router), adaptive context selection, and RAG-based preference fine-tuning of LLaVA-Med-1.5 (7B) across radiology, ophthalmology, pathology. IU-Xray VQA: base 68.99 -> +retrieval 77.12 -> +context selection 79.56 -> full 85.80.
    why: It quantifies the two RAG failure modes the study must control for: with a noisy image from a different ground truth the model still answered 'correctly' 55.08% of the time from the retrieved text alone (ignores the image), and 43.31% of originally-correct answers flipped to wrong after retrieval (over-reliance; 8.38% after tuning). The retrieval-only rows (+8 to +10 points on IU-Xray without fine-tuning) are the best-case prior for a training-free similar-case arm; 'over-reliance rate' and the swapped-image test are ready-made controls.
    code/data: https://github.com/richard-peng-xia/MMed-RAG (MIT); datasets need access applications (MIMIC-CXR, IU-Xray, FairVLMed, PMC-OA, Quilt-1M)

[3] Retrieval for chest X-ray report generation: V-RAG, FactMM-RAG and CXR-RePaiR (2021 / 2025; V-RAG: AAAI-25 GenAI4Health workshop; FactMM-RAG: NAACL 2025 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.15268); CXR-RePaiR: ML4H 2021 (https://proceedings.mlr.press/v158/endo21a.html)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.15040
    what: V-RAG retrieves similar MIMIC-CXR image-report pairs with BiomedCLIP + FAISS and feeds images and text to RadFM / LLaVA-1.5-7B, but only after fine-tuning for multi-image awareness; entity-probing (yes/no 'is entity X present') F1 0.751 on MIMIC-CXR and a ~19% relative RadGraph-F1 gain. FactMM-RAG trains a RadGraph-aware retriever (+6.5 F1CheXbert, +2 F1RadGraph). CXR-RePaiR is the canonical training-free retrieval-as-generation baseline (CLIP retrieval; CheXbert F1 0.352).
    why: Together they define the retrieval family the umbrella asks for and show that every published gain on X-rays comes with retriever training or VLM fine-tuning; none tests a frozen 2-4B model. V-RAG's entity probing is the closest published analogue of the plan's yes/no object-existence items and is the metric to cite when positioning. There is no paper titled 'RadRAG' that I could find (see cautions).
    code/data: V-RAG: no code URL in the paper; FactMM-RAG: none given in abstract; all need MIMIC-CXR (credentialed)

[4] When Retrieval Hurts: Retrieval-Induced Hallucination in CXR Report Generation (2026 (arXiv Sept 2026); arXiv preprint) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31733
    what: Defines retrieval-induced hallucination (a finding present in the generated report and the retrieved evidence but absent from the reference) with a coincidence control (0.18 base rate). With LLaVA-1.5-7B and BiomedCLIP retrieval over a 400-study MIMIC-CXR pool: RIH rate 0.76 with relevant retrieval, 0.98 with mismatched retrieval, 0.00 image-only; copy rate 0.91 / 1.00; clinical F1 0.201 -> 0.402 (relevant) but 0.043 (mismatched); normal images retrieved abnormal precedents in 24/28 cases. Label-agreement gating (Jaccard >= 0.2) removed 61% of unsupported evidence at 0.60 coverage; cosine-similarity gating did not help.
    why: This is the direct critique the advisor's 'retrieval-based' mitigation must answer and it supplies the mismatched-retrieval control, the copy-rate metric, and the finding that similarity-score gating fails while label-agreement gating works. Only a general 7B model was tested, leaving 2-4B and yes/no questions open.
    code/data: Evaluation protocol promised on acceptance; reports regenerable only under PhysioNet terms (MIMIC-CXR)

[5] MC-CXR: Multi-Context Chest X-ray Benchmark for Context-Induced Disruption (2026 (arXiv Aug 2026); arXiv preprint; data on PhysioNet (credentialed)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24118
    what: 240 radiologist-curated MIMIC-CXR cases expanded to 2,522 instances under 11 context conditions (reliable same-patient text, misleading rewritten text, cross-patient prior images). Ten models incl. MedGemma-1.5-4B and NV-Reason-CXR-3B: misleading text flipped 45.6-78.1% of correct image-only answers, 64.2-85.3% of flips following the suggested false diagnosis; misleading visual context flipped 35.7-61.7% with only 16.5-18.0% alignment; even reliable matching context destabilised 13.3-42.4% of answers; image-only accuracy 8.3-35.8%.
    why: Shows at exactly the 3-4B scale the plan uses that added text context (what RAG injects) overrides the image, and that irrelevant retrieved images confuse without biasing. Its 'reliable / misleading text / misleading visual' conditions are the template for the plan's retrieval controls; its 12-finding label space and flip-rate metric are citeable. Needs PhysioNet credentialing to reuse.
    code/data: PhysioNet Credentialed Health Data License (images not redistributed); no GitHub URL found in the text read

[6] Why text-level RAG causes copying, plus the open text corpora for knowledge-grounded prompting (MICCAI 2026 feature-level RAG; expert evaluation of medical RAG; MedRAG toolkit) (2024-2026; MICCAI 2026; arXiv 2511.06738; ACL 2024 Findings (MedRAG, https://github.com/Teddy-XiongGZ/MedRAG)) verified=True
    url: https://papers.miccai.org/miccai-2026/0889-Paper3169.html
    what: The MICCAI 2026 paper measures a vision-attention ratio of 0.06 for LLaVA-Med-1.5 on MIMIC-CXR that falls further when retrieved reports are appended as text (rising to 0.27 with its trained feature-level injection; negation-aware retrieval alone +5.36 pp on MIMIC-CXR; code not released). The text-only expert study (200 queries, 18 experts, 80,502 annotations, GPT-4o and Llama-3.1-8B) found only 22% of top-16 passages relevant and factuality/completeness drops of up to 6%/5% under standard RAG, recovered by evidence filtering and query reformulation. MedRAG provides retrievers (BM25, MedCPT, Contriever, SPECTER) and corpora (StatPearls 9.3k docs/301k snippets, 18 textbooks/125.8k snippets, PubMed, Wikipedia) under a public-domain notice.
    why: Gives the mechanism (attention leaves the image) that predicts text-RAG will raise yes-bias on X-ray existence questions, and the cheapest 'retrieval-based' arm that needs no credentialed images: a StatPearls/textbook snippet about the queried device or finding, with an irrelevant-snippet control, following the ACL-2024 recipe.
    code/data: MedRAG: https://github.com/Teddy-XiongGZ/MedRAG (LICENSE is a US-Government public-domain notice); corpora via HF datasets; MICCAI code 'N/A'

[7] CCD: Clinical Contrastive Decoding (training-free expert-grounded decoding for radiology MLLMs) (2025 (arXiv) / 2026 (Findings of ACL); Findings of ACL 2026 (https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.1755/); code https://github.com/X-iZhang/CCD (MIT)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23379
    what: Training-free, retrieval-free two-stage decoding: (1) symptom-grounded stage appends an expert-derived prompt ('Attention to: Atelectasis, Cardiomegaly ...' from torchxrayvision densenet121-res224-chex probabilities > 0.5) and blends logits with weight alpha; (2) expert-informed stage adds a clipped log-odds bias log(s/(1-s)) from the classifier (likelihood-ratio clip gamma in {2,5,10}, weight beta). Also uses MedSigLIP zero-shot for 13 device types. On MIMIC-CXR report generation RadGraph-F1 16.23 -> 19.01 (+17.13%); VCD, OPERA and ICD underperform it; VQA on Medical-CXR-VQA (78,124 pairs) also reported. Models: MAIRA-2, LLaVA-Med-1.5, Libra 3B/7B, LLaVA-Rad.
    why: This is the nearest prior for a 'detector-verified answer' arm and it is trivially portable to yes/no questions: with first-token yes/no logits already cached by the plan's Step 6, the expert bias is a CPU-only post-hoc fusion. Its two stages map onto two of the recommended arms (knowledge-grounded prompt; expert logit bias). The released code only wraps Libra/LLaVA-style models, so the Qwen3-VL port is a reimplementation of one equation, not a dependency.
    code/data: https://github.com/X-iZhang/CCD (MIT); experts: torchxrayvision densenet121-res224-chex, google/medsiglip-448; VQA data on PhysioNet

[8] Does VCD work on medical images? Evidence from VGS-Decoding, MedHEval and Med-VCD (2025-2026; VGS-Decoding arXiv 2603.20314; MedHEval arXiv 2503.02157 (code https://github.com/Aofei-Chang/MedHEval); Med-VCD Computers in Biology and Medicine 2026 (arXiv 2512.01922)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.20314
    what: MedHEval benchmarks seven training-free decoders on LLaVA-Med-1.5 over 9,197 closed-ended items from SLAKE/VQA-RAD/IU-Xray/MIMIC-CXR: overall accuracy base 0.499, VCD 0.491, DoLa 0.503, OPERA 0.514, AVISC 0.499, M3ID 0.499, DAMRO 0.490, PAI 0.514 (CheXagent 3B scored 0.739 but was not run with mitigations). Med-VCD reports LLaVA-Med-1.5 MIMIC-CXR VQA 75.79 -> VCD 70.89 -> Med-VCD 82.95 (no code found). VGS-Decoding (training-free, reweights tokens by (P_orig - P_noise)/(P_orig + P_noise); 2x passes) is the only one tested on 3-4B models (CheXagent 3B, MedGemma 4B, LLaVA-Med 7B) and reports up to +9.12% on MIMIC-Diff-VQA while VCD gave -5.93%, DoLa -6.30%, OPERA -5.25% on some models.
    why: Three independent sources agree that plain VCD is null-to-negative on chest X-ray VQA, which demotes the plan's VCD/VDD arm from headline mitigation to a general-domain reference row and makes the Mirage/Rethinking-CD controls (which have never been run on medical images or below 7B) a contribution in themselves. VGS-Decoding is the one contrastive variant worth adding because it already has 3-4B medical numbers; its code is promised 'upon acceptance'.
    code/data: MedHEval: https://github.com/Aofei-Chang/MedHEval; VGS-Decoding and Med-VCD: no code found

[9] ARCD: Anatomical Region-Guided Contrastive Decoding (mask-guided plug-in for medical VLMs) (2025 (arXiv Dec 2025); arXiv preprint; code https://github.com/ecoxial2007/ARegionCD (MIT)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.17189
    what: Training-free decoding that converts anatomical masks (expert or automatic torchxrayvision PSPNet) into token-, attention- and logit-level re-weighting (alpha ~0.01, beta 3-5, gamma 1.3-1.5). Phi-3.5V-Med (4.2B), LLaVA-Med-7B, HuatuoV-34B; MIMIC-Ext-VQA fine-tuned +8.66%, SLAKE zero-shot +5.11%, OBScan +3.94%; VCD on the same data +0.40 / +0.79 / +1.18. Automatic PSPNet masks are coarse (precision 0.70 at IoU 0.25, 0.06 at IoU 0.75) and give lower zero-shot accuracy (48.03%) than expert masks.
    why: Nearest prior for a region-grounded arm at 4B scale, and it demonstrates that free anatomy masks (CheXmask / torchxrayvision PSPNet) are good enough to move accuracy. Its ablations test label vs box vs mask prompts but never a random or wrong-region mask, so a random-region control is an open, cheap contribution. Released code targets Phi-3.5V only.
    code/data: https://github.com/ecoxial2007/ARegionCD (MIT); masks via torchxrayvision PSPNet or expert annotation

[10] Zoom-and-verify abstention and visual prompting: EVADE, HEAL-MedVQA/LobA, Visual Prompt Engineering in Radiology (2024-2026; EVADE arXiv 2608.18833; HEAL-MedVQA IJCAI 2025 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00744); VPE ECCV 2024 workshop / MIDL 2025 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15802, code https://github.com/MIC-DKFZ/VPE-in-Radiology)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18833
    what: EVADE (training-free, Qwen2.5-VL-7B only): when combined confidence c = lambda*c_verbal + (1-lambda)*c_seq is low (~44% of items) the model outputs a box, the crop is re-asked, and it commits only if full-image and zoomed answers agree with max confidence above tau, else abstains; ECE 0.241 -> 0.184 (VQA-RAD), 0.393 -> 0.282 (SLAKE), 0.317 -> 0.173 (PathVQA) at coverage 0.968 / 0.849 / 0.705 with ~2.1 passes per item; chain-of-thought collapsed VQA-RAD accuracy to 0.554 and self-verification worsened ECE to 0.519. HEAL-MedVQA (67K items with doctor masks) shows medical LMMs answer from linguistic patterns or irrelevant regions; its LobA fix is training-based. VPE shows drawn boxes/arrows/circles raise CLIP zero-shot AUROC by up to 0.185 on four CXR sets.
    why: EVADE is the nearest prior for a 'region-focused second pass with agree-or-abstain' arm and shows CoT and naive self-verification are not free wins; for 2B models that ground poorly, crops can come from detector boxes or CheXmask lung/heart masks instead of the model's own box. The random-crop and wrong-region controls are absent from all three papers.
    code/data: EVADE: no code URL; VPE: https://github.com/MIC-DKFZ/VPE-in-Radiology; HEAL-MedVQA: CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 paper, data URL not in abstract

[11] Confidence gating on medical VQA: 'Uncertainty Is Not a Safety Net' and HalluCXR (2026; Findings of EMNLP 2026; HalluCXR arXiv 2605.20469 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20469)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16583
    what: The EMNLP paper benchmarks 8 uncertainty estimators across 12 VLMs on clinical VQA: uncertainty quality tracks accuracy and degrades where models are weakest, and under none-of-the-above perturbations accuracy collapses while uncertainty barely moves, so uncertainty is a diagnostic for fragile predictions rather than a safety net. HalluCXR (856 MIMIC-CXR images; prompts include 'Is there evidence of [finding]? Answer YES or NO first'; BiomedCLIP, Gemini 3 Flash, GPT-5.1, InternVL2, LLaVA-NeXT-Mistral 7B, Qwen2-VL 7B) finds 61.9-82.3% of outputs contain hallucinations; finding-level ensembles cut fabrication by 83.6-84.8% at the cost of omissions, and response length alone predicts hallucination risk (AUC up to 0.908).
    why: These set expectations for the plan's abstention-gating arm (Step 6 signals) on X-rays: report risk-coverage per arm but also the NOTA-style perturbation check, and compare the logit gate against a trivial length gate and a random gate at equal coverage. HalluCXR's yes/no prompt is the closest published CXR existence question; its data needs PhysioNet credentialing and code is 'upon acceptance'.
    code/data: HalluCXR: MIMIC-CXR-JPG under PhysioNet credentialed access, code promised; EMNLP paper: no code URL in abstract

[12] Open detector and segmenter stack: torchxrayvision, ChestX-Det, CheXmask (2020-2023 (maintained); MIDL 2022 library paper; ChestX-Det https://github.com/Deepwise-AILab/ChestX-Det-Dataset; CheXmask https://physionet.org/content/chexmask-cxr-segmentation-data/1.0.0/) verified=True
    url: https://github.com/mlmed/torchxrayvision
    what: torchxrayvision (Apache-2.0, pip) ships DenseNet-121 224-px classifiers (-all, -rsna, -nih, -pc, -chex, -mimic_nb, -mimic_ch), resnet50-res512-all, 18 pathologies, and the ChestX-Det PSPNet with 14 anatomical classes (lungs, heart, clavicles, aorta, mediastinum, spine, diaphragm...). ChestX-Det: 3,578 NIH ChestX-ray14 images with radiologist boxes and polygons for 13 abnormality classes, Apache-2.0, direct zip download. CheXmask: 657,566 lung and heart masks (RLE) for ChestX-ray8, CheXpert, MIMIC-CXR-JPG, PadChest and VinDr-CXR with per-mask RCA quality scores, CC BY 4.0 open access on PhysioNet, no images included.
