    why: Everything needed for detector-verified answers, anatomy-guided crops and removal-mask construction runs on the GTX 1060 in minutes and needs no credentialing when paired with NIH images (CheXmask IDs match ChestX-ray8). Keep the detectors out of ground-truth labelling (use the radiologist boxes/labels for that) to avoid circularity between the oracle and the mitigation arm.
    code/data: torchxrayvision Apache-2.0; ChestX-Det Apache-2.0 (http://resource.deepwise.com/ChestX-Det/train_data.zip); CheXmask CC BY 4.0 (PhysioNet open access)

[13] Tubes, lines and devices: RANZCR CLiP labels and the ianpan/cxr-lines-and-tubes detector (plus CANDID-PTX chest tubes) (2021 / 2025; RANZCR CLiP: Kaggle competition + Scientific Data 2021 (https://www.kaggle.com/competitions/ranzcr-clip-catheter-line-classification); CANDID-PTX: Radiology: AI 2021 (DOI 10.17608/k6.auckland.14173982)) verified=True
    url: https://huggingface.co/ianpan/cxr-lines-and-tubes
    what: RANZCR CLiP annotates 30,083 NIH ChestX-ray14 radiographs with 11 image-level labels (ETT/NGT/CVC normal, borderline, abnormal; NGT incompletely imaged; Swan-Ganz present) and polyline tracings for ~9k images. The ianpan model card (EfficientNet-B1 U-Net device detection and tip localisation, carina heatmap, 5-fold classifier, FastViT presence screen; validation mean AUROC 0.9684; transformers pipeline with trust_remote_code; 384-1024 px input) is trained on those 30,083 images plus 31,448 NIH images; weights research-only, code MIT. CANDID-PTX adds 19,237 CXRs with chest-tube, pneumothorax and rib-fracture segmentations after an online ethics-course registration.
    why: This is the only non-credentialed source of radiologist device labels with geometry on CC-licensed images, so it is the natural object class for 'object-existence' questions (ETT, NGT, CVC, Swan-Ganz) and the polylines give LaMa removal masks for the edited arm; the detector is the verification tool and the 'expert' for the detector-gated arm. Kaggle's data terms were not readable from here (see cautions).
    code/data: HF ianpan/cxr-lines-and-tubes ('research-only-weights-and-mit-code'); RANZCR CLiP on Kaggle (license not verified); CANDID-PTX via figshare DOI with ethics-course registration (not opened)

[14] Domain models that fit the 2-4B budget: CheXagent-2-3b, MedGemma 1.5 4B, NV-Reason-CXR-3B (and MAIRA-2 as the 7B grounding reference) (2024-2026; HF model cards: https://huggingface.co/google/medgemma-1.5-4b-it, https://huggingface.co/nvidia/NV-Reason-CXR-3B, https://huggingface.co/microsoft/maira-2) verified=True
    url: https://huggingface.co/StanfordAIMI/CheXagent-2-3b
    what: CheXagent-2-3b (card: MIT; repo LICENSE file: CC BY-NC-ND 4.0; bf16, trust_remote_code) lists tasks incl. abnormality_detection, chest_tube_detection, foreign_objects_detection, phrase_grounding and VQA. MedGemma 1.5 4B-it (13 Jan 2026, Health AI Developer Foundations terms, bf16, 896 px -> 256 tokens) adds bounding-box localisation on CXRs; MIMIC-CXR macro-F1 89.5, VQA-RAD closed 70.2%. NV-Reason-CXR-3B (Qwen2.5-VL-3B + SFT/GRPO, NVIDIA OneWay Non-Commercial, bf16; trained on MIMIC-CXR, ChestX-ray14 and CheXpert). MAIRA-2 (Vicuna-7B + RAD-DINO, MSRLA, research-only) emits grounded findings with boxes.
    why: Gives a clean aligned-vs-base pair to replace SENTINEL-vs-base: MedGemma 1.5 4B against its general Gemma 3 4B backbone (same architecture per the model-card summaries). CheXagent-2-3b is the cheapest CXR specialist and already appears in VGS-Decoding and MedHEval. NV-Reason-CXR-3B was trained on ChestX-ray14, so it must be treated as contaminated for NIH-based items; MAIRA-2 is pod-only (7B, MSRLA) and best used as a grounding oracle, not a hero model.
    code/data: CheXagent-2-3b: MIT on card vs CC BY-NC-ND in repo; MedGemma: HAI-DEF terms (gated); NV-Reason-CXR-3B: NVIDIA OneWay Non-Commercial; MAIRA-2: MSRLA

[15] Retrieval embeddings without training: MedSigLIP and BiomedCLIP (2023 / 2025; HF model cards; BiomedCLIP https://huggingface.co/microsoft/BiomedCLIP-PubMedBERT_256-vit_base_patch16_224) verified=True
    url: https://huggingface.co/google/medsiglip-448
    what: MedSigLIP (0.9B two-tower, 448 px, HAI-DEF terms, gated) is trained on de-identified medical pairs including MIMIC-CXR and is recommended for 'semantic image retrieval' and zero-shot classification (CXR zero-shot AUC 0.844 over 13 findings); CCD uses it for zero-shot device detection. BiomedCLIP (MIT, PMC-15M figure-caption pairs, open_clip) is the retriever used by V-RAG and by 'When Retrieval Hurts', and is intended for research only.
    why: Either embeds a 30k-image open corpus on the GTX 1060 in well under an evening and makes similar-case retrieval (image-to-image kNN with labels) a training-free arm; using both gives a retriever-choice ablation. MedSigLIP's MIMIC-CXR training is irrelevant to leakage if the corpus is NIH-based, but its zero-shot device scores could also serve as a second, independent 'expert' signal.
    code/data: google/medsiglip-448 (HAI-DEF terms, gated); microsoft/BiomedCLIP-PubMedBERT_256-vit_base_patch16_224 (MIT)

[16] External tools as factual assessors: MedRAX, FactCheXcker, Expert-CFG, ChatCAD, MedPromptX (2023-2025; MedRAX ICML 2025 (arXiv 2502.02673, Apache-2.0); FactCheXcker CVPR 2025 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.18672); Expert-CFG arXiv 2507.09209; ChatCAD arXiv 2302.07257; MedPromptX MICCAI 2024 workshops (https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.15585)) verified=True
    url: https://github.com/bowang-lab/MedRAX
    what: MedRAX orchestrates torchxrayvision DenseNet-121, MedSAM and ChestX-Det PSPNet segmentation, MAIRA-2 grounding, CheXagent/LLaVA-Med VQA and RoentGen under GPT-4o, with ChestAgentBench (2,500 queries from 675 cases). FactCheXcker post-hoc corrects measurement claims (ETT placement) in reports from 11 models, improving 10 of 11. Expert-CFG triggers expert refinement when a 4.2B MedVLM is uncertain, training-free, matching 13B models. ChatCAD (2023) is the canonical 'classifier outputs -> text -> LLM' design. MedPromptX combines Grounding DINO crops with similarity-selected few-shot examples for Med-Flamingo (+11% F1 on a MIMIC-derived VQA set).
    why: These are the citations that make 'detector-verified answers' a recognised family rather than an ad-hoc trick, and they show that the verifier can be a small classifier, not a second VLM. None reports the two controls a reviewer will ask for: detector-alone accuracy (is the VLM adding anything?) and a shuffled-expert condition (is the gain just the bias term?).
    code/data: MedRAX Apache-2.0; MedPromptX https://github.com/BioMedIA-MBZUAI/MedPromptX (license not shown); FactCheXcker code linked from paper; Expert-CFG none found

[17] Non-credentialed CXR corpora and QA sets vs the credentialed ones (2017-2025; NIH ChestX-ray14 (NIH Clinical Center); ProbMed ACL 2025 Findings (https://github.com/UCSB-AI/ProbMed, MIT, HF rippleripple/ProbMed); VQA-RAD (https://huggingface.co/datasets/flaviagiammarino/vqa-rad, CC0); SLAKE (https://huggingface.co/datasets/BoKelvin/SLAKE, CC BY 4.0); VinDr-CXR-VQA (https://huggingface.co/datasets/Dangindev/VinDR-CXR-VQA, CC BY 4.0, no images); Open-i IU collection (HF mirror https://huggingface.co/datasets/ykumards/open-i, listed CC BY-NC-ND 4.0, 3,851 exams with findings/impression/MeSH)) verified=True
    url: https://docs.cloud.google.com/healthcare-api/docs/resources/public-datasets/nih-chest
    what: NIH ChestX-ray14 (112k images, 14 labels, a small BBox_List_2017 set) carries no usage restrictions beyond attribution per the Google Cloud page (Kaggle lists it as CC0 per search results). ProbMed pairs each question with a negated/hallucinated-attribute question on 6,303 images (ChestX-ray14 + MedICaT), 57,132 QA pairs, where GPT-4o scored 29.30% vs a 35.67% random baseline on finding identification. Everything MIMIC-derived (MIMIC-CXR-JPG 377,110 images; VinDr-CXR 18,000 scans with 22 box-level findings but no device labels; Chest ImaGenome; MedVH; HalluCXR; MC-CXR; Medical-CXR-VQA) is under the PhysioNet Credentialed Health Data License (CITI course + DUA). ReXVQA (653,834 questions incl. presence and negation; MedGemma 83.24%) sits under a non-commercial gated ReXGradient agreement.
    why: Decides the data question: a 4-6-week project should build its real arm on NIH images with RANZCR device labels, ChestX-Det boxes, CheXmask masks and Open-i reports, use ProbMed's negation-pair design for question construction, and treat MIMIC-based sets as citations only. VinDr-CXR-VQA's 'Is there' questions with boxes are reusable only if the VinDr images are obtained via PhysioNet.
    code/data: NIH: https://nihcc.app.box.com/v/ChestXray-NIHCC (attribution required); ProbMed MIT; VQA-RAD CC0; SLAKE CC BY 4.0; Open-i mirror CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 (NLM terms not re-read); MIMIC/VinDr/MedVH: PhysioNet credentialed

[18] RadEdit (and RoentGen) for the edited and generated X-ray arms (2024 (ECCV); ECCV 2024 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.12865); RoentGen Nature BME 2024 (https://github.com/StanfordMIMI/RoentGen)) verified=True
    url: https://huggingface.co/microsoft/radedit
    what: RadEdit is a 512x512 latent diffusion model (0.4B U-Net, BioViL-T text encoder, SDXL-VAE) trained on 487,680 frontal CXRs (MIMIC-CXR, ChestX-ray8, CheXpert) that edits a radiograph from a text prompt while masks restrict change to chosen regions; weights under MSRLA (research and reproducibility only), editing pipeline MIT, loadable with diffusers. RoentGen weights are released only to researchers already credentialed for MIMIC-CXR (Google form).
    why: SD 1.5 + ControlNet has no CXR prior, so RadEdit is the realistic replacement for the plan's LaMa/SD edited arm and its generated twin on X-rays: mask-restricted removal of a tube or finding, and whole-image re-synthesis from the same report. At 0.4B parameters it is plausibly within a 6 GB Pascal card in fp16, but that is untested here and bf16 is unavailable on the 1060.
    code/data: microsoft/radedit: weights MSRLA, pipeline MIT; RoentGen: credentialed-researcher form

-- gaps_and_opportunities --
  * No paper tests any retrieval or grounding mitigation on a frozen 2-4B general VLM (Qwen3-VL-2B, InternVL3.5-2B, Gemma 4 E2B) on chest X-ray yes/no existence questions; RULE/MMed-RAG/V-RAG/FactMM-RAG all fine-tune a 7B model, CCD wraps LLaVA-style radiology models, EVADE uses Qwen2.5-VL-7B, VGS-Decoding is the only 3-4B medical decoding result.
  * Every RAG-for-radiology paper reports gains without the controls the plan already uses for VCD: no yes-ratio-matched comparison, no mismatched-retrieval or label-shuffled-retrieval condition on VQA (only 'When Retrieval Hurts' on report generation and MC-CXR on diagnosis), and no retriever-alone (kNN label vote) baseline; adding these to a small-model study is a self-contained contribution.
  * Detector-as-assessor work (CCD, Expert-CFG, MedRAX, FactCheXcker, ChatCAD) never reports detector-alone accuracy next to the fused system or a shuffled-expert control, so it cannot say whether the VLM adds anything; the plan's constant-bias sweep from Rethinking-CD transfers directly to CCD's log-odds bias term.
  * Region-guided methods (ARCD, EVADE, VPE, HEAL-MedVQA) have no random-crop, wrong-region or random-box control, and none measures the cost in recall/coverage that 'Does Playing it Safe Count as Faithfulness?' demands.
  * Device (tube/line/catheter) existence is almost absent from VLM hallucination benchmarks (HalluCXR, MedHEval, ProbMed, MED-HALLUCINOGEN all use pathology findings), yet RANZCR CLiP supplies radiologist device labels with geometry on open NIH images; a device-existence TriPOPE is new and clinically meaningful (support devices are a CheXpert label class).
  * The matched real/edited/generated design has no CXR precedent on VQA: RadEdit was used to stress-test classifiers, not VLM question answering; RoentMod-style edits are credential-gated. A RadEdit- or LaMa-edited device-removal arm with a blind-text prior row would be the first measurement of 'trace vs prior' hallucination on radiographs.
  * Over-reliance rate (MMed-RAG: fraction of originally-correct answers flipped by retrieval), copy rate (When Retrieval Hurts) and context-induced flip rate (MC-CXR) have never been computed for a 2-4B model or on edited/generated inputs; they are cheap to add to Table 3.
  * Uncertainty signals in medical VQA have only been shown to 'track accuracy' (EMNLP 2026 Findings); whether the plan's hidden-state and attention signals keep their risk-coverage ordering across real, edited and generated X-rays is untested.

-- recommended_for_this_project --
  * Decision evidence: the X-ray re-fit is executable without credentialed data if the real arm is built on NIH ChestX-ray14 (attribution-only) + RANZCR CLiP device labels and polylines + ChestX-Det radiologist boxes (Apache-2.0) + CheXmask lung/heart masks (CC BY 4.0) + Open-i reports, with ProbMed's negation-pair question design; keep MIMIC-CXR, VinDr-CXR, Chest ImaGenome, MedVH, HalluCXR and MC-CXR as citations, not dependencies, unless the student already holds PhysioNet credentials on day one.
  * Object classes: use devices (ETT, NGT, CVC, Swan-Ganz from RANZCR CLiP) as the primary 'objects' and ChestX-Det findings (effusion, pneumothorax, nodule, mass, consolidation...) as the secondary class; ground truth comes from the radiologist labels, verification from ianpan/cxr-lines-and-tubes and torchxrayvision, and a human audit, never from the same detector used in a mitigation arm.
  * Arm 1, detector-gated answers (replicate CCD's expert-informed stage): add beta * clip(log(s/(1-s))) from torchxrayvision densenet121-res224-chex (findings) or the ianpan device classifier (devices) to the cached yes/no logits; cost is CPU-only given the Step-6 cache plus one 1060 pass of the detectors over all images. Controls: detector-alone row, shuffled-expert (bias taken from a different image), Rethinking-CD constant-bias sweep at matched yes-ratio, and selective-override (apply only when the expert is confident) reported with coverage. Nearest prior: CCD (Findings of ACL 2026), Expert-CFG, ChatCAD, MedRAX.
  * Arm 2, similar-case retrieval (training-free RAG): embed a held-out labelled pool (RANZCR/NIH images disjoint from the test items) with MedSigLIP or BiomedCLIP on the 1060, retrieve k nearest cases and insert their radiologist labels as text ('3 of 3 similar cases: ETT present'), optionally as interleaved images for Qwen3-VL/Gemma. Controls: kNN-label-vote alone, mismatched retrieval (cases chosen to have the opposite label, as in When Retrieval Hurts and MC-CXR), label-shuffled retrieval, k in {1,3,5}, and MMed-RAG's over-reliance rate; pod cost about one extra pass per condition, under $2 total. Nearest prior: RULE and MMed-RAG retrieval-only ablations, V-RAG entity probing, MedPromptX, CXR-RePaiR.
  * Arm 3, region-focused second pass with agree-or-abstain (replicate EVADE without native grounding): crop to the detector box or the CheXmask/PSPNet region relevant to the question, re-ask, commit only on agreement, else abstain; score with the plan's Effective Reliability and coverage. Controls: random crop of equal size, contralateral/wrong-region crop, full-image repeat (self-consistency) and, for drawn-box prompts, a box at a random location (VPE). Cost: at most one extra VLM pass per item plus one for the control. Nearest prior: EVADE, ARCD, VPE-in-Radiology, HEAL-MedVQA.
  * Arm 4, knowledge-grounded prompting (cheapest 'retrieval-based' arm): prepend a StatPearls/textbook snippet about the queried device or finding drawn from the MedRAG corpora, or CCD's 'Attention to:' expert prompt; control with an irrelevant snippet of equal length and the blind-text prior row. Expect null-to-negative on existence questions (RULE basic RAG, MICCAI 2026 attention-ratio evidence, expert evaluation of medical RAG) and state that prediction in the pre-registration.
  * Demote VCD/VDD to a reference row: MedHEval (0.499 -> 0.491), Med-VCD (75.79 -> 70.89) and VGS-Decoding (-5.93%) show plain VCD does not help on CXR VQA; keep the Mirage and Rethinking-CD controls because running them on medical images and below 7B is itself new; add VGS-Decoding's reweighting only if its code appears before Week 3.
  * Replace the SENTINEL-vs-base pair (COCO alignment is off-domain for X-rays) with MedGemma 1.5 4B-it vs its general Gemma 3 4B backbone, run in bf16 on the pod; keep CheXagent-2-3b as the CXR specialist row (custom script, trust_remote_code, bf16). Exclude NV-Reason-CXR-3B from NIH-based items because its card lists ChestX-ray14 in training; flag MedGemma/CheXagent for possible NIH exposure in the contamination appendix.
  * Edited and generated arms: build device-removal edits with LaMa on dilated RANZCR polylines (fast on the 1060) and, as the domain-faithful alternative, RadEdit mask-restricted edits (weights MSRLA, pipeline MIT, 512 px); use RadEdit whole-image re-synthesis from the Open-i or RANZCR label text as the generated twin instead of SD 1.5 + ControlNet, after a Day-3 VRAM test in fp16 on the 1060 with a pod fallback. Verify removals with the ianpan detector plus a human check, exactly as the plan does with OWLv2.
  * Cite as the positioning set: RULE, MMed-RAG, V-RAG, FactMM-RAG, CXR-RePaiR, When Retrieval Hurts, MC-CXR, MICCAI 2026 feature-level RAG, CCD, MedHEval, Med-VCD, VGS-Decoding, ARCD, EVADE, HalluCXR, 'Uncertainty Is Not a Safety Net', MedRAX, FactCheXcker, ChatCAD, Expert-CFG, VPE-in-Radiology, MedPromptX, HEAL-MedVQA, ProbMed, MED-HALLUCINOGEN (already in the digest), RadEdit, torchxrayvision, CheXmask.
  * Avoid: any arm that needs training (RULE/MMed-RAG preference tuning, V-RAG and FactMM-RAG retriever or VLM fine-tuning, HeteroRAG, CLARE, LobA); MAIRA-2 as a hero model (7B, MSRLA, pod-only); credentialed datasets as the core; SD 1.5 + ControlNet for X-ray generation; chain-of-thought as a mitigation arm (EVADE: accuracy 0.720 -> 0.554 on VQA-RAD).
  * Budget impact: detector, segmenter and embedding passes are free on the 1060; the four arms add roughly 3-4 extra VLM passes over the item set (about 4,500 items x 4 at 1-2 s on a 3090), i.e. an extra $2-5 of pod time, leaving the plan inside its $10-25 estimate and under the $40 ceiling.

-- cautions --
  * Web-search budget was exhausted near the end of the sweep (shared 200-call limit), so a few access terms could not be opened: RANZCR CLiP's Kaggle data rules (Kaggle pages return only a title), CANDID-PTX's figshare terms (search summary only), Chest ImaGenome (search only; credentialed), the NLM Open-i terms (only the HF mirror's CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 statement was read), CheXpert Plus registration terms, and PhysioNet credentialing turnaround time. Treat these as unverified and have the student check them on day one.
  * NIH ChestX-ray14 license wording differs by source: the Google Cloud public-dataset page says 'no usage restrictions' with attribution to NIH and the CVPR 2017 paper, while search results describe the Kaggle copy as CC0; either way it is usable, but cite the NIH Box site as required.
  * CheXagent-2-3b shows 'MIT' on its Hugging Face card while the GitHub repository LICENSE file is CC BY-NC-ND 4.0; for an academic report either is fine, but quote the repo license to be safe.
  * No paper titled 'RadRAG' was found; the retrieval-for-report-generation lineage is CXR-RePaiR (2021), FactMM-RAG, V-RAG and the 2026 'When Retrieval Hurts' critique. The 'MedRAG' name refers to the text-only ACL 2024 toolkit (and separately to a 2025 KG-RAG healthcare-copilot paper, arXiv 2502.04413, not opened); neither covers images.
  * Several key numbers come from WebFetch summaries of paper HTML rather than tables read directly: RULE's ablation (67.35 / 76.54 / 78.62), MMed-RAG's 55.08% / 43.31% / 8.38%, MedHEval's Table 4 row, Med-VCD's 70.89 / 82.95, VGS-Decoding's -5.93%, EVADE's ECE/coverage table, MC-CXR's flip ranges, HalluCXR's ensemble figures; re-read the tables before quoting them in the report.
  * Code is promised but not yet released for VGS-Decoding, EVADE, HalluCXR, When Retrieval Hurts, CoEV (arXiv 2606.18609) and the MICCAI 2026 feature-level RAG; Med-VCD and Expert-CFG have no code found. Only CCD, ARCD, MedHEval, RULE, MMed-RAG, MedRAX, MedPromptX, VPE and ProbMed have repositories, and CCD/ARCD support LLaVA- or Phi-style models only, so every arm must be reimplemented as a small hook on HF generate.
  * Contamination: NV-Reason-CXR-3B lists ChestX-ray14 in its training data; MedGemma and CheXagent (CheXinstruct) train on many public CXR sets whose exact lists were not confirmed; general 2B models are the cleaner hero models for NIH-based items. ReXVQA shows MedGemma already at 83% on presence questions, so easy pathology-presence items may be near ceiling for domain models; device and small-finding items are where differences will show.
  * HAI-DEF terms (MedGemma, MedSigLIP) and NVIDIA OneWay Non-Commercial and MSRLA (MAIRA-2, RadEdit) permit research use but are not open-source licenses; the released benchmark must not redistribute their outputs as training data and must state the terms.
  * RadEdit on a GTX 1060: the 0.4B U-Net at 512 px should fit in fp16 but was not tested, Pascal has no bf16 and runs fp16 at reduced rate; schedule a Day-3 timing test with the pod as fallback. LaMa removals of thin tubes may leave visible traces, which is exactly what the remover ablation in the plan measures.
  * PhysioNet credentialed data is licensed per person; an advisor's access cannot be shared with the student, and the DUA forbids redistribution, so any MIMIC-based items would also block the planned public release of per-item images.
  * Education-domain alternatives (diagram/textbook QA) were outside this angle and were not evaluated here.

-- confidence_notes -- All 18 items had at least one primary source opened on 2026-10-12 (arXiv abstract or HTML, ACL Anthology, MICCAI page, GitHub README or LICENSE, Hugging Face model/dataset card, PhysioNet page, PMLR page), and model ids, licenses, dataset sizes and the headline numbers quoted come from those pages. High confidence: RULE, MMed-RAG, When Retrieval Hurts, MC-CXR, CCD (paper, ACL entry, repo), MedHEval, ARCD, EVADE, VGS-Decoding, HalluCXR, torchxrayvision (README + Apache-2.0 LICENSE), ChestX-Det, CheXmask, ianpan lines-and-tubes card, CheXagent-2-3b card and repo LICENSE, MedGemma 1.5, NV-Reason-CXR-3B, MAIRA-2, MedSigLIP, BiomedCLIP, MedRAX, RadEdit, MIMIC-CXR-JPG and VinDr-CXR access pages, ProbMed, VQA-RAD, SLAKE, VinDr-CXR-VQA, ReXVQA. Medium confidence: numbers extracted from long HTML pages by the fetch summariser rather than read from tables (listed in cautions); FactMM-RAG, FactCheXcker, Expert-CFG, ChatCAD, MedPromptX, Uncertainty-Is-Not-a-Safety-Net, PROVE and CoEV were read at abstract level only; MedGemma's Gemma 3 backbone and MedSigLIP's instant gated approval come from search summaries. Low confidence / unverified: RANZCR CLiP and CANDID-PTX access terms, Chest ImaGenome details, NLM Open-i license, CheXpert Plus terms, PhysioNet processing time, RadEdit VRAM on a 6 GB Pascal card. Discovery coverage is good for 2024-2026 radiology RAG and decoding work but the search budget ran out before checks on knowledge-graph RAG for medical VQA beyond titles (mKG-RAG, MedRAG-KG, HeteroRAG, CLARE were seen as abstracts or search snippets only) and before a dedicated search on tube/line inpainting precedents.


######## ANGLE: edu_alternative ########
angle: Education-domain alternative: hallucination of open VLMs on educational visual QA (science diagrams, textbook figures, charts, geometry), assessed against the chest X-ray option for a 4-6-week solo undergraduate study on a GTX 1060, Kaggle free tier and under $40 of cloud.

[1] AI2D (Allen AI Diagrams) with AI2D-RST annotation layers (2016 (ECCV); AI2D-RST 2020; ECCV 2016; AI2D-RST in LREC/arXiv 2019-2020) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/1603.07396
    what: 4,817-4,903 grade-school science diagrams across 17 topics (life cycles, food webs, physiology, moon phases) with about 15k multiple-choice questions and dense JSON annotations: polygon coordinates for four element types (text, arrows, arrowheads, blobs) plus typed relationships (the AI2D-RST paper counts 118k constituents and 53k relations). AI2D-RST adds grouping, connectivity and discourse-structure graphs for a 1,000-diagram subset.
    why: The only education-domain diagram set with element-level geometry, so presence questions ('Is there a label X?', 'Is there an arrow from A to B?') have ground truth by construction and an edited arm can target one labelled polygon (inpaint a text block or arrow on a flat background, verify with OCR). Caveats: raster images of modest resolution (116-1.5k px on the HF copy), 2016-era content, no source code, so a generated twin is only feasible for graph-like diagrams by re-rendering the AI2D-RST connectivity graph (e.g., Graphviz).
    code/data: AWS Open Data registry entry states CC BY-SA 4.0 and anonymous S3 access (s3://ai2-public-datasets): https://registry.opendata.aws/allenai-diagrams/ ; AI2 project page with the 945 MB images+questions+annotations zip: https://prior.allenai.org/projects/diagram-understanding ; AI2D-RST (CC BY 4.0): https://github.com/thiippal/AI2D-RST ; QA-only copy without polygons (3,088 test rows): https://huggingface.co/datasets/lmms-lab/ai2d

[2] TQA / CK12-QA textbook corpus and 'Evaluating MLLMs on Educational Textbook Question Answering' (multimodal RAG study) (2017 (TQA, CVPR); 2025 (RAG study); CVPR 2017 (TQA); arXiv 2025, IEEE conference proceedings per the paper) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21596
    what: TQA: 1,076 CK-12 middle-school science lessons, 26,260 questions split into diagram (DQ_) and non-diagram (NDQ_) items, 6,229 images including letter-labelled 'ABC' diagrams and teaching images. The 2025 study builds a multimodal RAG pipeline (ImageBind retriever over lesson paragraphs and diagrams, KDB.AI vector store) for LLaVA-1.5 and LLaMA 3.2-Vision 11B on CK12-QA and reports 'catastrophic context interference': LLaMA 3.2-Vision diagram-question accuracy falls from 74.07% to 25.93% when three paragraphs and three diagrams are retrieved; after fine-tuning it recovers to 71.16%. No hallucination or abstention metrics.
    why: Supplies the education-domain retrieval corpus (lesson text plus diagrams) needed for the umbrella's 'retrieval-based mitigation' arm, and the single most relevant published hypothesis to test at 2-4B: retrieved textbook context can hurt diagram answers rather than ground them. Licence conflict to resolve before redistributing anything derived: the AI2 page says CC BY-NC 3.0, the AWS registry entry says CC BY-SA 4.0.
    code/data: TQA zip (1.6 GB): https://s3.amazonaws.com/ai2-vision-textbook-dataset/dataset_releases/tqa/tqa_train_val_test.zip ; AI2 page: https://prior.allenai.org/projects/tqa ; AWS registry: https://registry.opendata.aws/allenai-tqa/ ; the RAG paper gives no CK12-QA download link, licence or code (treat CK12-QA as not obtainable until located; use TQA)

[3] Education VQA sets without element annotations: ScienceQA, IconQA, MathVista, K12Vista (2021-2025; NeurIPS 2022 (ScienceQA); NeurIPS 2021 D&B (IconQA); ICLR 2024 (MathVista); arXiv 2025 (K12Vista)) verified=True
    url: https://github.com/lupantech/ScienceQA
    what: ScienceQA: 21,208 K-12 multiple-choice questions (a subset with image context), CC BY-NC-SA 4.0, no boxes. IconQA: 107,439 abstract-diagram questions in three sub-tasks plus 645,687 Icon645 icons, CC BY-NC-SA 4.0. MathVista: 6,140 items (testmini 1,000) aggregated from IconQA, TQA, AI2D, ScienceQA, GeoQA+, Geometry3K, FigureQA, DVQA, ChartQA, PlotQA and others, CC BY-SA 4.0, metadata only. K12Vista: 33k Chinese K-12 questions over five subjects with a process-evaluation model and K12-PEBench, CC BY 4.0.
    why: Useful as capability context and as sources of textbook-style images for a real arm, but none carries element-level labels, so presence ground truth would need manual annotation; the NC licences on ScienceQA and IconQA restrict redistribution of edited derivatives; MathVista testmini is small and widely trained on; K12Vista is Chinese-language.
    code/data: ScienceQA https://github.com/lupantech/ScienceQA (CC BY-NC-SA 4.0); IconQA https://github.com/lupantech/IconQA (CC BY-NC-SA 4.0); MathVista https://huggingface.co/datasets/AI4Math/MathVista (CC BY-SA 4.0); K12Vista https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01676 and https://github.com/lichongod/K12Vista (CC BY 4.0)

[4] ChartHal: fine-grained hallucination evaluation of LVLMs in chart understanding (2025; arXiv (HIT/iFLYTEK Joint Lab)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.17481
    what: 1,062 human-validated questions on CharXiv charts crossing three question types (descriptive, reasoning, open-ended) with four chart-question relations: irrelevant, inexistent (asks for information not in the chart), contradictory (false premise) and normal; the first three are unanswerable and the model must recognise this. Scored by GPT-4o-2024-11-20 as a binary judge. GPT-5 34.46%, o4-mini 22.79%; open models: Qwen2.5-VL-3B 8.57%, InternVL2.5-4B 12.71%, Qwen2.5-VL-7B 25.71%, Llama-3.2-11B-Vision 24.95%, Qwen2.5-VL-72B 54.24%.
    why: The closest existing benchmark to 'presence hallucination on educational/scientific figures with an abstention requirement': its 'inexistent' relation is the chart analogue of POPE negatives, and its small-model numbers show the problem is far from saturated below 7B. Gaps it leaves: open-ended answers judged by a proprietary LLM (no yes/no, no penalty scoring, no logit-level selective prediction), real arXiv charts only (no edited or generated twins), no 2026 2B models, no mitigation. Position the re-fitted plan against it explicitly.
    code/data: https://github.com/ymcui/ChartHal ; data https://huggingface.co/datasets/hfl/ChartHal (8.8 MB); licence not stated on the README or HF card as fetched

[5] CharXiv: realistic chart understanding with element-level descriptive questions and a 'Not Applicable' convention (2024; NeurIPS 2024 Datasets and Benchmarks) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.18521
    what: 2,323 charts from arXiv papers with descriptive questions about chart elements (title, axis labels, legend presence and content, number of subplots, tick labels) that are answered 'Not Applicable' when the element is absent, plus reasoning questions. Annotations CC BY-SA 4.0, code Apache-2.0, charts remain under their authors' copyright. Leaderboard includes 4B-9B open models (Phi-3 Vision 4B 31.6% reasoning, MiniCPM-V2.6 8B 31.0%); humans 80.5%.
    why: A ready-made real arm whose element presence/absence is already human-labelled (legend, subplots, axis labels), i.e., verified absent-element negatives without new annotation, plus a published precedent for an explicit 'not applicable' answer option. Release only chart ids and scripts because of copyright. It is scientific rather than K-12 material, so the education framing should describe it as chart reading in science education or pair it with textbook-derived charts.
    code/data: https://github.com/princeton-nlp/CharXiv ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/princeton-nlp/CharXiv

[6] Paired yes/no chart QA precedents: ChartBench (Acc+), FigureQA, DVQA (2017-2024; arXiv 2023/2024 (ChartBench); ICLR 2018 workshop (FigureQA); CVPR 2018 (DVQA)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.15915
    what: ChartBench: 2,100 test charts / 18,900 QA pairs (66.6k charts, 600k QA overall) rendered with matplotlib and online plotting sites from desensitised Kaggle data and GPT-generated data, many without data labels; Acc+ counts a chart as understood only if a true assertion and its paired false twin are both answered correctly; 18 open and 3 proprietary models, best open Acc+ 54.52% (InternLM-XComposer2 8B), GPT-4o 60.02%. FigureQA: over 100,000 Bokeh-rendered line, dot-line, bar and pie figures with over one million templated yes/no questions (15 templates) and bounding boxes for all plot elements plus source data. DVQA: synthetic bar-chart QA on structure, data retrieval and reasoning.
    why: Establishes the POPE-style paired yes/no protocol for charts (Acc+ is the chart analogue of adversarial negatives) and shows that synthetic charts with element boxes are a solved rendering problem; FigureQA's generator is a drop-in way to produce a generated arm with perfect labels. None scores abstention, none pairs real/edited/generated items, and the evaluated models are pre-2025.
    code/data: ChartBench https://github.com/IDEA-FinAI/ChartBench (MIT badge on repo); FigureQA https://github.com/Maluuba/FigureQA (generation code, LICENSE.txt in repo) and https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.07300 ; DVQA https://arxiv.org/abs/1801.08163

[7] Code-paired charts and diagrams for programmatic editing: ChartX, ChartGen, Diagram-MMU (2024-2026; arXiv 2024 (ChartX/ChartVLM); arXiv 2025 (ChartGen); arXiv 2026 (Diagram-MMU)) verified=True
    url: https://github.com/Alpha-Innovator/ChartVLM
    what: ChartX: 48k charts over 18 types, 22 topics and 7 tasks, each shipped as image + CSV + Python plotting code + description, CC BY 4.0 (val 4,848 / test 1,152). ChartGen: 222.5k chart-image/code pairs across 27 chart types and 11 plotting libraries, reconstructed from 13k seed images by a VLM and augmented by a code LLM, pipeline and data released under CC BY 4.0, six open 3B-26B models evaluated on chart-to-code. Diagram-MMU: 3.7k scientific diagrams / 18.3k questions with diagram-to-TikZ parsing, editing and QA tasks; 12 MLLMs; models reason well but parse and edit poorly.
    why: Because every chart has executable code, an edited arm (delete one series, legend, label or bar in code and re-render at identical pixel size) and a generated arm (re-render the same CSV in another style or library) cost CPU seconds and carry ground truth by construction, removing the LaMa-trace and label-verification burden of the COCO plan. Limitation: ChartX/ChartGen images are themselves synthetic, so they serve as E/S sources or as a fully redistributable sub-arm, not as the 'real' arm.
    code/data: ChartX https://github.com/Alpha-Innovator/ChartVLM (HF and Google Drive, CC BY 4.0); ChartGen https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.19492 (CC BY 4.0); Diagram-MMU https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12262 (paper CC BY 4.0; project page linked from abstract)

[8] Chartographer: counterfactual chart generation for evaluating VLMs (2026; EMNLP 2026 main (per abstract)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27311
    what: Reverse-engineers ChartQA, CharXiv and ChartMuseum charts into executable code, validates reconstruction fidelity, then perturbs the underlying data with a seed-controlled generator to produce 10 counterfactual variants per chart (440 chart-question families, 4,400 charts) with answers derived from executable QA logic. Open models evaluated: Gemma 4 E4B, InternVL3-8B, LLaVA-OneVision-7B, Pixtral-12B, Qwen2.5-VL-7B, Qwen3-VL-8B; relative accuracy on variants drops about 14.5% (open) to 20.6% (proprietary) on ChartMuseum; models that answer the original correctly often fail the variant.
    why: The direct 2026 precedent for 'real chart -> code twin -> controlled variant', including a fidelity-validation step that can be reused for the generated arm. It varies data values rather than element presence, uses no 2B models, no abstention scoring and no mitigation, which is exactly the space left for a presence-focused, penalty-scored three-source chart benchmark.
    code/data: https://github.com/compling-wat/Chartographer (code, data-generation modules, prompt templates); paper CC BY 4.0

[9] HallusionBench: human-edited charts, tables and maps with paired yes/no questions (2023/2024; CVPR 2024) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.14566
    what: 346 images / 1,129 expert-written yes/no questions in Visual Dependent (easy = original web images, hard = human-edited counterfactual versions) and Visual Supplement categories covering illusion, math, OCR, video, chart, table, map and figure; metrics are question-pair accuracy and figure accuracy (all questions on one figure correct); 15 models, GPT-4V best at 31.42% pair accuracy. Repository under BSD-3-Clause.
    why: The canonical hallucination benchmark that already uses edited charts and tables with paired questions, so it is the edited-arm precedent reviewers will cite; its edits are manual and few, unpaired with any generated twin, and scored without abstention or penalty. It is also the chart-heavy benchmark already on the existing plan's list, which eases the domain shift.
    code/data: https://github.com/tianyi-lab/HallusionBench (BSD-3-Clause); note the README excerpt fetched lists 69 images / 254 questions, which differs from the paper's 346 / 1,129, so confirm which release you use

[10] Perturbed diagram and chart robustness studies: SciDiagram-Robust, CHART NOISe, RobustCQA (2024-2025; arXiv 2025 (Robust Diagram Reasoning; Losing the Plot); arXiv 2024 (Unraveling the Truth)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16972
    what: Robust Diagram Reasoning builds SciDiagram-Robust with programmatically generated noise, blur, occlusion and rotation versions of scientific diagram questions (GPT-4V clean 85.2% vs perturbation-robustness score 72.1%) and a multi-view consistency self-correction loop. 'Losing the Plot' releases CHART NOISe (corruptions, occlusions, CSAT-style multiple choice) and reports value fabrication, overconfidence and 'prompt reverse inconsistency' (confirm vs deny) in GPT-4o, Claude Sonnet 4 and Gemini 2.5 Pro. 'Unraveling the Truth' introduces RobustCQA with 75 perturbation types including legend and axis modifications and finds large consistency gaps across chart and question types.
    why: These are the nearest neighbours to an edited-diagram arm and must be cited; all perturb appearance or occlude, none removes a labelled element with verified absence, none pairs with a generated twin, and none evaluates 2-4B open models under abstention scoring, which keeps the proposed E arm novel. The confirm/deny inconsistency finding motivates keeping the negated-question template from the current plan.
    code/data: https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16972 (release not stated in abstract); https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.18425 (CHART NOISe stated as released); https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.11229 (paper CC BY 4.0; dataset release not stated in abstract)

[11] Procedurally generated geometry diagrams with existence questions: GePBench and MATHGLANCE/GeoPeP (2024-2026 (GePBench); 2025 (MATHGLANCE, also titled Math Blind); arXiv (GePBench); arXiv 2025 (MATHGLANCE)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.21036
    what: GePBench renders sampled shape descriptions with matplotlib (15 shape types, 11 relations, xkcd/gradient line styles, Gaussian and Perlin noise) into 80k images / 285k four-option questions over six dimensions: existence, counting, location, size, reference, relationship; best model (Qwen3.5-397B-A17B) 79.2% vs human 99.3%, with size questions at 29.0-54.9%. MATHGLANCE: 1,198 matplotlib-rendered geometry images / 1,609 questions (shape classification MC; counting and relation as true/false or MC; grounding as boxes); Qwen2.5-VL-7B 44.0% on plane-geometry shape classification, near-zero grounding; training on the 200k-pair GeoPeP set lifts a 7B baseline from 35.4% to 84.6%.
    why: Shows that a fully synthetic diagram arm with exact labels and an explicit 'existence' dimension is cheap (CPU rendering on any machine) and far from saturated, and provides a ready renderer for a generated arm. Limits: geometry is mathematics rather than science diagrams; forced-choice only, no abstention, no real or edited counterparts; model lists are mostly 7B+.
    code/data: https://github.com/Changhao-Xiang/GePBench (code and data; licence not stated); MATHGLANCE https://arxiv.org/html/2503.20745v1 (project page mathglance.github.io returned 404 when fetched; release unverified)

[12] Behavioural evaluations on scientific and textbook figures: SciFigBench ('How Do VLMs Behave When Blind or Misled?') and DISSECT (2026; arXiv 2026 (both)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13267
    what: SciFigBench: 250 bar/line/pie figures from 187 arXiv papers (600+ annotation hours) with selective blur of OCR-detected text regions, noise/contrast/rotation transforms, misleading captions and non-existent-element probes (34k setups) scored under an Admittance-Resistance-Inductance framework; GPT-5.2 hallucinates unreadable content in 96% of cases while Gemini 3.1 Pro acknowledges uncertainty in 71%; open models Qwen3-VL-8B/30B/235B and Gemma-3-27B. DISSECT: 12,000 grade 9-12 chemistry and biology questions from Indian textbooks, exams and question banks (Extramarks) under five input modes (vision+text, text-only, vision-only, human oracle, model oracle) across 18 VLMs including InternVL3-1B/2B/4B, Qwen2.5-VL-3B and LLaVA-OneVision-0.5B; defines a Language Prior Gap and finds open models reason better from their own image descriptions than from the image (integration bottleneck).
    why: The 2026 state of the art on 'does the model admit it cannot see' and 'language prior vs vision' for educational and scientific figures, and the natural related-work anchors for RQ1 (blind/text-only baselines) and RQ2 (abstention). Both state data will be released only upon publication or acceptance, so neither can be reused now, and neither uses penalty scoring, edited/generated twins, or 2B models with abstention.
    code/data: SciFigBench: https://scifigbench.nlp4sci.com/ ('released upon publication', CC BY 4.0); DISSECT: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.06250 ('upon acceptance', CC BY-SA 4.0)

[13] Retrieval and grounding mitigation precedents: VLM-DeflectionBench, ChartLens, ChartCap, ChartCheck (2024-2026; ACL 2026 (DeflectionBench); ACL 2025 (ChartLens); ICCV 2025 highlight (ChartCap); ACL 2024 Findings (ChartCheck)) verified=True
    url: https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.1307/
    what: VLM-DeflectionBench (Amazon): 2,775 knowledge-based VQA samples under four retrieval scenarios that disentangle memorisation from retrieval robustness and score whether 20 LVLMs deflect ('Sorry, I cannot answer') under noisy or conflicting evidence; models usually fail to deflect; CC BY-NC-SA 4.0, resources on publication. ChartLens (Adobe): post-hoc visual attribution that segments chart elements and uses set-of-marks prompting to cite the elements supporting an answer, +26-66% on ChartVA-Eval (real and synthetic charts). ChartCap: 565k dense chart captions generated only from discernible data with cycle-consistency human verification and a reference-free Visual Consistency Score. ChartCheck: 1.7k real charts with 10.5k human claims and explanations for chart fact-checking.
    why: These define the mitigation family the umbrella asks for, in an education-adjacent setting: (i) retrieval with explicit deflection scoring, (ii) element-grounded answering, (iii) OCR/data-grounded verification of claims. None is run on 2-4B models for presence questions with yes-bias controls, so a small-model comparison of OCR/table grounding against VCD under Mirage and Rethinking-CD controls is open.
    code/data: DeflectionBench https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.12033 ; ChartLens https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19360 (release not stated in abstract) ; ChartCap https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.03164 ; ChartCheck https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.07453 (seen via search result only, not opened)

[14] Chest X-ray comparison evidence: HalluCXR, CXR-ContraBench, MC-CXR, VinDr-CXR, RoentGen/RoentMod (2025-2026; arXiv 2026 (HalluCXR, CXR-ContraBench); EMNLP 2026 Findings (MC-CXR); PhysioNet (VinDr-CXR); arXiv 2025 / npj Digital Medicine (RoentMod)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20469
    what: HalluCXR (May 2026): 856 MIMIC-CXR-JPG radiographs, 15,408 evaluations over open report, targeted yes/no ('Is there evidence of X?') and differential queries; eight hallucination categories validated on 250 human annotations; 61.9-82.3% of outputs hallucinate across InternVL2, LLaVA-NeXT-Mistral 7B, Qwen2-VL 7B, BiomedCLIP, GPT-5.1 and Gemini 3 Flash; mitigation is ensembles and length-based risk, no retrieval or grounding; data under PhysioNet credentialing, code 'upon acceptance'. CXR-ContraBench (2026): MedGemma and Qwen2.5-VL select negated options on over 62% of presence questions (about 30% presence accuracy), fixed at inference by a polarity verifier. MC-CXR: 240 cases / 2,522 instances on PhysioNet showing misleading text flips image-only decisions. VinDr-CXR: 18,000 PA films, 22 local findings with radiologist boxes, PhysioNet Credentialed Health Data License 1.5.0 plus CITI training. RoentGen weights are provided only to MIMIC-CXR-credentialed researchers via a request form; RoentMod builds on it for synthetic pathology insertion (93% rated realistic, 89-99% correct finding).
    why: This is what the healthcare option competes with and depends on: presence-question hallucination on CXR is already a crowded 2026 topic using MedGemma and Qwen2.5-VL, every validated image set and the only validated CXR editor sit behind PhysioNet credentialing, and label validity for findings, tubes and lines needs a radiologist the student does not have. Un-credentialed CXR sets with device annotations (RANZCR CLiP on Kaggle, Object-CXR) exist but their current terms could not be opened this session.
    code/data: HalluCXR https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20469 ; CXR-ContraBench https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05810 (code on GitHub per abstract) ; MC-CXR https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24118 (PhysioNet) ; VinDr-CXR https://www.physionet.org/content/vindr-cxr/ (credentialed; details from search summary, page not opened) ; RoentGen https://github.com/StanfordMIMI/RoentGen (credentialed request form) ; RoentMod https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08640

-- gaps_and_opportunities --
  * No benchmark pairs a real educational chart or diagram with an element-removed edit and a layout-matched re-render under identical presence questions: ChartHal covers real charts only, Chartographer varies data in code twins, HallusionBench edits by hand, and the perturbation studies (SciDiagram-Robust, CHART NOISe, RobustCQA) blur, occlude or restyle without verified absence.
  * Abstention on figures is scored only by LLM judges on open answers (ChartHal, SciFigBench) or as deflection on knowledge-based VQA (DeflectionBench); nobody has run Kalai et al.'s penalty instruction or Effective Reliability on element-presence questions, nor compared verbal abstention with logit/hidden-state selective prediction on charts or diagrams, and CharXiv's 'Not Applicable' convention gives a validated precedent for the unpenalised option.
  * Flat backgrounds make element removal in charts and diagrams nearly trace-free (fill the element's box or delete it in code, then verify with OCR and a bounding-box-limited pixel diff), so the COCO plan's three-remover 'traces vs prior' ablation collapses into a cleaner prior-only measurement; the language prior for chart elements (legends, axis labels, titles) is strong and directly measurable with a text-only run.
  * Retrieval harm is documented but untested at small scale: the CK12-QA RAG study found catastrophic context interference at 11B; nothing tests it at 2-4B, for presence questions, or with a DeflectionBench-style contradictory-evidence condition, which is cheap to build here because tables, code and lesson text are ground truth.
  * OCR grounding as mitigation is unexplored at small scale: injecting the OCR'd text strings (EasyOCR as in SciFigBench, or PaddleOCR) or the chart's data table into the prompt, in the spirit of ChartLens/ChartCap evidence grounding, versus VCD under Mirage output-bias and Rethinking-CD constant-bias controls, has not been run on 2-4B models for presence questions.
  * Text-to-diagram generation remains unreliable for raster science diagrams (DiagrammerGPT/AI2D-Caption https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.12128 ; DiagramAgent/DiagramGenBenchmark CVPR 2025 https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.11916), so the practical generated arm is code rendering (matplotlib, Altair/Vega-Lite as in EncQA https://github.com/apple/ml-encqa, Graphviz/TikZ); EncQA additionally reports that 2B-26B performance does not improve with size for many encoding-task pairs, which is a favourable sign for small-model studies.
  * Charts are text-dense, so dynamic-resolution tiling in Qwen3-VL/InternVL3.5 decides legibility; no chart hallucination benchmark logs visual-token counts, so the token-matched comparison from the existing plan transfers unchanged and is still novel.
  * No English education-specific VLM hallucination benchmark exists by name (no 'EduVQA'-style set surfaced); K12Vista (Chinese) and DISSECT (unreleased) are the closest, so an English, element-level, released benchmark on educational figures would be a first.

-- recommended_for_this_project --
  * Decision: choose the education domain (charts and science diagrams) over chest X-rays for this term, unless by Oct 19 the student already holds PhysioNet credentialing with CITI training, a named radiologist rater, and RoentGen access. Comparison on the eight requested dimensions: data access speed - education wins (AI2D CC BY-SA 4.0 via anonymous S3, CharXiv/ChartX/ChartGen/FigureQA open today) versus credentialed VinDr-CXR/MIMIC-CXR and a request-form RoentGen; label validity without experts - education wins (element polygons, chart source code and CharXiv descriptive labels give ground truth by construction and any literate rater or OCR can audit) versus findings, tubes and lines that need a radiologist; synthetic/edit tooling maturity - education wins for charts and geometry (code rendering, Chartographer, FigureQA/GePBench generators) and is adequate for raster diagrams (LaMa on flat backgrounds), versus gated RoentGen/RoentMod and unvalidated SD inpainting on radiographs; small open models - tie (the same 2-4B generalists read charts, e.g., Qwen2-VL-2B ChartQA 73.5 / MathVista 43.0; CXR has CheXagent-2-3b under CC BY-NC-4.0 and MedGemma 4B under HAI-DEF terms, verified only via search); mitigation literature - both thin on retrieval/grounding at small scale, education has ChartLens/ChartCap/DeflectionBench and the CK12-QA retrieval-harm finding to build on; novelty - education higher (no three-source presence benchmark with abstention exists; CXR presence hallucination with MedGemma/Qwen2.5-VL already has HalluCXR, CXR-ContraBench and MC-CXR in 2026); stakes for Responsible AI - CXR higher, education real and explicitly named in the umbrella ('e.g., healthcare or education'); risk - CXR carries credentialing delay against a Nov 8 checkpoint, no expert rater and gated editing tools; education carries a ceiling risk on clean real charts that the removed/absent negatives and penalty prompts are designed to address.
  * Core design, 'TriChart' (keep the TriPOPE skeleton): arm R = real charts from CharXiv's descriptive subset (legend, subplot count, axis labels already labelled, 'Not Applicable' negatives) and/or ChartQA (Pew/Statista; check its data-table coverage), plus ChartX as a fully redistributable sub-arm; arm E = exactly one element removed (legend, one named series, one text label or one bar) by flat fill on raster charts or by deletion in code where code exists, verified by OCR (the string must be gone) and a pixel diff confined to the element's box; arm S = a twin re-rendered from the ground-truth data (ChartX/ChartGen code, ChartQA tables, or Chartographer-style reverse-engineered code with its fidelity check) at the real chart's exact pixel size; six yes/no presence questions per item (title, legend, x-axis label, named series, named text label, named category) with adversarial negatives absent by construction (ChartHal 'inexistent' analogue) and the removed element; prompts P0/P1/P2 with Kalai's verbatim penalty text, Effective Reliability, a text-only blind run and a negated template; the same cluster-aware statistics, OSF pre-registration and token-count logging. Rendering is CPU-only, so the GTX 1060 is needed only for the AI2D extension.
  * Mitigation family (the umbrella's 'grounding and retrieval-based techniques'): (a) OCR grounding - append the OCR'd text strings (and, where available, the data table) to the prompt as evidence; (b) retrieval - retrieve the chart's caption/table or the matching TQA lesson paragraph and diagram, replicating the CK12-QA 'context interference' test at 2-4B; (c) a conflicting-evidence condition (retrieved table that contradicts the image) scored for deflection as in DeflectionBench; keep VCD plus the Mirage output-layer bias and Rethinking-CD constant-bias controls as the training-free baseline so the yes-bias critique is answered for grounding methods too; SENTINEL stays only if its checkpoint still applies to Qwen2-VL-2B on charts (gate it).
  * AI2D science diagrams as the gated education extension rather than the core: arm R from AI2D, arm E by LaMa-inpainting one annotated text block or arrow polygon on the 1060 with OCR verification, questions from the annotation JSON ('Is there a label X?', 'Is there an arrow from A to B?'), text-only blind run for the prior; skip arm S except for graph-like diagrams (food webs, cycles) re-rendered from AI2D-RST connectivity graphs with Graphviz; kill the extension if Week 3 slips. If the advisor reads 'education' strictly as K-12 material, promote this arm and describe the chart arm as charts in science education.
  * Models and expectations unchanged (Qwen3-VL-2B, InternVL3.5-2B, Gemma 4 E2B, Qwen2-VL-2B); the bf16-pod decision carries over. Because 2B models already score about 73.5 on ChartQA value reading (Qwen2-VL-2B card), the Day-1 pilot must confirm that absent-element negatives and the penalty prompts produce discordance before N is fixed; if clean-chart presence accuracy is above about 95% with near-zero abstention, that itself is a finding, and the ChartHal 'inexistent' style questions (asking about information not in the chart) become the primary stressor.
  * Cite and position against: ChartHal (nearest benchmark; its LLM-judge scoring and lack of edits are the gap), Chartographer (code twins, data-varied), HallusionBench (manual edits), CharXiv (element labels and 'Not Applicable'), ChartBench Acc+ and FigureQA/DVQA (paired yes/no and synthetic precedents), GePBench/MATHGLANCE (procedural existence questions), SciFigBench and DISSECT (2026 blind and language-prior analyses), DeflectionBench, the CK12-QA RAG study, ChartLens/ChartCap/ChartCheck (grounding and verification). Use as model-capability context only: ScienceQA, IconQA, MathVista, K12Vista. Avoid: LLM-as-judge as a primary metric, NC-licensed ScienceQA/IconQA images in redistributed edits, MathVista testmini as a base set, and any claim that text-to-diagram generation produces a valid generated arm.
  * Licensing and release: redistribute the ChartX-derived arms in full (CC BY 4.0); for CharXiv and ChartQA release ids plus edit/re-render scripts only (charts copyrighted; ChartQA on HF is tagged GPL-3.0); AI2D derivatives are allowed under CC BY-SA 4.0 share-alike (AWS registry statement; AI2's own page shows no licence, so cite the registry); resolve TQA's conflicting licence statements (CC BY-NC 3.0 vs CC BY-SA 4.0) before releasing lesson text.
  * Budget and schedule effect: arms E and S for charts need no GPU, so expected spend falls to roughly $8-20 (pod inference only); the Week 1-2 image-generation nights on the 1060 are freed for the AI2D extension or dropped; everything else in the week-by-week plan, the Nov 8 report v1 checkpoint and the validity appendix carries over with 'object' read as 'chart or diagram element'.

-- cautions --
  * The shared web-search budget ran out at my last two queries, so RANZCR CLiP and Object-CXR access terms, the VinDr-CXR PhysioNet page, and ChartCheck's ACL page were not opened directly; VinDr-CXR and CheXagent/MedGemma licence statements come from search summaries only.
  * Licence statements conflict or are missing: TQA is CC BY-NC 3.0 on AI2's page but CC BY-SA 4.0 on the AWS Open Data registry; AI2D is CC BY-SA 4.0 per the AWS registry while AI2's download page shows no licence and hyper.ai lists 'Other'; ChartHal's licence is not stated on its README or HF card; GePBench's repo shows no licence; ChartQA's HF card is tagged GPL-3.0 and its Pew/Statista charts carry their own copyrights; CharXiv charts remain copyrighted by paper authors.
  * Several 2026 resources are not yet usable: SciFigBench ('upon publication'), DISSECT ('upon acceptance'), DeflectionBench ('upon publication'), HalluCXR code ('upon acceptance'); MATHGLANCE's project page returned 404, so its data release is unverified.
  * HallusionBench counts differ between the paper (346 images / 1,129 questions) and the README excerpt fetched (69 images / 254 questions); confirm the release before quoting numbers.
  * CK12-QA's download location and licence were not found; the RAG study does not state LLaVA-1.5's size (presumably 7B, unverified) and reports no hallucination or abstention metrics.
  * Qwen3-VL-2B and InternVL3.5-2B chart/diagram scores are published only as images on their cards and README, so the 2B numbers quoted (ChartQA 73.5, MathVista 43.0) are from the older Qwen2-VL-2B card; CharXiv's small-model entries are 4B-9B, not 2B.
  * ChartHal's small-model scores come from GPT-4o-judged open answers and are not comparable to yes/no accuracy; do not use them as a baseline for TriChart numbers.
  * Domain framing: CharXiv, ChartHal and SciFigBench charts come from arXiv papers, not K-12 textbooks; if the advisor reads 'education' strictly, use AI2D/TQA images or textbook-style re-renders and say so in the report, and note K12Vista is Chinese-language.
  * Ceiling risk: presence questions on clean real charts may be near ceiling for 2026 models; the design depends on absent-by-construction negatives, removed elements and penalty prompts producing discordance, which the Day-1 pilot must confirm before N and power are claimed.
  * The chest X-ray comparison relies on the abstracts of HalluCXR, CXR-ContraBench and MC-CXR; model sizes in HalluCXR are given loosely in the paper (e.g., 'InternVL2 6B'), and no open CXR line/tube dataset was verified as usable without credentialing.

-- confidence_notes -- All 14 items were checked against at least one primary page (arXiv abs/HTML, GitHub README, Hugging Face card, AI2 or AWS registry page) on 2026-10-12; quoted counts, scores, model lists and licence strings are taken verbatim from those pages. Sub-items flagged in the text (ChartCheck, VinDr-CXR page, CheXagent/MedGemma terms, RANZCR/Object-CXR) rest on search summaries only. The healthcare-vs-education comparison is my synthesis of the verified facts, and the recommendation assumes the student has neither PhysioNet credentialing nor a radiologist rater today; if either exists, re-weigh the stakes dimension.


######## ANGLE: fit_and_ethics ########
angle: Fit with the advisor's program (Dr. Muzammil Behzad / JRCAI), reviewer expectations for workshop-scale medical VLM hallucination papers, data-use ethics and compliance for public chest X-ray data at a Saudi university, and 2026-2027 venues (Parts A-D).

[1] Hallucination in Medical Imaging AI: A Cross-Modality Analytical Framework for Taxonomy, Detection, and Mitigation under Regulatory Constraints (Alshahrani & Behzad) - the advisor's own paper on the umbrella topic (2026; arXiv preprint cs.AI, 11 Jun 2026, CC-BY 4.0; authors at KFUPM ICS and SDAIA-KFUPM JRCAI) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13211
    what: Structured narrative synthesis across CT, MRI, PET/SPECT, ultrasound, pathology and chest radiography. Unifies three taxonomies (Brooks & Anastasio data-driven vs confabulation; DREAM factual vs faithfulness; MediHall five severity tiers), reviews detection (uncertainty, attention, reconstruction/sFRC, cross-modal verification, benchmarks) and mitigation (physics-informed constraints, chain-of-thought, retrieval-augmented generation, human-in-the-loop), mapped to FDA TPLC/PCCP. Literature-derived headline: general-purpose foundation models beat medical-specialized ones on hallucination benchmarks (median hallucination-free rate 76.6% vs 51.3%, p=0.012); names MIMIC-CXR, Med-HallMark, CXR-VisHal, MedHallBench, POPE, MMHal-Bench. Open problems listed: a unified cross-modality taxonomy, 'direct comparative evaluations across reconstruction and vision-language tasks', and 'FDA-compliant retrieval-augmented and reasoning strategies'. Purely analytical, no experiments or code.
    why: This is the advisor's current position on exactly the umbrella question, four months old. The student's component should be framed as its first controlled empirical instantiation on chest X-rays: test claim 2 (general vs medical-specialized) with 2-4B open models plus MedGemma-4B under matched image interventions, and test claim 3 (RAG/grounding compatibility) with training-free retrieval and grounding arms. Cite it first, adopt its taxonomy vocabulary (MediHall severity tiers) and its 'lifecycle not checklist' framing in the discussion.
    code/data: none (analytical paper, no code or data)

[2] Dr. Muzammil Behzad: verified publication record, lab direction and active projects (KFUPM Pure, arXiv author listing, Semantic Scholar) (2023-2026; KFUPM Pure profile; arXiv API listing (29 preprints); Semantic Scholar (39 papers, 183 citations, h-index 6); PhD University of Oulu (3D/4D facial affect; 4DME, IEEE TAFFC 2023)) verified=True
    url: https://pure.kfupm.edu.sa/en/persons/muzammil-behzad/
    what: Assistant Professor, ICS, and Research Scholar at JRCAI; interests 'AI for Healthcare, LLMs/VLMs, Affective Computing'. 2025-2026 output is dominated by medical VLM work with (apparently student) co-authors: MedCT-VLM LoRA on CT-CLIP (arXiv 2512.00597, 18 thoracic pathologies, AUROC 61.3 to 68.9), XAI-CLIP ROI-guided perturbation explainability for segmentation (2602.07017), PULSE cardiac multi-task (IEEE JBHI), SwinTF3D text-guided 3D segmentation (2512.22878), MOSAIC (CVIU), a knowledge-distillation-in-medical-imaging survey (Information Fusion 2026), plus VLM papers (FACET-VLM, Neurocomputing; MultiviewVLM, IEEE TMM 2026; The Professor, 2606.23897). Active project 'Multimodal Vision-Language Models for Trustworthy, Clinically-Deployable and Anatomically-Aware Medical Image Segmentation' (May 2026-May 2029). No education-domain work, no chest X-ray datasets or models, and no public page for the 'Evaluating VLMs/LLMs Hallucination in Domain-Specific Tasks' project were found. Local related work: Alansari & Luqman (KFUPM), 'Large Language Models Hallucination: A Comprehensive Survey', arXiv 2510.06265.

Cite XAI-CLIP as the lab's own precedent for region-level interventions and the KFUPM LLM-hallucination survey as local related work. There are no lab datasets/models for CXR to reuse, so the open data path below is the practical route.

[3] Vision-language models for chest radiography do not always need the image (Lotfinia et al., FAU Erlangen / TUM / RWTH Aachen) (2026; arXiv preprint (v1 16 Jun 2026, v3 1 Oct 2026); not yet peer-reviewed) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17710
    what: Nine systems (Gemma-4-26B, Qwen3-VL-32B, Mistral-Small-4-119B, GPT-5, MedGemma-1.5-4B, LLaVA-Med-7B, two text-only baselines, a RAD-DINO linear probe) on about 2,550 yes/no finding questions from MIMIC-CXR (CheXpert-labeler labels), a 1,380-case CheXpert cohort and MS-CXR radiologist boxes. Interventions: image swap, target-box occlusion, equal-size irrelevant occlusion, noise/photos. Metrics: Causal Grounding Rate (correct answers that flip when the target region is masked), Unrelated-Image Answer Rate, Irrelevant-Mask Stability. Text-only MedGemma-27B reached 60.1% vs best multimodal 66.2%; three systems had CGR 0.0; two radiologists read an 80-case subset (81.3%, CGR 23.1). Inference on RTX PRO 6000; full code, prompts and masks public.
    why: Direct precedent for the plan's blind/text-only arm and for occlusion-based attribution on CXR, so it must be cited and positioned against: the student's distinct contributions are anatomically plausible inpainting removal instead of box occlusion, layout-matched generated twins, abstention-aware scoring, sub-4B open models, and mitigation. Adopt CGR/UAR/IS as comparability metrics (CGR analogue = correct 'yes' answers that flip after the finding is removed).
    code/data: https://github.com/mahshadlotfinia/causal (code, prompts, masks, scripts); MIMIC-CXR and MS-CXR are PhysioNet-credentialed

[4] HalluCXR (Wang & Li, King's College London) and CXR-ContraBench (Fang et al.): the two 2026 chest-X-ray hallucination benchmarks (2026; arXiv preprints, May 2026 (HalluCXR 2605.20469; CXR-ContraBench 2605.05810); neither peer-reviewed yet) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20469
    what: HalluCXR: 856 MIMIC-CXR frontal images from 220 patients, stratified by CheXpert-label complexity (normal 153 / single 212 / 2-3 findings 241 / 4+ 250); three query types (open report, targeted 'Is there evidence of X? YES/NO' VQA, differential); six systems (BiomedCLIP, Gemini 3 Flash, GPT-5.1, InternVL2-6B, LLaVA-NeXT-Mistral-7B, Qwen2-VL-7B) giving 15,408 evaluations; Gemini LLM-judge cross-checked by GPT-5.1 on 2,808 records and by 250 human annotations from two co-authors with clinical-informatics training (judge F1 0.907); eight-category taxonomy (fabrication, omission, spatial, severity, reasoning, template overfitting, self-contradiction, detail fabrication); hallucination rates 61.9-82.3%; local models in 4-bit on one A100; code/data 'upon acceptance'. CXR-ContraBench: MedGemma and Qwen2.5-VL pick negated options ('No consolidation') against visual evidence on ReXVQA/OpenI/CheXpert protocols; code public.
    why: Shows the credible unit of a two-author CXR study (about 850 images, six models, 250 human checks, one GPU) and supplies a taxonomy for any free-text categorization. ContraBench's negated-option attraction is exactly the plan's PN (negated-premise) prompt, so keep PN and cite it as a known failure mode; both papers used MIMIC-CXR, so an NIH/ChestX-Det test bed is also a contamination-reducing differentiator.
    code/data: HalluCXR: none released yet; CXR-ContraBench: https://github.com/fangzr/cxr-contrabench-code

[5] MedVH: Towards Systematic Evaluation of Hallucination for LVLMs in the Medical Context (Gu et al., Ohio State / Oxford), with CARES and Med-HallMark as canonical siblings (2024; arXiv 2024 (CC-BY 4.0); data hosted on PhysioNet (medvh 1.0.1, credentialed)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.02730
    what: Five tasks: wrongful image, none-of-the-above, clinically incorrect question, false-confidence justification, and report generation; 500 questions per multiple-choice task drawn from RAD-VQA, SLAKE, PMC-VQA, Path-VQA, VQA-Med-2021 and MIMIC-Diff-VQA, plus 200 MIMIC-CXR images for report generation; seven models (GPT-4V, MiniGPT, LLaVA, LLaVA-Med, Med-Flamingo, CheXagent, LLM-CXR); finding: medical LVLMs are 'often more' prone to hallucination than general ones. Canonical siblings to cite: CARES (NeurIPS 2024 Datasets & Benchmarks, about 41k QA pairs, 16 modalities, https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06007) and Med-HallMark / MediHall score (https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10185), whose severity tiers the advisor's framework adopts.
    why: Must-cite baselines for 'medical VLM hallucination benchmark'. MedVH's PhysioNet hosting is the concrete precedent that MIMIC-derived benchmark data must be redistributed through PhysioNet, not GitHub/HF. Its 'clinically incorrect question' task is the medical analogue of the plan's false-premise arm, and its medical-vs-general finding is the hypothesis the student can test on small open models.
    code/data: https://github.com/dongzizhu/MedVH ; https://physionet.org/content/medvh/1.0.1/ (credentialed); CARES https://cares-ai.github.io/ ; Med-HallMark https://github.com/ydk122024/Med-HallMark

[6] MedHEval: Benchmarking Hallucinations and Mitigation Strategies in Medical LVLMs (Chang et al., Penn State / GE Healthcare) (2025; arXiv, Mar 2025, 'preprint, under review'; lead author a Penn State student interning at GE Healthcare) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02157
    what: 15,976 VQA pairs built with GPT-4 from reports and metadata (chest X-ray subset: 5,587 pairs on 790 images from MIMIC-CXR and IU-Xray); 11 models (GPT-4o, LLaVA-NeXT 7B/13B, MiniGPT-4, LLaVA-Med 1/1.5, LLM-CXR, Med-Flamingo, RadFM, CheXagent, XrayGPT); causal taxonomy visual misinterpretation / knowledge deficiency / context misalignment; seven training-free mitigations compared (VCD, DoLa, OPERA, AVISC, M3ID, DAMRO, PAI); human check by two annotators on 50 responses (Pearson 0.834); A6000 GPUs; code public. Finding: vision-grounding methods (PAI) help visual misinterpretation, nothing helps context misalignment, some methods degrade performance.
    why: The only paper that runs VCD/PAI-class decoding on chest X-ray questions, so it is the published baseline for the plan's VCD arm and the published reason to add a grounding-based arm. Its 50-item human check shows how small the audit can be and still pass review.
    code/data: https://github.com/Aofei-Chang/MedHEval (datasets, implementations, mitigation baselines)

    url: https://papers.miccai.org/miccai-2025/paper/0083_paper.pdf

[10] RULE (EMNLP 2024) and MMed-RAG (ICLR 2025): retrieval-augmented factuality for medical VLMs (Xia et al.) (2024-2025; RULE: EMNLP 2024 main (https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.05131); MMed-RAG: ICLR 2025) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13085
    what: RULE: calibrated selection of the number of retrieved contexts to control factuality risk, plus preference fine-tuning to balance parametric vs retrieved knowledge (+47.4% factual accuracy on medical VQA and report generation over three datasets). MMed-RAG: domain-aware retriever, adaptive context selection and RAG-based preference fine-tuning (+43.8% over five radiology/ophthalmology/pathology datasets). Both require fine-tuning; code public.
    why: These are the canonical 'retrieval-based techniques' the umbrella names and the citations for the fact that retrieved context can also induce hallucination. The student cannot fine-tune, so the feasible unit is the training-free half: nearest-neighbour exemplar retrieval with a RULE-style calibrated k, reported as 'retrieval without alignment' with these papers as the fine-tuned upper bound.
    code/data: https://github.com/richard-peng-xia/MMed-RAG ; https://github.com/richard-peng-xia/RULE

ChestX-Det: 3,578 ChestX-ray14 images, 13 classes (atelectasis, calcification, cardiomegaly, consolidation, diffuse nodule, effusion, emphysema, fibrosis, fracture, mass, nodule, pleural thickening, pneumothorax), bounding boxes and polygon masks by three board-certified radiologists, Apache-2.0, direct zip download. Oakden-Rayner: a radiologist's visual review found ChestX-ray14 label positive predictive values 'mostly between 10% and 30% lower' than documented, with hidden stratification.
    why: Gives the student an openly redistributable, radiologist-verified, mask-ready 'real' arm with no credentialing delay: ChestX-Det polygons double as LaMa/RadEdit edit masks for the removed-finding arm, laterality comes from box position, and edited/generated derivatives can be released on HF/GitHub with the NIH attribution trio. Never use the NLP-derived 14 labels as ground truth; reviewers will cite Oakden-Rayner.

[12] PhysioNet credentialed path: MIMIC-CXR, VinDr-CXR and MS-CXR under the Credentialed Health Data License 1.5.0, and PhysioNet's LLM-API notice (2019-2026; PhysioNet (MIT Laboratory for Computational Physiology)) verified=True
    url: https://physionet.org/about/licenses/physionet-credentialed-health-data-license-150/
    what: License 1.5.0 clauses: the licensee 'will not share access to PhysioNet restricted data with anyone else', will not attempt re-identification, uses data 'for the sole purpose of lawful use in scientific research', must 'contribute code associated with publications ... to a repository that is open', must maintain physical and electronic security and current human-subjects (CITI) training. VinDr-CXR (18,000 DICOMs, 17 radiologists, 22 local + 6 global labels, 3 readers per training image, 5-reader consensus test, IRB-approved with consent waived) and MS-CXR (1,162 radiologist box-sentence pairs for 8 findings on MIMIC-CXR) are both under this credentialed license; VinDr's Kaggle copy exists but its rules were not retrievable. PhysioNet's notice forbids 'sending it through APIs provided by companies like OpenAI, or using it in online platforms like ChatGPT'; Azure OpenAI (with human-review opt-out), Bedrock, Vertex Gemini and Claude are listed as acceptable, with the licensee responsible for verifying any platform. Derived datasets (MS-CXR, MedVH) are re-hosted on PhysioNet under the same license.
    why: A credentialed arm costs CITI plus credentialing time, forbids GitHub/HF release of edited or generated MIMIC/VinDr images (they must go through PhysioNet), and makes RunPod community pods and Kaggle uploads a DUA risk. Keep it as an optional local-hardware replication reported in aggregate, not the core of a six-week project.
    code/data: VinDr-CXR https://physionet.org/content/vindr-cxr/1.0.0/ ; MS-CXR https://physionet.org/content/ms-cxr/1.1.0/ ; notice https://physionet.org/news/post/gpt-responsible-use

[13] Saudi research-ethics framework for secondary use of public de-identified data: NCBE Implementing Regulations v3 (2025) and the KFUPM IRB (2025-2026; KFUPM Deanship of Research IRB page; National Committee of BioEthics (KACST) official regulations PDF) verified=True
    url: https://ri.kfupm.edu.sa/dr/scholarly-support/institutional-review-board
    what: KFUPM IRB: reviews 'all research activities that involve human subjects', explicitly including 'personal/sensitive data use', for any 'faculty, researcher staff, or student', funded or unfunded, before the study starts; review is full-board, expedited or exempt by risk; the application needs the Research Application Form, an NCBE certificate for every team member (https://ncbeportal.kacst.gov.sa), 2-page CVs and an informed-consent statement; contact IRB@kfupm.edu.sa; no processing time stated. NCBE Implementing Regulations v3 (2025, https://ncbe.kacst.gov.sa/media/wpokc4ij/implementing-regulations-of-the-law-of-ethics-of-research-on-living-creatures_.pdf): Article 10.19 allows expedited review when risk is minimal, identity is not revealed, or data are 'information, records or samples that were previously collected ... for non-research purposes'; Article 10.33 lets the committee exempt from periodic follow-up research on previously collected data that 'is generally and publicly available' or 'recorded in a manner that does not reveal the identity of the source person'. No article exempting public de-identified secondary data from review altogether was found; the Law defines a human subject as a person who consents to research 'on him'.
    why: The report must state the IRB determination. Safe sequence: NCBE certificate in week 1; a one-page exemption/expedited query to IRB@kfupm.edu.sa citing Articles 10.19/10.33 and the source datasets' own IRB and de-identification statements (VinDr IRB-approved with consent waived; NIH de-identified; PhysioNet credentialed); a report sentence that no new human data were collected and that only public de-identified images were analysed.
    code/data: none (regulatory documents; text extracted locally from the official PDF)

[14] Reporting and Responsible-AI framing: TRIPOD-LLM, CLAIM 2024, WHO LMM guidance, and the RadEdit / MedGemma use terms (2024-2025; TRIPOD-LLM, Nature Medicine 2025; CLAIM 2024 update, Radiology: AI; WHO guidance 18 Jan 2024; Hugging Face model cards) verified=True
    url: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39779929/
    what: TRIPOD-LLM: 19 items and 50 sub-items, modular by task, emphasising transparency, human oversight and task-specific performance (checklist builder https://tripod-llm.vercel.app/). CLAIM 2024: 44-item medical-imaging AI checklist (https://www.equator-network.org/reporting-guidelines/checklist-for-artificial-intelligence-in-medical-imaging-claim-a-guide-for-authors-and-reviewers/). WHO LMM guidance: 40+ recommendations including post-release audits (https://www.who.int/news/item/18-01-2024-who-releases-ai-ethics-and-governance-guidance-for-large-multi-modal-models). RadEdit (ECCV 2024, https://huggingface.co/microsoft/radedit): 400M-parameter 512-px latent diffusion editor trained on MIMIC-CXR, NIH ChestX-ray14 and CheXpert frontal images; weights MSRLA (research-only), pipeline MIT; needs edit and keep masks plus a text prompt; 'not intended for use in clinical decision-making'. MedGemma-4b-it (https://huggingface.co/google/medgemma-4b-it): gated under Health AI Developer Foundations terms; outputs 'not intended to directly inform clinical diagnosis'; trained on MIMIC-CXR among others (MIMIC-CXR classification macro-F1 88.9 vs Gemma 3 4B 81.2).
    why: ML4H/MIDL/BioNLP reviewers expect a limitations block that says: no clinical claims; ground truth is the radiologist annotation, not the student's reading; edited/generated images were checked by non-experts only for artefacts; any LLM judge was validated against humans; all model and data terms are listed. Use TRIPOD-LLM items as the methods checklist, and note MedGemma's MIMIC-CXR training as a contamination caveat that favours the NIH/ChestX-Det test bed.
    code/data: RadEdit weights MSRLA / pipeline MIT at https://huggingface.co/microsoft/radedit ; MedGemma gated at https://huggingface.co/google/medgemma-4b-it
