    url: https://ml4h.ahli.cc/submit/call-for-papers/
    what: ML4H 2026's deadline (10 Sep 2026) has passed; its Findings track is 4 pages, non-archival, permits prior or concurrent submission with disclosure and has author mentorship, which is the model for ML4H 2027 (expected about Sep 2027, not announced). MIDL 2027 (https://2027.midl.io/important-dates): full papers abstract 30 Nov / paper 4 Dec 2026, notification 12 Feb 2027; short papers are 3 pages, welcome early-stage and negative results, dates TBD (the track opens after the full-paper cycle). MICCAI 2027: workshop calls not yet published. ISBI 2027 (https://biomedicalimaging.org/2027/): 4-page papers due 26 Oct 2026, 1-page abstracts due 1 Feb 2027 (poster only, not in proceedings). EMBC 2027 (https://embc.embs.org/2027/papers/): contributed papers due 24 Jan 2027 (4 pages plus a reference page), decisions 16 Apr 2027, 1-page poster papers due 23 Apr 2027. AIIT 2027 (University of Jeddah, IEEE Xplore, https://easychair.org/cfp/aiit2027): listed deadline 20 Oct 2026 with notification 5 Dec 2026, a year before the event.
    why: Best domain fit, in order: ML4H 2027 Findings with the advisor as co-author, MIDL 2027 short paper (3 pages, spring 2027), EMBC 2027 (IEEE, 24 Jan 2027, regional-friendly); all are compatible with a Nov 2026 report, whereas ISBI's 4-page deadline is not and AIIT's calendar needs a sanity check.
    code/data: none

-- gaps_and_opportunities --
  * No paper combines matched real / inpainted-removal / generated chest-X-ray triplets with abstention-aware scoring: Lotfinia et al. use box occlusion and swaps, HalluCXR uses natural stratification, RadEdit was used to stress-test classifiers rather than VLM hallucination. Anatomically plausible removal plus Kalai-style confidence targets and Effective Reliability is an open, cheap contribution.
  * The advisor's claim that general-purpose models hallucinate less than medical-specialized ones (76.6% vs 51.3% median, literature-derived) has never been tested on sub-4B open models under controlled image interventions; MedGemma-4B vs Qwen3-VL-2B / InternVL3.5-2B / Gemma 4 E2B on an NIH/ChestX-Det test bed (outside MedGemma's MIMIC-CXR training data) is a direct, low-cost test.
  * Grounding-based mitigation on small models is untested: oracle region crops from ChestX-Det polygons (upper bound) vs model self-grounding via grid prompting (Gosai et al.) vs a counter-evidence verification pass (CoEV, MICCAI 2026, https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18609, code not yet released) are all training-free and comparable in one table.
  * Training-free retrieval (nearest-neighbour radiologist-labelled exemplars via BiomedCLIP or RAD-DINO embeddings with a RULE-style calibrated k) has no student-scale result showing whether retrieved context raises or lowers 'yes' bias; this connects directly to the plan's Mirage and Rethinking-CD yes-bias controls.
  * Negated-option attraction (CXR-ContraBench) has not been measured under abstention instructions; the plan's PN prompt plus the Kalai instruction answers this with no extra data.
  * Tubes, lines and devices: no openly licensed radiologist-labelled device dataset was verified in this pass (RANZCR CLiP on Kaggle unverified; Chest ImaGenome is credentialed), so object-level device questions are a gap the student should not try to fill in six weeks.
  * For yes/no questions the student needs no LLM judge, which removes the judge-validation burden that HalluCXR (250 labels) and RadFlag (547 labels) had to carry; stating this explicitly is a reviewer-friendly simplification.
  * A PhysioNet-hosted MIMIC-derived version of the benchmark (MedVH precedent) would be a durable lab asset for the advisor's 2026-2029 trustworthy-medical-VLM project, but it is a post-course extension, not a six-week deliverable.
  * The education domain has no footprint in the advisor's record and no data or ethics advantage; dropping it costs nothing.

-- recommended_for_this_project --
  * Choose healthcare / chest X-ray and frame the component as the first controlled empirical test of Alshahrani & Behzad (2026): claim 2 (general vs medical-specialized) and claim 3 (retrieval/grounding mitigation). Cite it first in the introduction, reuse its taxonomy vocabulary (MediHall severity tiers; HalluCXR's eight categories if any free-text is scored) and its 'lifecycle obligation' framing in the discussion; cite XAI-CLIP as the lab's precedent for region-level interventions.
  * Replace the COCO core with ChestX-Det (Apache-2.0, 3,578 NIH ChestX-ray14 images, 13 finding classes, boxes and polygons by three radiologists) as the real arm: polygons become the LaMa (or RadEdit, if it fits the 1060) masks for the removed-finding arm, boxes give laterality, and all edited/generated derivatives can be released on HF/GitHub with the NIH attribution trio plus the ChestX-Det citation. Do not use the NLP-derived NIH labels as ground truth (Oakden-Rayner).
  * Keep MIMIC-CXR / VinDr-CXR / MS-CXR only as a gated, credentialed replication: CITI course and credentialing in week 1 if attempted, run only on local hardware (never RunPod community pods, never Kaggle uploads, never OpenAI-style APIs), results reported in aggregate, no derived images released outside PhysioNet.
  * Models: keep Qwen3-VL-2B, InternVL3.5-2B and Gemma 4 E2B; add google/medgemma-4b-it as the medical-specialized arm (accept the HAI-DEF terms on HF, cite them, note MIMIC-CXR in its training data); drop the general-domain SENTINEL checkpoint and Qwen2-VL-2B base unless a specific comparison needs them. Budget impact: one to two extra pod-hours in bf16.
  * Questions: object-level presence/absence on the 13 ChestX-Det classes (plus laterality where boxes allow), six per image as planned; keep the PN negated-premise prompt (CXR-ContraBench) and the verbatim Kalai abstention instructions at t = 0.5 and 0.9; drop tubes/lines/devices from the core.
  * Keep the blind text-only arm but cite Lotfinia et al. (2026) as prior evidence (text-only 60.1% vs best multimodal 66.2% on MIMIC-CXR) and add their CGR/UAR/IS metrics; the removed-finding arm gives a CGR analogue (correct 'yes' answers that flip to 'no' after removal) and the generated twin gives the layout-matched control they lack.
  * Mitigation family (umbrella-compliant, all training-free): (a) retrieval-augmented prompting with k nearest radiologist-labelled exemplars (BiomedCLIP or RAD-DINO embeddings; k calibrated as in RULE); (b) grounding: oracle crop from the ChestX-Det box and self-grounding via grid prompting (Gosai) followed by a verification pass; (c) VCD as the decoding baseline with MedHEval's CXR numbers as the reference. Report all under the existing yes-bias controls and cite RULE/MMed-RAG as the fine-tuned upper bound not attempted.
  * Human audit: the student audits edits only for artefacts and plausibility (not findings); ground truth stays with the radiologist annotations; target at least 200 audited items with Cohen's kappa (RadFlag 547, HalluCXR 250, MedHEval 50 as reference points); no LLM judge is needed for yes/no answers.
  * Ethics in week 1: obtain the NCBE certificate (ncbeportal.kacst.gov.sa); email IRB@kfupm.edu.sa a one-page exemption/expedited determination request citing NCBE Implementing Regulations v3 Articles 10.19 and 10.33 and the datasets' own IRB and de-identification statements; put the determination, the 'no new human data' sentence, and the data and model terms (NIH attribution, ChestX-Det Apache-2.0, RadEdit MSRLA research-only, MedGemma HAI-DEF) in the report's methods.
  * Report structure: follow TRIPOD-LLM's modular items and CLAIM 2024 where applicable; include an explicit 'not for clinical use' statement and limitations on non-expert verification, label noise, contamination (MedGemma and CheXagent trained on MIMIC-CXR) and small-model scope; cite the WHO LMM guidance for the responsible-AI framing.
  * Venues: keep the EACL 2027 SRW mentoring request (6 Nov 2026) only if report v1 exists by then; realistic primary targets are NAACL 2027 workshops/SRW (5 Feb 2027 window), EMBC 2027 (24 Jan 2027, IEEE 4+1 pages), MIDL 2027 short paper (3 pages, spring 2027) and ML4H 2027 Findings (about Sep 2027) with the advisor as co-author; BioNLP 2027 (about Apr 2027) if the abstention/scoring angle dominates. Skip the ISBI 2027 4-page deadline (26 Oct 2026) and treat AIIT 2027's 20 Oct 2026 deadline as unreliable until confirmed.
  * Minimum credible unit implied by Part B for a student paper: 300-900 images, 4-6 models including one medical-specialized, 1-3k yes/no items, human validation on 200+ items, one rented GPU; the existing plan's power calculation (940-1,742 pairs) already sits inside this envelope.

-- cautions --
  * The web-search budget was exhausted near the end; the following were NOT verified: RANZCR CLiP license, the NIH BBox_List_2017 counts, the Kaggle VinBigData competition rules, Clinical NLP 2027 dates, ML4H 2027 dates, MICCAI 2027 workshop deadlines, MIDL 2027 short-paper dates, the AIIT 2027 calendar's plausibility, the exact HF id of 'MedGemma 1.5 4B' used by Lotfinia et al., ReXTrust's venue ('AIMedHealth'), and CheXlocalize access terms.
  * DBLP was bot-blocked (Anubis) on two attempts; the advisor's record was verified instead through the arXiv API, KFUPM Pure and Semantic Scholar. No public page for the JRCAI project titled 'Evaluating VLMs/LLMs Hallucination in Domain-Specific Tasks' was found; the title appears only in the student's pasted listing.
  * Alshahrani & Behzad (2026), HalluCXR, CXR-ContraBench, Lotfinia et al. and MedHEval are preprints without peer review; the advisor's 76.6% vs 51.3% figure is a literature-derived median, not an experiment, so cite it as a hypothesis.
  * Student status of first authors in Part B is not stated on any opened page (HalluCXR: KCL biostatistics authors; MedHEval: Penn State intern at GE; RadFlag: Stanford/Harvard; Xu: single author); treat 'student first author' as plausible but unverified.
  * PhysioNet terms: my reading that RunPod community pods and Kaggle uploads conflict with the 'will not share access' and security clauses is an interpretation; PhysioNet's explicit prohibition covers OpenAI-style APIs and ChatGPT, and it lists Azure OpenAI, Bedrock, Vertex and Claude as acceptable. Confirm with PhysioNet before any cloud use of credentialed data.
  * NCBE regulations contain no explicit category exempting public de-identified secondary data from review; Article 10.19 (expedited) and 10.33 (follow-up exemption) are the closest provisions, and the KFUPM IRB page lists 'personal/sensitive data use' as in scope. Whether a secondary analysis of foreign public de-identified images needs a KFUPM application is for the IRB to determine; budget a week for the answer.
  * NIH ChestX-ray14 terms ('no restrictions', three attribution requirements) were read from a Hugging Face mirror that reproduces the NIH README; the NIH Box page and NIH news release could not be fetched. Re-read the README on the Box site before publishing derivatives.
  * RadEdit on a GTX 1060 (400M-parameter LDM at 512 px, masks required) is untested here; its weights are MSRLA research-only and the card leaves research redistribution of outputs unaddressed. MedGemma's HAI-DEF terms require acceptance and define clinical use broadly; an evaluation paper is research use but the terms do not explicitly discuss publication.
  * ChestX-Det's class list (fracture, calcification, diffuse nodule, etc.) differs from the NIH 14 labels and from VinDr's 22 local labels; cross-dataset comparisons need a mapping, and base rates per class are small for rarer findings (power check needed per class).

-- confidence_notes -- Part A: high confidence; the advisor's June 2026 hallucination framework paper, 2025-2026 medical VLM preprints, journal venues and active projects were read from arXiv (abs and HTML), the KFUPM Pure profile/project page and the Semantic Scholar API; DBLP itself was inaccessible. Part B: high confidence for all listed papers (arXiv abs/HTML, PMLR pages, the MICCAI open-access PDF extracted locally); student status of first authors is not verifiable from the pages. Part C: medium-high; PhysioNet license 1.5.0, VinDr-CXR, MS-CXR, the PhysioNet GPT notice, ChestX-Det, RadEdit and MedGemma cards, Oakden-Rayner, the KFUPM IRB page and the official NCBE Implementing Regulations v3 (2025) PDF were opened; the NIH terms come from a mirror; the IRB-applicability reading is my interpretation, not legal advice. Part D: medium-high; EACL 2027 SRW, the ACL 2027 joint workshop call, ML4H 2026 CFP, MIDL 2027 dates, MICCAI 2027, ISBI 2027, EMBC 2027 and AIIT 2027 pages were opened; BioNLP 2027, Clinical NLP 2027, NAACL 2027 SRW, ML4H 2027 and MICCAI 2027 workshop deadlines are not yet published and are inferred from 2026 patterns.


######## CRITIC ########
DOMAIN VERDICT: Chest X-ray, with devices and foreign objects as the primary 'objects' and one or two coarse findings as a bridge; no hybrid, and the general domain survives only as citations (POPE, BEAF, Kalai) and reusable pipeline code. Decisive evidence: (1) Advisor fit is verified, not inferred: Alshahrani & Behzad, arXiv 2606.13211 (June 2026, KFUPM and SDAIA-KFUPM JRCAI) is a medical-imaging hallucination framework whose open problems the component can answer empirically; the advisor has no education-domain work, and the umbrella is JRCAI-funded. (2) A fully credential-free CXR path exists and every link in it was verified this session: NIH ChestX-ray14 images carry no use restrictions beyond three attributions (Google Cloud public-dataset page); RANZCR CLiP supplies radiologist device labels and 17,999 polylines on 30,083 of those same NIH images (CLiP abstract) via a Kaggle late-join; Object-CXR's mirror offers 5,000 foreign-object positives with boxes and 5,000 real negatives for non-commercial research; ChestX-Det adds radiologist boxes and polygons under Apache-2.0; CheXmask masks are CC BY 4.0; RadEdit (gated click-through, MSRLA weights, MIT pipeline), XReal (CC BY-NC 4.0) and SynthCheX-75K-v2 (Apache-2.0) give editing and generation tools; MedGemma 1.5 4B (gated auto), CheXagent-2-3b, CheXOne and NV-Reason-CXR-3B give sub-4B medical comparators. (3) The 2026 positioning set is dense and verified (HalluCXR, CXR-ContraBench, MC-CXR, Lotfinia et al., CheXpercept, When Retrieval Hurts, CoEV, EVADE, DualRead), and none uses same-scene edited or generated radiographs, device-level objects or instructed, penalised abstention, so the three-source plus Effective Reliability design keeps its novelty. (4) The education alternative's real advantages (labels by construction, CPU-only edits) are matched on CXR by polyline-masked removal with by-construction labels, and its recommendation rested on the false premise that a CXR study needs PhysioNet credentials, a radiologist and RoentGen. Conditions attached to the verdict: PhysioNet/MIMIC must be off the critical path entirely (June 2026 'significant delays' notice; cloud and API use constrained by the Sept 2025 policy); contamination must be reported rather than claimed absent, because RANZCR, Object-CXR and ChestX-Det images are in the training sets of MedGemma 1.5, CheXagent, CheXOne, NV-Reason-CXR-3B and RadEdit, which makes the generalists (Qwen3-VL-2B, InternVL3.5-2B, Gemma 4 E2B) the clean hero models and the edited/generated arms the only contamination-free comparison; the ianpan detector may verify edits but must not score real RANZCR items or serve as the grounding expert on them (use the CCD device detector, torchxrayvision Support-Devices heads or MedSigLIP zero-shot instead); CANDID-PTX is out; RadEdit removal is not to be relied on (cxr-grounding), so removal is LaMa over dilated polylines or boxes in fp32 on the 1060 with a human audit; and the Day-1 pilot must clear the floor-effect test in gap 2 before the primary object class and N are frozen.

GAP: RANZCR CLiP is a re-annotation of NIH ChestX-ray14: UID-to-filename mapping, Kaggle terms, contamination matrix and realised item yield
  why: Four agents put RANZCR CLiP on the critical path as the device-labelled real arm while disagreeing about where its images come from. The CLiP descriptor's abstract (verified, PMC8553862) says all 30,083 radiographs are 'from the publicly available NIH ChestXRay14 dataset'; the Victorian hospitals are labeller affiliations. That changes licensing (NIH 'no restrictions' pixels vs Kaggle competition rules on the JPEGs/labels), makes CheXmask masks and attribution-only release possible if a mapping exists, and means every proposed medical model has seen the real-arm pixels (MedGemma 1.5's card lists ChestX-ray14 as training data; CheXinstruct/CheXagent, CheXOne and NV-Reason-CXR-3B list ChestXray14; RadEdit trained on NIH-CXR). Nobody computed how many single-device images and patients the selection rule yields, so the power calculation is still floating.

GAP: Floor-effect check: can 2-4B generalist VLMs detect tubes, lines and devices at all, and which object class is safest?
  why: The edited-arm logic needs models that answer 'yes' on real device images so that a removed device can expose a hallucinated 'yes'; if Qwen3-VL-2B, InternVL3.5-2B and Gemma 4 E2B are at chance on ETT/NGT/CVC presence at 512-1024 px, the design collapses to a floor and no agent has a single published number for them on device questions. The education agent flagged the mirror-image ceiling risk, but the CXR floor risk is unexamined, and it also decides between devices, foreign objects and coarse findings as the primary object class.

GAP: Edited-arm validity on radiographs: generic inpainters on grayscale CXRs, a leak-free removal verifier, and a reader-audit path
  why: Every agent assumes LaMa works on chest X-rays, but LaMa was trained on natural RGB photos; the one CXR-trained editor (RadEdit) failed at removal in the only precedent (cxr-grounding, verified: 'RadEdit could not remove findings convincingly, so removals were dropped'); Cheff's 1024-px stage probably exceeds 6 GB; and the proposed verification oracle (ianpan/cxr-lines-and-tubes) is a five-fold ensemble trained on RANZCR itself, so its presence scores on real RANZCR items are in-sample. Nobody identified an independent device detector or a concrete route to a radiologist reader.

GAP: References in the advisor's own framework that nobody resolved: Kim et al. 2025 (Med-HALT), MedHallBench, 'CXR-VisHal', plus GEMeX and ReXErr-v1
  why: The fit agent frames the project as testing the advisor's claim that general models hallucinate less than medical ones, but the verified source of the 76.6% vs 51.3% figure in Alshahrani & Behzad 2026 is Kim et al. 2025 on Med-HALT, a text-only LLM benchmark, and the advisor's paper cites 'CXR-VisHal' to the MedHallBench reference (arXiv 2412.18947), which looks like a mislabel. A student paper co-authored with the advisor must cite these correctly, and two groundable CXR resources (GEMeX, ReXErr-v1) that could supply device-level questions were only mentioned in passing.

-- contradictions --
  * RANZCR CLiP provenance and licence: med_data says the images come from RANZCR hospital sites and Kaggle rules govern; med_synthetic says images are CC BY 4.0 and metadata CC0; med_mitigation says they are NIH ChestX-ray14 radiographs 'on CC-licensed images'; med_models and fit_and_ethics mark it unverified. Verified: the CLiP abstract (PMC8553862) says all 30,083 radiographs are 'from the publicly available NIH ChestXRay14 dataset'; the Victorian hospitals are labeller affiliations; the paper says Kaggle access 'requires user registration and acceptance of data licensing'; the CC BY/CC0 wording is Springer Nature article boilerplate, not a dataset licence; the Kaggle rules page is JS-only and still unread.
  * CANDID-PTX: med_data says it closed to non-NZ users in Feb 2024; med_synthetic and med_mitigation recommend its chest-tube masks (the latter 'after an online ethics-course registration'). Verified closed (figshare API record v2, modified 2025-06-27: 'not available for access by users outside Health New Zealand'); it is also in CheXinstruct training.
  * Contamination of a credential-free real arm: med_mitigation, fit_and_ethics and med_synthetic claim NIH/ChestX-Det/RANZCR items reduce contamination relative to MIMIC and that only NV-Reason-CXR-3B is contaminated; med_models says only official MIMIC-CXR/VinDr test splits are safe. Verified: MedGemma 1.5's card lists ChestX-ray14 (NIH Clinical Center) in its training-data ownership section; CheXinstruct (CheXagent) lists ChestXray14, Object-CXR, CANDID-PTX, VinDr-CXR, PadChest, SIIM and RSNA; CheXOne lists ChestXray14 and Object-CXR; RadEdit trained on NIH-CXR. No credential-free real-arm source is unseen by the proposed medical models or the editor; only edited and generated arms are clean by construction.
  * Primary object class: fit_and_ethics says drop tubes/lines/devices from the core and use ChestX-Det's 13 finding classes; med_benchmarks, med_data, med_models and med_mitigation make devices the primary objects; med_synthetic wants devices (removal only) plus findings (effusion, consolidation, edema, pneumothorax). Not resolved by evidence; see verdict and gap 2.
  * Definition of the S arm: med_data redefines S as a synthetic object cut-pasted or RadEdit-inserted into a real negative; med_synthetic (XReal layout twin at 256 px or RadEdit img2img), med_mitigation (RadEdit whole-image re-synthesis) and med_benchmarks (SynthCheX-75K unpaired stratum) keep a fully generated image. Verified: SynthCheX-75K-v2 carries only MIMIC-CXR pathology labels and never mentions devices, so it cannot supply device-positive twins.
  * RadEdit as a remover and its dtype on the GTX 1060: med_synthetic and med_mitigation list RadEdit masked removal as an edited-arm option; med_data cites the cxr-grounding repository, which (verified) states 'RadEdit could not remove findings convincingly, so removals were dropped' and 'edited films look sicker than real ones'. Dtype: med_benchmarks says fp16 with attention slicing, med_mitigation 'plausibly fp16', med_synthetic fp32 because Pascal fp16 is a throughput trap; RadEdit's card recommends bfloat16, which the 1060 lacks. fp32 is the only choice consistent with the stated 1/64 fp16 rate.
  * PhysioNet LLM and cloud policy: med_data says no GPT/Claude judge on PhysioNet images; fit_and_ethics says Azure OpenAI, Bedrock, Vertex Gemini and Claude are 'listed as acceptable'. Verified: the April 18 2023 post named those four; the Sept 24 2025 post (/news/post/llm-responsible-use/) names no platform, 'strongly' prefers locally deployed LLMs, allows cloud services only with zero data retention, no training and no human review, and says PhysioNet cannot verify external services. Neither post addresses third-party GPU hosts, so fit_and_ethics's 'never RunPod community pods' versus med_data's pod-based scored runs stays unresolved.
  * PhysioNet credentialing time: med_data quotes an FAQ saying 'typically 1-2 weeks'; med_benchmarks says the FAQ is 404. Verified: /about/faq/ and /about/faq both return 404, and a June 18 2026 notice (repeating a June 17 2024 one) warns that applications 'are likely to be subject to significant delays' with no timeframe. Anything MIMIC/VinDr-based cannot be on the Nov 8 critical path.
  * Aligned-versus-base pair and medical comparator: med_models proposes CheXOne vs Qwen2.5-VL-3B and warns that MedGemma-vs-Gemma-3 is confounded by the MedSigLIP encoder swap; med_mitigation proposes exactly MedGemma 1.5 4B vs Gemma 3 4B; fit_and_ethics picks google/medgemma-4b-it (v1.0); med_benchmarks picks MedGemma-1.5-4B with optional NV-Reason-CXR-3B. Note CheXOne's card metadata says base_model Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct while the paper says Qwen2.5-VL-3B, and its safetensors total is 3.75B, not 4.07B.
  * CheXagent-2-3b licence: med_models (MIT card, GitHub 'Other/NOASSERTION'), med_mitigation (MIT card, repo LICENSE CC BY-NC-ND 4.0), edu_alternative (CC BY-NC-4.0). Verified: HF card license 'mit'; the repository LICENSE file is CC BY-NC-ND 4.0. XReal likewise: med_synthetic says MIT, the LICENSE file is CC BY-NC 4.0.
  * Lotfinia et al. 2026 (arXiv 2606.17710): fit_and_ethics reports nine systems including GPT-5, text-only 60.1% vs best multimodal 66.2%, and two radiologists on an 80-case subset; med_models reports eight systems, MedGemma-1.5-4B pooled ~58.2 and a '50/50 support-devices block'; the v3 abstract says eight open-weight systems, a text-only medical model at 55.3% pooled, and a reader study with three radiologists, two reading 200 cases at 86.0% and 82.0%. Numbers are version-dependent; the support-devices block appears in neither the abstract nor the repository README.
  * Domain: edu_alternative alone recommends education, conditional on the student holding PhysioNet credentials, a radiologist rater and RoentGen access by Oct 19; the other six recommend chest X-rays. Its CXR comparison assumes credentialed data and RoentGen are necessary for a CXR study, which the data, synthetic and mitigation agents show is false (NIH, RANZCR, Object-CXR, ChestX-Det, CheXmask, RadEdit, XReal, SynthCheX are all credential-free).
  * Minor numeric and venue drift: HalluCXR 'InternVL2 (6B)' is flagged as non-standard by med_benchmarks but quoted as fact by fit_and_ethics (the abstract gives no sizes); ReXVQA MedGemma 83.84% (med_benchmarks) vs 83.24% (med_mitigation); CCD is 'Findings of ACL 2026' in med_mitigation but 'accepted to ACL 2026 (not Findings)' in the repository README; MIMIC-Ext-CXR-QBA shows two publication dates (med_data).

-- suspicious_claims --
  * CHECKED, false: 'RANZCR CLiP images are CC BY 4.0, metadata CC0' (med_synthetic). That is the Scientific Data article licence; the data sit on Kaggle under competition terms (unread), and the pixels are NIH ChestX-ray14 images (CLiP abstract, PMC8553862).
  * CHECKED, false: 'RANZCR CLiP images come from RANZCR hospital sites / previously de-identified hospital archives' (med_data). The abstract states the 30,083 radiographs are from NIH ChestXRay14; Royal Melbourne, Alfred, Monash, Eastern Health and Barwon are author/labeller affiliations.
  * CHECKED, false: 'CANDID-PTX obtainable after an online ethics-course registration' (med_mitigation) and its use for chest-tube masks (med_synthetic). The figshare record says it has been unavailable to users outside Health New Zealand since Feb 2024 (v2, modified 2025-06-27).
  * CHECKED, false: 'an NIH/ChestX-Det test bed lies outside MedGemma's MIMIC-CXR training data' (fit_and_ethics; echoed by med_mitigation and med_synthetic). google/medgemma-1.5-4b-it's card lists ChestX-ray14 (NIH Clinical Center) among its training datasets; CheXinstruct lists ChestXray14, Object-CXR and CANDID-PTX; CheXOne lists ChestXray14 and Object-CXR; nvidia/NV-Reason-CXR-3B (licence 'nsclv1', base Qwen2.5-VL-3B) lists ChestX-ray14; microsoft/radedit trained on NIH-CXR.
  * CHECKED, partly misleading: ianpan/cxr-lines-and-tubes as 'verification tool and grounding oracle' (med_benchmarks, med_data, med_mitigation). The card confirms the model (ETT/NGT/CVC/Swan-Ganz masks and presence probabilities, mean validation AUROC 0.9684, weights 'for non-commercial research, reproducibility, and educational use', MIT code) but the released models are five patient-disjoint-fold ensembles trained on RANZCR plus 31,448 NIH negatives, so presence scores on any RANZCR image are in-sample for four of five folds; it must not score real RANZCR items and is only safe on edited/generated images.
  * CHECKED, outdated: 'PhysioNet lists Azure OpenAI, Bedrock, Vertex Gemini and Claude as acceptable' (fit_and_ethics) comes from the April 2023 post; the Sept 24 2025 post names no platform, prefers local deployment and requires zero retention, no training and no human review. 'Approval typically takes 1-2 weeks' (med_data) is stale: the FAQ is 404 and the June 18 2026 notice warns of significant delays without a timeframe.
  * CHECKED, scope error: the advisor's '76.6% vs 51.3% median hallucination-free rate, general vs medical-specialized' (fit_and_ethics) is attributed in Alshahrani & Behzad 2026 to Kim et al. 2025 (arXiv 2503.05777) on Med-HALT, a text-only LLM benchmark, not a vision-language result; the abstract's own question (2) is phrased as the opposite of its finding, so quote it carefully. The paper exists with KFUPM and SDAIA-KFUPM JRCAI affiliations and has no chest-radiography RAG content.
  * CHECKED, unresolved: 'CXR-VisHal' (fit_and_ethics, quoting the advisor) is cited in the advisor's paper to the MedHallBench reference (arXiv 2412.18947); no independent source could be checked because the shared web-search budget was exhausted; treat as a possible mislabel until verified (gap 4).
  * CHECKED, wrong details: XReal 'MIT' (med_synthetic) is CC BY-NC 4.0 per the repo LICENSE; CheXOne '4.07B' (med_models) is 3,754,622,976 parameters with card base_model 'Qwen/Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct'; CheXagent-2-3b 'CC BY-NC-4.0' (edu_alternative) is MIT on the card and CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 in the repo; SynthCheX-75K-v2 Apache-2.0 is stated only in the card text (API metadata has no licence) and the set has no device labels.
  * CHECKED, exist as described (with the drifts noted above): HalluCXR arXiv 2605.20469 (856 MIMIC-CXR images, 61.9-82.3%, no abstention, no release statement); CXR-ContraBench arXiv 2605.05810 (code at github.com/fangzr/cxr-contrabench-code); MC-CXR arXiv 2608.24118 (Findings of EMNLP 2026, PhysioNet); Lotfinia et al. arXiv 2606.17710 (MIT code at github.com/mahshadlotfinia/causal); When Retrieval Hurts arXiv 2609.31733; VGS-Decoding arXiv 2603.20314 (code upon acceptance); EVADE arXiv 2608.18833; DualRead arXiv 2609.06419; CoEV arXiv 2606.18609 (MICCAI 2026, code upon acceptance); CheXpercept arXiv 2606.21020; CCD repo (MIT, ships a 13-type device detector and VQA support); ARegionCD repo (MIT, Phi-3.5V only); ChestX-Det (Apache-2.0, three radiologists, direct zips); Object-CXR mirror hlk-1135/object-CXR ('free to download for scientific research and non-commercial usage', Google Drive link untested); ReXGradient-160K (gated auto, non-commercial, no redistribution, no credentialing); NIH ChestX-ray14 'no restrictions' plus three attribution requirements (Google Cloud public-dataset page); MedGemma 1.5 (gated auto, HAI-DEF, 4.3B); cxr-grounding repo (exists, but is an unpublished single-author project with a lab notebook, not a paper).
  * NOT VERIFIED by me (all agents also flagged or could not read): Kaggle rules text for RANZCR, VinBigData and SIIM; the Object-CXR Google Drive link; RadEdit, XReal and Cheff VRAM and per-image time on a GTX 1060; MedGemma 8-bit issue #5; Lotfinia's 'support-devices block'; Latent Drifting's pacemaker-removal claim (abstract mentions adding/removing diseases only); Rad-Phi4-Vision-CXR weights and numbers; PadChest-GR licence conflict; TQA/AI2D licence conflicts; Chartographer and other education-side 2026 arXiv ids; Qwen3.5-2B and Gemma 4 E2B cards (agent-verified via the HF API today, not re-checked). Method note: the shared WebSearch budget was exhausted before my checks, so every spot-check above used direct WebFetch of arXiv, Hugging Face API, GitHub, PMC, figshare API, PhysioNet and Google Cloud pages.


######## ANGLE: FILL: RANZCR CLiP is a re-annotation of NIH ChestX-ray14: UID-to-filename mapping, Kaggle terms, contamination matrix and realised item yield ########
angle: RANZCR CLiP provenance audit for the chest X-ray re-fit: where the pixels come from (NIH ChestX-ray14), whether a UID-to-NIH mapping exists, what the Kaggle terms allow, which candidate models/generators have seen those pixels, and what the label structure implies for the realised item count and the power calculation.

[1] RANZCR CLiP descriptor (Tang et al., 'CLiP, catheter and line position dataset', Scientific Data 8:285) (2021; Scientific Data (open access, CC BY 4.0 article); full-text XML also at https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/PMC8553862/fullTextXML) verified=True
    url: https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8553862/
    what: Verbatim: relevant radiographs were selected from NIH ChestXray14 'by an algorithm that was developed on the MIMIC-CXR set' (DenseNet on keyword-mined MIMIC labels; ~45,000 identified, ~35,000 manually selected); 'The dataset includes 30,083 chest radiographs from 3791 patients with a median age of 49. From the 30,083 there are 50,612 image level annotations and 17,999 manually segmented annotations.' Table 2 label counts: ETT Abnormal 79 / Borderline 1,138 / Normal 7,240; NGT Abnormal 267 / Borderline 530 / Incompletely Imaged 2,748 / Normal 4,799; CVC Abnormal 3,198 / Borderline 8,460 / Normal 21,323; Swan-Ganz present 830 (sums to 50,612). 43 labelers; ~30% of radiographs double-labelled, 10% triple-labelled; 5,100 consensus labels re-reviewed with ~15% discrepancies. Key sentence: 'No image processing was performed on the chest radiographs prior to upload... All images were upscaled to original resolution before conversion to JPEG and uploaded into the Kaggle repository.' Also: 'As both datasets are publicly available IRB approval is waived... we complied with... NIH ChestXray14 and MIMIC-CXR data usage guidelines.' Data Records say only that labels are 'a CSV in a single file' and segmentations are coordinate lists; 'Access requires user registration and acceptance of data licensing.' The paper gives no UID-generation method, no NIH-index mapping, no per-image device co-occurrence, and no train/test sizes.
    why: Settles the provenance: the Kaggle JPEGs are interpolated copies of the public NIH PNGs, so the real arm's pixels can be sourced from NIH under attribution-only terms and the whole pipeline can run at 1024 px. The 7.9 images per patient and the label totals (not image counts) are why the item yield must be computed from train.csv, not read off Table 2. The ~15% discrepancy rate is the label-noise figure to cite for the real arm.
    code/data: Kaggle competition ranzcr-clip-catheter-line-classification ('Subject to Competition Rules'); article CC BY 4.0

[2] NIH ChestX-ray14 terms, metadata columns and the Kaggle CC0 mirror (2017 (data), mirrors current; NIH Clinical Center release (Wang et al., CVPR 2017); HF mirror README; Kaggle dataset nih-chest-xrays/data) verified=True
    url: https://huggingface.co/datasets/alkzar90/NIH-Chest-X-ray-dataset
    what: HF mirror README quotes the NIH terms verbatim: 'There are no restrictions on the use of the NIH chest x-ray images. However, the dataset has the following attribution requirements' (link the NIH Box download site, cite the CVPR 2017 paper, acknowledge the NIH Clinical Center). 112,120 frontal PNGs at 1024x1024 from 30,805 patients, filenames like 00000798_022.png. The metadata file Data_Entry_2017_v2020.csv (header fetched from the mirror) has columns: Image Index, Finding Labels, Follow-up #, Patient ID, Patient Age, Patient Gender, View Position, OriginalImage[Width,Height], OriginalImagePixelSpacing[x,y] (e.g., 00000001_000.png ... PA, 2682, 2749). The Kaggle mirror's server-rendered page carries JSON-LD license 'CC0: Public Domain' (https://www.kaggle.com/datasets/nih-chest-xrays/data).
    why: Attribution-only pixels for the real arm and for any released item list. OriginalImage[Width,Height] is the expected pixel size of each RANZCR JPEG if 'upscaled to original resolution' used these values, which gives a near-unique matching key before any hashing. Both the NIH mirror and the RANZCR data can be attached to one Kaggle notebook, so the mapping job needs no download to the student's machine.
    code/data: NIH: no restrictions, attribution required; Kaggle mirror declared CC0; HF mirror alkzar90/NIH-Chest-X-ray-dataset

[3] No public RANZCR-to-NIH mapping found: i-pan/cxr-lines-and-tubes and analokmaus/kaggle-ranzcr-clip-public (7th place) (2021-2025; GitHub) verified=True
    url: https://github.com/i-pan/cxr-lines-and-tubes
    what: i-pan/cxr-lines-and-tubes (MIT) is Ian Pan's 'Chest X-ray line, tube, endpoint, and carina segmentation with RANZCR classification' pipeline (ensemble 0.97314 private AUROC); it references only RANZCR, releases no weights yet (HF release planned, 'not validated for clinical use'), and contains no UID-to-NIH table. analokmaus/kaggle-ranzcr-clip-public (https://github.com/analokmaus/kaggle-ranzcr-clip-public) pretrained on NIH ChestX-ray14, PadChest and MIMIC-CXR as external data; its input/nih_files.csv (67,468 unique NIH PNG paths under a column misleadingly headed 'StudyInstanceUID') is a list of NIH images used for pretraining, not a mapping. Neither repo, nor the descriptor, publishes the correspondence; Kaggle discussions/datasets could not be searched this turn (search budget exhausted), so that channel is unchecked.
    why: The mapping has to be built, and it is cheap: read each RANZCR JPEG's (width,height) without decoding (PIL .size), join to Data_Entry_2017_v2020.csv on OriginalImage[Width,Height] to get candidates, then confirm with pHash/dHash at 256 px (or SSIM on the top candidates). Expect most of the 30,083 images to resolve uniquely; log the rest as unmatched and exclude them. Rescale polyline coordinates by (1024/W, 1024/H) to use them on the NIH PNGs.
    code/data: i-pan repo MIT; analokmaus repo (licence not stated in README); nih_files.csv downloaded to C:\Users\messi\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\c--Belly-sProjects-Ai-Hallucination---RES-299\eb4d07d4-7ed1-423e-808e-65e052c42c40\scratchpad\dl_untrusted\nih_files.csv

Data Security' (recalled, unverified: 'not to transmit, duplicate, publish, redistribute or otherwise provide or make available the Competition Data to any party not participating in the Competition'). [5] CheXmask Database v1.0.0 (Gaggion et al., Scientific Data 11:511) (2024 (paper), v1.0.0 released 2025-01-22; PhysioNet, Scientific Data) verified=True

Open access under CC BY 4.0; each CSV's first column is the image ID of the source dataset, so NIH rows join on Image Index. CANDID-PTX is not included.
    why: Once a RANZCR image is mapped to its NIH filename, lung/heart masks come for free: they give the layout condition for the generated twin (ControlNet-style on the 1060), a 'keep' region for inpainting, and anatomy-relative location questions (e.g., 'is the CVC tip projected over the heart?') without any extra annotation.
    code/data: PhysioNet open access, CC BY 4.0

[6] ChestX-Det (Deepwise) is an NIH subset; Object-CXR provenance unverified (2020-2021; GitHub (paper: 'A Structure-Aware Relation Network for Thoracic Diseases Detection and Segmentation')) verified=True

    what: ChestX-Det comprises '3578 images from NIH ChestX-14' with boxes and polygon masks for 13 categories (Atelectasis, Calcification, Cardiomegaly, Consolidation, Diffuse Nodule, Effusion, Emphysema, Fibrosis, Fracture, Mass, Nodule, Pleural Thickening, Pneumothorax), Apache-2.0 repository. Object-CXR's project pages (github.com/jfhealthcare/object-CXR and jfhealthcare.github.io/object-CXR) returned 404; it appears as 'ObjectCXR' in MaCheX, but its source (independent hospital data vs NIH) could not be verified here.
    why: Any model listing ChestX-Det has seen NIH pixels, so the contamination matrix should fold ChestX-Det into the ChestX-ray14 column. ChestX-Det's polygon masks are also the quickest route to a findings-level item set (pathology removal/insertion) if the device-level pool proves too small.
    code/data: ChestX-Det: Apache-2.0 repo, annotations on GitHub; Object-CXR: none found

[7] CheXagent-2-3b and CheXOne (Stanford AIMI): CheXinstruct and CheXinstruct-v2 include ChestXray14 and Object-CXR, not RANZCR or ChestX-Det (2024 (CheXagent), 2026 (CheXOne, arXiv 2604.00493); arXiv; Hugging Face model cards StanfordAIMI/CheXagent-2-3b (MIT) and StanfordAIMI/CheXOne (cc-by-nc-4.0 on the card; CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 on the paper page)) verified=True

CheXOne (https://huggingface.co/StanfordAIMI/CheXOne; base Qwen2.5-VL-3B, 'reasoning-enabled' CXR model) uses CheXinstruct-v2 with 30 sources that again include ChestXray14 and Object-CXR and exclude RANZCR and ChestX-Det; held-out: IU-Xray and official test splits (ReXVQA, MIMIC-CXR, Chest ImaGenome, CXR-LT, MS-CXR, VinDr-CXR). The CheXagent-2-3b card itself does not enumerate datasets and points to the paper.

The gated HAI-DEF terms must be accepted before download, so budget time for that on day one.

RANZCR, Object-CXR and ChestX-Det are not named. The HF card (MIT licence) gives evaluation benchmarks only and defers to the paper.

[11] Chest X-ray editors/generators: RadEdit (NIH-trained), XReal (MIMIC-only), Cheff/MaCheX (NIH-trained) (2023 (Cheff), 2024 (RadEdit, XReal); Hugging Face (microsoft/radedit, MSRLA weights, MIT pipeline code, disclaimer gate); GitHub BioMedIA-MBZUAI/XReal; GitHub saiboxx/chexray-diffusion and saiboxx/machex (MIT)) verified=True
    url: https://huggingface.co/microsoft/radedit
    what: RadEdit card: trained on three public frontal-view sets, listed as MIMIC-CXR (377,110 image-report pairs), NIH-CXR (112,120 images with labels converted to text) and CheXpert (224,316), with '487,680 frontal view images' stated as the total; components BioViL-T text encoder, SDXL-VAE, U-Net (300 epochs); examples use 100-200 steps; research-only, must not give medical opinions. XReal README (https://github.com/BioMedIA-MBZUAI/XReal): trained on MIMIC-CXR-JPG preprocessed to 256x256 .pt tensors, ControlNet-based anatomy and pathology controllers, weights via a SharePoint link. Cheff (https://github.com/saiboxx/chexray-diffusion): cascaded latent diffusion with five released checkpoints (autoencoder, unconditional and report-conditioned LDMs, two super-resolution models) producing ~1-megapixel images; phases 2-3 train on MaCheX (https://github.com/saiboxx/machex), which aggregates CheX-ray14, CheXpert, PadChest, MIMIC-CXR, VinDr-CXR, BRAX, RSNA, OpenI, SIIM-ACR and ObjectCXR (~930k images at 1024 px, ~680k frontal).
    why: RadEdit and Cheff have seen the exact NIH pixels of the real arm, so a generated 'twin' can partially reproduce its source; run a pHash near-duplicate check of every twin against its NIH source and report it. XReal is NIH-clean but 256 px and MIMIC-styled. None of the three has a verified 6 GB fp16 footprint; the LaMa removal step on the 1060 remains the safe default and RadEdit/Cheff belong on the pod.
    code/data: microsoft/radedit (MSRLA weights, MIT code, gated); XReal weights via SharePoint (licence file present, terms not read); Cheff MIT with downloadable checkpoints

[12] General 2B-class VLMs (Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct, InternVL3.5-2B, Gemma 4 E2B-it): training exposure to ChestX-ray14 unknown (2025-2026; Hugging Face model cards; InternVL3.5 paper https://arxiv.org/html/2508.18265; Gemma 4 card https://huggingface.co/google/gemma-4-E2B-it) verified=True
    url: https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct
    what: Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct (Apache 2.0, bf16 recommended) gives no training-data list. The InternVL3.5 paper describes a ~116M-sample multimodal pretraining corpus that includes 'medical data' generically and names no medical datasets; family sizes 1B/2B/4B/8B/14B/38B plus MoE. Gemma 4 E2B-it (Apache 2.0; 2.3B effective, 5.1B total; text, image and audio input) states its pretraining data is 'web documents, code, images, audio, with a cutoff date of January 2025' and names no medical datasets.
    why: Mark all three 'unknown' in the matrix rather than 'clean': ChestX-ray14 has been on Kaggle (CC0) and Hugging Face mirrors since 2017, so web-scale crawls plausibly include it, and their cutoffs postdate the RANZCR competition and its public notebooks (which expose images with device labels). Their text-only blind run already in the plan is the right control for label leakage through text.
    code/data: HF Qwen/Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct (Apache 2.0), OpenGVLab/InternVL3_5-2B, google/gemma-4-E2B-it (Apache 2.0)

-- gaps_and_opportunities --
  * No public StudyInstanceUID-to-NIH-filename table exists in the descriptor or the two solution repositories checked; building one (dimension key from OriginalImage[Width,Height] plus perceptual hash, validated by a 200-pair visual spot check) is a half-day Kaggle-notebook job that also unlocks CheXmask masks and attribution-only pixel sourcing, and is itself a small reusable artefact for the community.
  * Nobody has published RANZCR's per-image device co-occurrence; the paper only gives label totals (ETT 8,457; NGT 8,344; CVC 32,981; Swan-Ganz 830 labels over 30,083 images). Bounds: at least 12,452 images carry no ETT/NGT/Swan-Ganz label (so the CVC-only-or-no-device pool is at least ~12k images), at least 2,898 images carry two or more CVC labels, and the mean is 7.9 images per patient. A one-image-per-patient rule therefore caps the entire study at 3,791 items and the single-device ETT/NGT/Swan-Ganz strata at a few hundred at most; these strata must be computed before the power calculation is redone.
  * The 'upscaled to original resolution before conversion to JPEG' statement means the Kaggle JPEGs carry no information beyond the 1024x1024 NIH PNGs; the whole three-source pipeline can run at 1024 px on NIH pixels, which matches CheXmask, the 6 GB GPU, and the VLMs' input resolutions.
  * Contamination becomes a measurable factor rather than a nuisance: every medical VLM except MedGemma lists ChestX-ray14 in training, none lists RANZCR, so the real-minus-edited accuracy gap (pixels seen, device labels unseen) can be reported per model as a pixel-memorisation signal, with MedGemma 4B as the claimed-clean comparator and the general 2B models as 'unknown'.
  * The polyline coverage (17,999 segmentations, images covered not stated) and the device-removal step intersect the single-device pool further; the realised pool is single-device AND polyline-available AND NIH-matched AND frontal, and that intersection has never been reported.
  * ChestX-Det's 3,578 NIH images with 13-category polygon masks offer a ready findings-level fallback (pathology removal/insertion) using the same NIH pixel source, CheXmask masks and licence story if the device-level pool is too small.

-- recommended_for_this_project --
  * Adopt the chest X-ray core with NIH pixels: accept the RANZCR rules on Kaggle, keep the JPEGs and CSVs inside Kaggle notebooks, and build the UID-to-NIH mapping there (PIL .size on each JPEG -> join Data_Entry_2017_v2020.csv on OriginalImage[Width,Height] -> confirm with pHash at 256 px -> spot-check 200 pairs). Treat the dimension-key assumption as a hypothesis to test in the first hour; fall back to pHash alone if sizes do not line up.
  * Compute the realised item count before touching the power calculation. In pandas: presence per device = any positive label among its columns; single-device images = exactly one of {ETT, NGT, CVC, Swan-Ganz} present; report image counts, unique PatientID counts and the subset with a train_annotations.csv polyline for that device, per device and for frontal-only. Then choose the sampling rule from the numbers: strict one-image-per-patient if the stratum exceeds the target N, otherwise at most two images per patient with patient as the cluster in the cluster-robust statistics already in the plan.
  * Rewrite the contamination matrix with these verified entries: ChestX-ray14 pixels seen by CheXagent-2-3b, CheXOne, NV-Reason-CXR-3B, Lingshu-7B, RadEdit and Cheff; not seen per card/tech report by MedGemma 4B / 1.5 (evaluation-only, 'OOD') and by XReal (MIMIC-CXR-JPG only); unknown for Qwen3-VL-2B, InternVL3.5-2B and Gemma 4 E2B. RANZCR device labels: listed by none. Object-CXR: CheXagent, CheXOne and Cheff/MaCheX; ChestX-Det: none listed, but fold it into the NIH column because its images are NIH images. Report the real-minus-edited gap per model as the contamination-sensitive metric.
  * Model set for the budget: MedGemma 4B-it (claimed-clean medical, gated HAI-DEF terms) plus one pixel-contaminated medical model (CheXOne or NV-Reason-CXR-3B, both Qwen2.5-VL-3B based, non-commercial licences) plus the three general 2B models; drop Lingshu-7B unless the pod budget has slack. Run all bf16 inference on the rented 3090/4090 pod as planned.
  * Generated arm: keep LaMa on the GTX 1060 for device removal (the edited arm) using rescaled polylines, and if a diffusion twin is kept, prefer XReal for an NIH-clean generator or RadEdit/Cheff with a mandatory pHash near-duplicate check of every twin against its NIH source; none of the three has a verified 6 GB fp16 footprint, so schedule them on the pod, not the 1060.
  * Release plan: publish code, the NIH filename list of the final items, your own question/answer items, CheXmask-derived masks and all statistics under attribution to NIH and Tang et al. 2021; do not redistribute train.csv/train_annotations.csv rows or RANZCR StudyInstanceUIDs. State in the report that the real arm is public NIH imagery re-annotated by RANZCR-affiliated labellers, not Australian hospital imagery.
  * Avoid: describing any medical model as 'unseen' on the real arm without the card citation above; treating Table 2 counts as image counts; planning N from the 30,083 figure; and using fp16 on Kaggle T4s for the bf16-trained medical models (already flagged in the plan).

-- cautions --
  * The shared WebSearch budget for this turn was exhausted before any search ran, so Kaggle discussions, Kaggle dataset re-uploads of the RANZCR images (and their licences), and any community-built mapping could not be checked; 'no public mapping found' covers only the descriptor and the two GitHub repositories opened.
  * Kaggle's competition rules page is client-rendered and its text was not captured; the 'academic research and education ... non-commercial' and 'not to ... redistribute' clauses quoted here are Kaggle's standard wording from memory and must be verified on the page before being cited. The post-deadline join/late-submission flow for data access is standard practice but was not verified for this competition.
  * The descriptor gives label counts, not image counts, and no per-image co-occurrence, polyline coverage, UID-generation method or train/test split; the bounds given (>=12,452 images without ETT/NGT/Swan-Ganz labels; >=2,898 images with two or more CVC labels) are arithmetic on Table 2, and the recollection that train.csv holds all 30,083 images with a separate hidden test set of roughly 3.5k is unverified.
  * The matching key (RANZCR JPEG size equals NIH OriginalImage[Width,Height]) is an inference from 'upscaled to original resolution'; it is testable in minutes but unproven.
  * MedGemma 1.5: two automated reads of the card disagreed on whether ChestX-ray14 is a training set; the verbatim read of the 'Dataset overview' and the technical report both say evaluation-only ('OOD'), but the student should confirm by eye on the card before citing it.
  * Object-CXR's project pages returned 404; its provenance (independent hospital data vs NIH) is unverified here. The RadEdit card's stated total (487,680) does not equal the sum of the per-dataset figures it lists; numbers are quoted as given.
  * No VRAM or fp16 figures were verified for RadEdit, Cheff or XReal on a 6 GB Pascal card; the CheXagent-2-3b card's architecture statement came from an automated read and is not load-bearing.
  * CheXOne's licence differs between the card (cc-by-nc-4.0) and the paper page (CC BY-NC-ND 4.0); use the card's LICENSE file as authoritative when releasing results.

-- confidence_notes -- High confidence (primary sources opened): descriptor facts and verbatim sentences (PMC and Europe PMC XML), NIH terms and metadata header (HF mirror files fetched directly), Kaggle NIH mirror CC0 JSON-LD and the RANZCR competition metadata JSON (Kaggle's own endpoint), CheXmask PhysioNet page, ChestX-Det README, the CheXagent and CheXOne dataset lists (paper HTML), NV-Reason-CXR and RadEdit cards, XReal and Cheff/MaCheX READMEs, Lingshu appendix text, Gemma 4 and Qwen3-VL cards, InternVL3.5 paper, and the two GitHub repositories (tree listings via the GitHub API; nih_files.csv downloaded and inspected). Medium confidence: MedGemma's training-set status (consistent verbatim card read and tech report, but one conflicting automated read). Low confidence / unverified: the verbatim Kaggle rules wording, Kaggle-hosted re-uploads or mappings, Object-CXR provenance, train/test sizes, and the dimension-key assumption. ######## ANGLE: FILL: Floor-effect check: can 2-4B generalist VLMs detect tubes, lines and devices at all, and which object class is safest? ########
angle: Floor-effect check for the chest-X-ray re-fit: can 2-4B generalist VLMs detect tubes, lines and devices at all, at what resolution, and which object class is safest as the primary target for a real / edited / generated triplet design. Evidence gathered from primary sources only (arXiv HTML, PMLR PDF parsed locally, HF/PhysioNet/GitHub pages); the shared WebSearch budget was exhausted at the start of this task, so coverage is limited to sources reachable by known URL.

[1] ReXVQA (Pal et al., Rajpurkar lab) - per-category results for 'Tubes and Lines' and 'Implanted Devices' (2025; arXiv 2506.04353; dataset on Hugging Face (rajpurkarlab/ReXVQA)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/html/2506.04353
    what: Largest CXR VQA benchmark (~650-700k 4-option multiple-choice questions over ReXGradient-160K images supplied at 1/4 of original DICOM resolution), five skills incl. presence and negation, evaluated on a 41,007-question private test set. Table 3 is the only published per-category device result for generalists: Tubes and Lines accuracy = MedGemma-4B 83.86, Qwen2.5-VL 65.04, Gemini 1.5 Pro 59.45, Janus-Pro-7B 58.87, Eagle2-9B 58.26, Qwen2-VL 48.78, Phi-3.5-vision (4.2B) 32.10, LLaVA-1.5 22.81; Implanted Devices = 73.14, 53.48, 54.46, 60.79, 52.64, 52.40, 43.16, 29.50 (same order). Chance is 25%; per-category question counts, the Qwen model sizes and the MCQ prompt are not stated; no yes-bias analysis. Access: gated non-commercial DUA (Harvard-administered), no PhysioNet/CITI.
    why: Direct evidence that a 4.2B generalist (Phi-3.5-vision) is at chance on tubes/lines while Qwen2.5-VL is clearly above chance and MedGemma-4B is near the ceiling: the floor is real for the weakest small models but not for the Qwen family. For weak models implanted devices score higher than tubes/lines; for strong models the order flips. ReXVQA is also an off-the-shelf, non-credentialed source of device questions for cross-checking the pilot.
    code/data: https://huggingface.co/datasets/rajpurkarlab/ReXVQA - gated, non-commercial research only, DUA click-through; images PNG at 25% of original size; GitHub repo rajpurkarlab/ReXVQA returned 404 today

[2] Lotfinia et al. - 'Vision-language models for chest radiography do not always need the image' (support devices as a yes/no finding at 224 px) (2026; arXiv 2606.17710 (v3)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/html/2606.17710
    what: Audits eight open-weight systems with yes/no finding-presence questions (prompt: 'Is support device present in this chest X-ray? Answer with a single word: Yes or No.') on MS-CXR/MIMIC-CXR/ReXErr (n=2,548) and CheXpert (n=1,380), with image-swap, occlusion, image-removal and 224-vs-512 px interventions. Support devices are one of 13 findings, 50 present / 50 absent per block. Pooled accuracy / balanced accuracy: Gemma-4-26B 68.8 / 63.4, Qwen3-VL-32B 65.0 / 61.0, MedGemma-1.5-4B 58.2 / 57.4, Mistral-Small-4-119B 46.6 / 48.9 (yes-rate 8.4), LLaVA-Med-7B 47.9 / 50.0 with a 100% yes-rate, text-only MedGemma-27B 55.3 / 53.0, RAD-DINO linear probe 72.2 / 66.0. Working resolution was 224x224; a 512 px rerun on 100 MS-CXR cases did not raise grounding (CGR 28.2 vs 34.3 for Gemma-4-26B, 30.6 vs 37.3 for MedGemma-1.5-4B). Per-finding support-device numbers exist only in Supplementary Fig. 1 (graphical, not in text).
    why: Closest precedent to the planned design (image interventions, yes/no device question, yes-bias measurement) and it must be positioned against; it shows a 4B medical model only ~7 points above a text-only control at 224 px and a 7B medical model answering Yes to everything. It did not test 2-4B generalists, did not disaggregate device classes, and used 224 px, which is the gap the student can fill.
    code/data: Paper gives prompt texts (Supplementary Note 1) and dataset composition tables; no code link found in the fetched text

[3] Rad-Phi4-Vision-CXR (Ranjit et al.) - 3.8B specialist with explicit tubes/lines presence, classification and segmentation results (2026; PMLR v297 (ML4H 2025 symposium proceedings)) verified=True
    url: https://proceedings.mlr.press/v297/ranjit26a.html
    what: Phi-4-mini-instruct (3.8B) + SigLIP2-so400M-patch14-224 encoder fine-tuned on CXR (i.e., a 224 px encoder), LoRA-tuned on ~700k Chest ImaGenome tubes/lines presence QAs. Reported: Tubes and Lines Presence F1 0.91 [0.90, 0.91] and Tubes and Lines Classification F1 87.54 on 11,145 Chest ImaGenome test questions (no baseline could be run); RANZCR segmentation DICE ETT 0.800, CVC 0.791, NGT 0.782; ReXVQA overall 0.969 vs MedGemma 0.835 and CheXagent 0.806. Question templates: image-level 'Is there any presence of tubes, lines, or devices visible in the chest X-ray?' and tube-type 'Is a <> visible in this chest X-ray?'.
    why: Specialist ceiling: with enough supervision, tube/line presence is learnable to F1 ~0.9 even from a 224 px encoder, so the task is not intrinsically invisible at low resolution; the floor risk is about zero-shot generalists, not the pixels. Its templates are a ready-made, published wording for the pilot's yes/no questions, and its per-tube DICE order (ETT easiest, NGT hardest) is weak evidence for class ranking.
    code/data: PDF: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mlresearch/v297/main/assets/ranjit26a/ranjit26a.pdf ; no model-weight or code link found in the paper text (treat weights as unavailable)

[4] MedGemma technical report Table 16 + MedSigLIP-448 / MedGemma-4B model cards - zero-shot 'Support Devices' AUC and the 896 px / 256-token budget (2025; arXiv 2507.05201; Hugging Face google/medsiglip-448, google/medgemma-4b-it, google/medgemma-1.5-4b-it) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/html/2507.05201
    what: MedSigLIP (448x448) zero-shot AUC on 13 CheXpert-test findings (n=518): Support Devices 0.852 vs 0.894 for the 1280x1280 HAI-DEF CXR Foundation model; average 0.844 vs 0.824 (support devices is one of only four labels where the higher-resolution model wins). MedGemma 4B and 1.5-4B encode each image at 896x896 into 256 tokens; their published CXR classification covers only 5 (MIMIC/CheXpert) or 3 (CXR14) conditions, no device label (MedGemma 4B macro-F1 88.9 MIMIC top-5, 48.1 CheXpert, 50.1 CXR14; Gemma 3 4B 81.2 / 32.6 / 32.0). Access: HAI-DEF terms, HF gated click-through.
    why: Gives a free, open, calibrated device-presence signal (MedSigLIP zero-shot, AUC 0.85) usable both as a pilot sanity reference and as the 'grounding' expert for the mitigation family, and shows resolution matters more for devices than for most pathologies. MedGemma-4B is the obvious open 4B positive-control model for the pilot (bf16 only; 256 tokens per image regardless of input size).
    code/data: https://huggingface.co/google/medsiglip-448 and https://huggingface.co/google/medgemma-4b-it - gated under Health AI Developer Foundations terms (login + acceptance), research use

[5] MIMIC-CXR-VQA (EHRXQA, Bae et al.) + Chest ImaGenome - device and tubes/lines attribute labels and presence-question baselines (2023/2024; NeurIPS 2023 Datasets & Benchmarks; PhysioNet mimic-ext-mimic-cxr-vqa v1.0.0 (2024) and chest-imagenome v1.0.0) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/html/2310.18652
    what: 377,391 QA triples over 142,797 MIMIC-CXR images; 7 content types (presence 37.7% of train), 56.1% yes/no 'verify' questions; attributes come from Chest ImaGenome's 68 attributes in 5 categories incl. 'device' and 'tubes/lines' (labels listed in Table B2 include endotracheal tube, enteric tube, ij line, picc, chest tube, swan-ganz catheter, cardiac pacer and wires, prosthetic valve). Baseline presence accuracy (validation): M3AE 81.0, MedViLL 76.6, PubMedCLIP 70.2; overall test accuracy 69.2 for M3AE. Chest ImaGenome supplies per-image tube/line/device presence for 242,072 frontal images. No generalist VLM results.
    why: Only source with per-image, per-device presence/absence labels at scale (needed to pick true device-negative images and to write class-specific questions), but it is PhysioNet-credentialed (CITI course + DUA + credentialing wait), which may not fit a 4-6 week timeline; the content-type template vocabulary is reusable without the data.
    code/data: https://physionet.org/content/mimic-ext-mimic-cxr-vqa/1.0.0/ and https://physionet.org/content/chest-imagenome/1.0.0/ - credentialed access, CITI training, PhysioNet Credentialed Health Data License 1.5.0; code https://github.com/baeseongsu/mimic-cxr-vqa (MIT)

[6] CCD toolkit (Clinical Contrastive Decoding) - 13-class zero-shot support-device model built on MedSigLIP (2025/2026; GitHub (MIT); paper arXiv 2509.23379, accepted ACL 2026) verified=True
    url: https://github.com/X-iZhang/CCD
    what: ccd_utils.py defines a zero-shot device detector over google/medsiglip-448 with positive/negative prompt pairs ('a chest x-ray with/without a central venous catheter'), default threshold 0.5, classes ET_tube, Tracheostomy_tube, NG_tube, OG_tube, Feeding_tube, Enteric_tube, CVC, Central_line, PICC_line, Chest_tube, Pigtail_catheter, Pacemaker, ICD, plus No_devices. The README reports no accuracy for it, and the paper's evaluated expert is a DenseNet CheXpert-14 pathology classifier (MAIRA-2 RadGraph-F1 16.23 -> 19.01; no device-specific analysis). Supports Libra, MAIRA-2, LLaVA-Med, LLaVA-Rad backbones.
    why: Ready-made, training-free 'grounding signal' for the umbrella's grounding-based mitigation, with exactly the device vocabulary the student needs; because it is unvalidated, measuring its AUC on RANZCR labels is a cheap, publishable by-product and a necessary step before using it to steer decoding.
    code/data: https://github.com/X-iZhang/CCD (MIT); device prompts in ccd/ccd_utils.py; requires gated google/medsiglip-448

[7] RANZCR CLiP - Catheter and Line Position dataset (Tang et al.) (2021; Scientific Data 8:285; Kaggle competition ranzcr-clip-catheter-line-classification) verified=True
    url: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41597-021-01066-8
    what: 30,083 chest radiographs from 3,791 patients drawn from NIH ChestX-ray14 (openly downloadable 1024 px PNGs), with 50,612 image-level labels (ETT normal/borderline/abnormal; NGT normal/borderline/abnormal/incompletely imaged; CVC normal/borderline/abnormal; Swan-Ganz) and 17,999 manually drawn catheter path annotations. The paper notes NGT side holes are often not visible on the radiograph. Per-class counts (Table 2) could not be extracted from the fetched page; no baseline AUC per class is given in the paper.
    why: The only non-credentialed source of real images with per-catheter labels and line masks: it enables class-specific yes/no questions (ETT vs CVC), true negatives within the same image (e.g., CVC present but no ETT), and mask-guided LaMa removal for the edited arm, all without PhysioNet. Data use is governed by the Kaggle competition rules (registration + licence acceptance), which were not readable here.
    code/data: https://www.kaggle.com/competitions/ranzcr-clip-catheter-line-classification/data (Kaggle login; competition data licence - terms not verified); images also in NIH ChestX-ray14

[8] Object-CXR - foreign objects on chest X-rays (JF Healthcare, MIDL 2020 challenge) (2020; MIDL 2020 challenge) verified=True
    url: https://2020.midl.io/challenges.html
    what: 5,000 frontal CXRs with manually annotated foreign objects (buttons, jewellery and similar external/internal objects; the page notes buttons resemble nodules) and 5,000 without; evaluated by classification AUC and localization FROC. The original challenge/data site (jfhealthcare.github.io/object-CXR) and the GitHub repo both returned 404 today; baseline numbers and current download terms could not be verified.
    why: Foreign objects are the most conspicuous candidate object class (high contrast, compact), so they are the safest fallback if tubes/lines floor out, and the closest CXR analogue of the general-domain 'removed object' design; but data availability is currently uncertain, so it cannot be the primary plan without a mirror.
    code/data: Challenge site https://jfhealthcare.github.io/object-CXR/ (404 on 2026-10-12); https://github.com/jfhealthcare/object-CXR (404); licence unverified

[9] RAD-DINO (Perez-Garcia et al.) - resolution ablation and chest-tube detection (2024/2025; arXiv 2401.10815 (Nature Machine Intelligence 2025)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/html/2401.10815
    what: Self-supervised CXR encoder at 518x518 (14 px patches). Linear-probe chest-tube detection on CANDID-PTX reaches AUPRC 90.8 +/- 1.6; Appendix C / Fig. C.1 sweeps 224-512 px and states performance 'diminishes at lower resolutions due to the ambiguity and loss of detail', singling out 'small structures such as chest tubes'; the 224 px model still beats image-text contrastive baselines. Exact per-resolution numbers are only plotted.
    why: Best available evidence that thin devices lose signal below ~512 px even for a strong CXR encoder, supporting a resolution sweep (448 / 896 / 1024) as a planned factor and warning that any 224-448 px pipeline is a lower bound for CVC/PICC questions.
    code/data: Encoder weights: microsoft/rad-dino on Hugging Face (MSRLA licence, per model card; not re-verified today)

[10] Sabottke & Spieler - The Effect of Image Resolution on Deep Learning in Radiography (2020; Radiology: Artificial Intelligence 2(1):e190015) verified=True
    url: https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8017385/
    what: CNNs trained on NIH ChestX-ray14 at 32-600 px: most labels peak between 256 and 448 px; nodules and emphysema need the highest resolution (nodule AUC 0.689 at 64 px vs 0.854 at 320 px). Pneumothorax was deliberately excluded because 'many of the radiographs with the pneumothorax label already had chest tubes in place', i.e., the tube was a conspicuous confounder. Lines, tubes and devices were not studied as labels.
    why: Canonical resolution reference to cite; its chest-tube confound remark is indirect evidence that large-bore tubes are visually salient at <=320 px, while the absence of any device-by-resolution curve is a gap the student's resolution sweep can fill for small VLMs.
    code/data: none found

[11] CheXpert (Irvin et al.) label prevalence - 'Support Devices' positive in 56.4% of training studies; MIMIC-CXR-JPG analogue (2019; AAAI 2019; MIMIC-CXR-JPG arXiv 1901.07042) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.07031
    what: CheXpert Table 1: Support Devices positive 105,831 (56.4%), uncertain 898 (0.48%), negative 80,912 (43.12%); labeler F1 for support-device mention 0.975. In MIMIC-CXR-JPG (Table 2, layout-scrambled in extraction; verify) support devices are positive in roughly 29% of the 222,750 training studies with only ~120 explicit negatives, because reports almost never state that no lines are present. CheXpert images are distributed at 320 px (small) or full resolution (large).
    why: Devices are the most prevalent label in both big public CXR corpora, so real positives are plentiful, but true negatives cannot be selected from report labels (no explicit negation); negatives must come from per-image annotations (RANZCR, Chest ImaGenome) or manual review, which shapes the item-selection rule and the power calculation.
    code/data: CheXpert: https://stanfordmlgroup.github.io/competitions/chexpert/ (registration, research use); MIMIC-CXR-JPG: PhysioNet credentialed

[12] Visual-token budgets of the three target models (Qwen3-VL-2B, InternVL3.5-2B, Gemma 4 E2B) - what a 2-3 px line becomes at the token level (2025/2026; GitHub README; Hugging Face model cards OpenGVLab/InternVL3_5-2B and google/gemma-4-E2B-it) verified=True
    url: https://github.com/QwenLM/Qwen3-VL
    what: Qwen3-VL uses 16 px patches with 2x2 merge, i.e., one token per 32x32 px, user-settable via min/max_pixels (README suggests 256-1280 tokens): a 1024 px CXR = 1024 tokens, 512 px = 256 tokens. InternVL3.5-2B (InternViT-300M + Qwen3 2B) tiles at 448 px, up to 12 tiles, 256 tokens per tile after pixel shuffle (28 px per token). Gemma 4 E2B-it (2.3B effective, 5.1B with embeddings, Apache-2.0, released 2026-07-02) exposes fixed visual-token budgets of 70/140/280/560/1120. Computed geometry (not published): on a 1024 px NIH image (~0.4 mm/px for a ~40 cm field) a 7 Fr CVC (~2.3 mm) is ~5-6 px wide, a PICC ~3-4 px, an NGT ~12 px, an ETT ~25 px and a pacemaker generator ~120 px; at 512 px halve these, at 224 px a CVC/PICC is ~1 px.
    why: Sets the pilot's resolution/token settings and explains why CVC/PICC are the floor-risk class (sub-token-width structures at 512 px and below) whereas ETT, NGT, chest tubes and pacemakers span multiple patches even at 448 px; also shows that MedGemma's fixed 256 tokens (56 px per token at 896) is coarser than Qwen3-VL at 1024 px.
    code/data: https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct (Apache-2.0); https://huggingface.co/OpenGVLab/InternVL3_5-2B (Apache-2.0); https://huggingface.co/google/gemma-4-E2B-it (Apache-2.0)

-- gaps_and_opportunities --
  * No published number exists for Qwen3-VL-2B, InternVL3.5-2B or Gemma 4 E2B on any tube/line/device question; the nearest evidence is Qwen2.5-VL (size unstated) at 65% and Phi-3.5-vision (4.2B) at 32% on 4-option ReXVQA tubes/lines, and Qwen3-VL-32B / Gemma-4-26B at ~61-63% balanced accuracy on pooled yes/no findings at 224 px. The Day-1 pilot will produce the first such numbers, which is itself a reportable contribution.
  * No study disaggregates generalist-VLM device detection by class (ETT vs NGT vs CVC/PICC vs pacemaker vs foreign object) or by input resolution; RAD-DINO's chest-tube curve and the MedSigLIP (448) vs CXR Foundation (1280) support-device gap are the only indirect evidence. A per-class x resolution table for three 2B models is cheap (~2,000 inferences) and fills a clear hole.
  * Lotfinia et al. 2026 is the direct precedent for image-intervention audits with a support-device question, but at 224 px, on >=4B models, with one coarse 'support device' label and no per-class numbers in the text; positioning must state these three differences explicitly.
  * The CCD device detector (13 MedSigLIP zero-shot classes) has no published accuracy; validating it on RANZCR labels and then using it as the grounding signal for contrastive decoding is a self-contained mitigation experiment that matches the umbrella's 'grounding and retrieval-based techniques' wording.
  * Report-derived labels (CheXpert/MIMIC 'Support Devices') have essentially no explicit negatives, so device-absent real images must come from per-image annotations; RANZCR's within-image structure (e.g., CVC present, ETT absent) gives natural class-specific negatives without credentialing.
  * Medical-Diff-VQA contains no device entities (its presence questions are pathology-only per the paper text), ReXVQA mixes devices into MCQ with unknown per-category n, and Object-CXR's site is down: there is no clean, public, yes/no device-presence benchmark for small VLMs, which the student's item set could become if released with the paper.
  * A text-only blind run (as in Lotfinia and in the existing plan) has never been reported per device class; device priors in language models (e.g., 'ICU-looking AP film implies lines') are an unexamined hallucination driver that the blind arm can quantify.

-- recommended_for_this_project --
  * Go/no-go rule for the Day-1 pilot: for each of the three target models, run 100 device-positive and 100 device-negative real NIH/RANZCR images per candidate class (pacemaker/ICD, ETT, CVC) at 448, 896 and 1024 px with the yes/no wording 'Is a <device> visible in this chest X-ray? Answer with a single word: Yes or No.' (Lotfinia/Rad-Phi4 templates). GO for a model-class pair if balanced accuracy >= 0.70, hit rate on real positives >= 0.70 and yes-rate within 0.25-0.75 at the best resolution (Wilson 95% CI on n=200 is about +/-0.07, so 0.70 excludes chance); CONDITIONAL at 0.60-0.70 (raise resolution or switch to an easier class); NO-GO below 0.60 or if the yes-rate is <0.10 or >0.90 (constant-answer policy, as LLaVA-Med-7B and Mistral-Small-4 showed). Include MedGemma-4B-it as a positive control (expected near ceiling from ReXVQA) and MedSigLIP zero-shot (AUC 0.85 on support devices) as the classifier reference; if even MedGemma fails on a class, blame the data, not the model.
  * Primary object class ranking (safest first): 1) pacemaker/ICD generator (~5 cm, highest contrast, labelled as 'cardiac pacer and wires' in Chest ImaGenome, detectable by the CCD/MedSigLIP prompts, compact enough for clean LaMa removal); 2) endotracheal tube (~10 mm, radiopaque stripe, midline, RANZCR labels and masks, highest Rad-Phi4 DICE); 3) external foreign objects (necklaces, buttons; easiest to see and to inpaint but Object-CXR access is currently unverified); 4) NGT; 5) CVC/PICC last (1-2 px at 512 px; keep only as the 'hard' class for the resolution analysis, never as the primary class). Pre-register the primary class after the pilot, not before.
  * Data path that avoids PhysioNet credentialing: NIH ChestX-ray14 images (open download) + RANZCR CLiP labels and 17,999 catheter masks from Kaggle for ETT/NGT/CVC, plus MedSigLIP/CCD prompts and a quick manual audit to select pacemaker positives/negatives; ReXVQA (gated DUA, no CITI) as an external cross-check. Start Chest ImaGenome/MIMIC credentialing on Monday anyway, but do not put it on the critical path.
  * Resolution is a design factor, not a nuisance: set Qwen3-VL-2B max_pixels to >=1024 tokens (1024x1024 input), InternVL3.5-2B to >=6 tiles, Gemma 4 E2B to the 1120-token budget, and report every device number with its token budget; MedGemma-4B is fixed at 256 tokens (896 px), so note that it is architecturally coarser for thin lines.
  * Positioning: cite Lotfinia 2026 as the direct precedent (image swap/removal with a support-device question) and state the three differences (2B generalists, per-device class, matched real/edited/generated triplets with a resolution sweep and abstention scoring); cite ReXVQA for the generalist baseline and Rad-Phi4-Vision-CXR for the specialist ceiling (F1 0.91 at 224 px after fine-tuning); cite RAD-DINO and Sabottke & Spieler for resolution; cite CheXpert/MIMIC-CXR-JPG for the missing-negatives problem.
  * Mitigation family: use the CCD 13-class MedSigLIP zero-shot device scores as the grounding signal (validate AUC on RANZCR first), and compare contrastive decoding steered by that signal against the plan's existing VCD/prior-contrast arms; this satisfies the umbrella's grounding requirement without training and reuses MIT-licensed code.
  * Avoid: Phi-3.5-vision-class models (at chance on tubes/lines), 224-448 px inputs for any line class, Medical-Diff-VQA (no devices), fp16 on T4 for MedGemma (bf16 model), and counting a model as 'detecting devices' from report-label negatives (there are almost none).
  * Cut list if the pilot floors on tubes: fall back to pacemaker + external foreign objects as the two primary classes, keep ETT as secondary, and drop CVC entirely; the triplet design, abstention scoring and statistics carry over unchanged.

-- cautions --
  * WebSearch quota was exhausted before any search ran; every item came from URLs fetched directly, so 2026 papers I do not already know (e.g., a dedicated lines-and-tubes VLM benchmark) may have been missed. The user can re-run a search pass in a follow-up turn.
  * ReXVQA Table 3 values were cross-checked between the arXiv HTML and a pdftotext extraction whose row labels were shifted by one line; the numbers reported here follow the HTML reading (MedGemma 83.86 tubes/lines, 73.14 implanted devices). Qwen2-VL and Qwen2.5-VL sizes are not stated in the paper or appendix; the appendix finer-grained rows (e.g., 'Other (Artifacts and External Foreign Bodies)' near 100% for most models) have uncertain column alignment and likely tiny n, so do not quote them.
  * Lotfinia et al.'s per-finding support-device results are only in supplementary figures (Fig. 1g); pooled numbers quoted here are from Table 1 and the text. Their Table 1 extraction was layout-scrambled; Gemma-4-26B 68.8/63.4, Qwen3-VL-32B 65.0/61.0, Mistral 46.6/48.9, MedGemma-1.5-4B 58.2/57.4 were reconciled between the HTML summary and the PDF text.
  * Rad-Phi4-Vision-CXR weights and code were not found in the paper text; treat the model as unavailable. Its tubes/lines F1 is on in-distribution Chest ImaGenome test questions with no baseline, so it is a ceiling indicator, not a comparison point.
  * MIMIC-CXR-JPG 'Support Devices' counts (about 65.6k positive / 120 negative of 222,750 studies) were read from a layout-scrambled PDF table and must be verified against Table 2 of arXiv 1901.07042; CheXpert's 105,831 (56.4%) was read directly and is reliable.
  * RANZCR CLiP per-class counts, Kaggle data-licence terms and the NIH 1024 px resolution were not verified in this pass; Object-CXR's challenge site and GitHub repo are down (404) and its baseline AUC/FROC and licence are unverified; CANDID-PTX (figshare) returned 403 so its access terms are unverified.
  * The physical-size-to-pixel conversions (CVC ~5-6 px at 1024 px, ~1 px at 224 px, etc.) are my own computations from typical catheter diameters and a ~40 cm field of view, not published measurements; use them for planning, not as citable facts.
  * MedSigLIP's support-device AUC (0.852) is on CheXpert-test labeler labels (n=518), not radiologist reads, and the CCD device prompts have no published validation; gated HAI-DEF terms apply to MedSigLIP and MedGemma (login and acceptance, research use).

-- confidence_notes -- High confidence in the ReXVQA per-category numbers, Lotfinia's pooled metrics and prompt wording, Rad-Phi4-Vision's tubes/lines F1 and architecture, MedSigLIP Table 16, the CCD class list, the MIMIC-CXR-VQA/Chest ImaGenome label structure and access terms, and the Qwen3-VL/InternVL3.5/Gemma 4 token budgets (all read from primary pages or locally parsed PDFs). Moderate confidence in the class-difficulty ranking: it is inferred from MCQ category scores (unknown n), specialist DICE ordering, resolution evidence and geometry rather than from any direct per-class generalist study. Low confidence in the MIMIC-CXR-JPG support-device counts, RANZCR per-class counts, Object-CXR availability and CANDID-PTX access. The go/no-go thresholds are my proposal calibrated to n=200 per class, not published criteria.


######## ANGLE: FILL: Edited-arm validity on radiographs: generic inpainters on grayscale CXRs, a leak-free removal verifier, and a reader-audit path ########
angle: Edited-arm validity on chest radiographs: what is actually documented for generic vs CXR-trained inpainters, which removal verifiers are independent of RANZCR CLiP, and the smallest reader-audit protocol with a realistic route to a reader for a KFUPM/JRCAI undergraduate (GTX 1060, Kaggle, <$40).

[1] Canonical CXR inpainting precedents: Sogancioglu et al. 2018 (deep inpainting + 2AFC observer study) and Le et al. 2020 (foreign-object removal pipeline) (2018 / 2020; arXiv 1809.01471 (cs.GR); arXiv 2008.06828) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/1809.01471
