    what: Sogancioglu, Hu, Belli and van Ginneken trained three deep inpainters (context encoders, semantic inpainting, contextual attention) on 1.2M 128x128 patches from 60K healthy CXRs to fill a central 64x64 hole, and ran a 2AFC observer study in which 'an experienced human observer performs poorly in detecting inpainted regions' for the contextual-attention model. Le et al. (https://arxiv.org/abs/2008.06828) remove foreign objects from smartphone-photographed CheXphoto CXRs with YOLOv4 detection, an EfficientNet-B0 U-Net segmenter and classical Fast-Marching inpainting (not a deep inpainter); they report 85% of images 'completely inpainted', mAP@0.5 of 61% and 3.3-3.8% downstream accuracy gains, with no human artefact evaluation and the admission that 'there is an acknowledged lack of quantitative metrics for image inpainting quality'.
    why: These are the only CXR-specific removal precedents with any artefact evidence, and neither covers LaMa or SD-inpainting. Sogancioglu's 2AFC design (edited vs untouched crops, forced choice) is the cheapest credible detectability test the student can run on himself and a second rater before any radiologist sees an image.
    code/data: none found for either paper (no code links on the arXiv pages)

[2] MaskMedPaint (ML4H 2024): SD 1.5 fine-tuning with LaMa background removal on chest X-rays (2024; Machine Learning for Health (ML4H) 2024) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.10686
    what: Jin, Gerych and Ghassemi fine-tune runwayml/stable-diffusion-v1-5 to inpaint everything outside a segmented region of interest on MIMIC-CXR and NIH ChestX-ray14 (Atelectasis, Cardiomegaly, Edema, Pneumothorax, No Finding), and use LaMa to erase the background of target-domain images before fine-tuning. Validity is judged only by classifier accuracy/AUROC and a 'ground-truth' spurious-feature classifier; there is no FID, no reader study, and the authors only note qualitative differences in 'radiology markers and general image contrast'. Code lives at https://github.com/QixuanJin99/generative_validation (scripts visible; README/licence not readable from the listing).
    why: This is the one documented use of LaMa on grayscale CXRs, and it is used as a crude background eraser, not as a device remover; nobody has measured LaMa artefact detectability on radiographs. It also shows SD 1.5 (which fits the GTX 1060) can be adapted to CXRs, but only with fine-tuning the student cannot afford.
    code/data: https://github.com/QixuanJin99/generative_validation (licence not visible from listing; 'available upon publication' per paper)

[3] Latent Drifting in Diffusion Models for Counterfactual Medical Image Synthesis (CVPR 2025 highlight) (2025; CVPR 2025 (highlight)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.20651
    what: Yeganeh et al. fine-tune Stable Diffusion v1.4 with a latent-drift term to generate counterfactual CXRs (e.g., 'Cardiomegaly and Consolidation -> No finding', 'No finding -> Pleural Effusion', sex changes) on 800 CheXpert samples, evaluated with FID/KID, downstream AUC, SSIM, LPIPS and PSNR; a 'user study' is relegated to the supplement and no radiologist protocol is given in the main text. The project page (https://latentdrifting.github.io/) says 'Code (Coming Soon)'.
    why: Shows that whole-image disease removal on CXRs is validated almost entirely by classifier and distribution metrics, which is exactly the circularity the edited arm must avoid; with no code or weights it cannot be a tool for the project, only a citation for the gap.
    code/data: none released (project page: 'Code (Coming Soon)')

[4] MedDiME: latent classifier-guided counterfactuals with adaptive masking, including pacemaker removal on CheXpert (2026; arXiv 2609.15647 (Sept 2026)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15647
    what: Zeng et al. run classifier-guided counterfactual generation in the 4x64x64 latent space of a frozen SDXL VAE (512x512 images, T=1000 steps, start at t=400) with a gradient-driven adaptive mask; the CXR task is explicitly restricted to pacemaker removal on CheXpert with a DenseNet121 classifier, reaching a 100% flip rate. Validity is measured only by flip rate, mean absolute difference, FID, L1 and SimSiam similarity; no reader study; experiments on an NVIDIA A100 80GB; the paper claims 13x lower peak memory and 40x faster inference than DiME but gives no absolute GB or seconds, and no code URL.
    why: It is the closest 2026 precedent for device removal on CXRs and confirms that the field validates removal by classifier flip, not by radiologists. Not runnable for the project (no weights, A100-class reference hardware), but it is the paper to cite when arguing that a reader-audited removal arm is new.
    code/data: none found (no repository or weights mentioned in the HTML full text)

[5] Cheff (cascaded latent diffusion for CXR synthesis) with a 256-px inpainting notebook, but weights currently 404 (2023; PAKDD 2023 (arXiv 2303.11224)) verified=True
    url: https://github.com/saiboxx/chexray-diffusion
    what: Weber et al. train an f4 autoencoder (3x64x64 latents for 256-px images), a 256-px latent diffusion model and a pixel-space super-resolution stage to 1024 px on MaCheX (650K+ CXRs; 8x A100 40GB for training). The paper's Fig. 5 is literally 'Removal of support devices via image inpainting' and the repo ships notebooks/02_cheff_inpainting.ipynb, which loads only cheff_autoencoder.pt and cheff_diff_uncond.pt, expects 256x256 input, masks a 64x64 latent region and runs DDIM with 100 steps on CUDA (no SR stage, no RePaint resampling). All five LRZ Sync+Share weight links return HTTP 404 (confirmed today for the autoencoder and unconditional LDM; GitHub issue #13 open since Sept 11, 2026 with no maintainer reply), and no Hugging Face mirror exists.
    why: Cheff was the only CXR-trained inpainter that would plausibly fit 6 GB fp32 (256-px latent model, no 1024-px stage needed for inpainting), so the 'Cheff 256-px LDM inpainting' assumption in the plan is correct in design but dead in practice unless the author re-hosts the weights; do not schedule it on the critical path.
    code/data: https://github.com/saiboxx/chexray-diffusion (MIT); weights: syncandshare.lrz.de links all 404 as of 2026-10-12

[6] RadEdit (ECCV 2024): mask-constrained CXR editing with keep masks; gated weights on Hugging Face (2024; ECCV 2024 (arXiv 2312.12865)) verified=True
    url: https://huggingface.co/microsoft/radedit
    what: A 512x512 latent diffusion editor (U-Net with base channels 128, multipliers 1-2-4-6-8; BioViL-T text encoder; SDXL-VAE) trained on 487,680 frontal CXRs from MIMIC-CXR, NIH and CheXpert; edit_mask marks where to change and keep_mask reverts any change elsewhere ('any changes made within the region of the keep mask are reverted'). Validation in the paper is classifier-based (BioViL-T editing score >= 0.2, weak/strong predictor agreement), with no radiologist review; the authors state they cannot 'completely guarantee that unwanted changes will not occur' and that out-of-distribution edits drift toward the training distribution. In the paper devices are preserved rather than removed (pneumothorax removal uses the chest-drain mask as the keep mask). Repo is gated (login + clinical-use disclaimer); unet/diffusion_pytorch_model.safetensors is 1.76 GB; U-Net under MSRLA, pipeline MIT.
    why: The only CXR-trained masked editor with downloadable weights. The Spatially Gated Diffusion paper (item 11) independently scored RadEdit at 82.1% (197/240) joint validity under a two-reader protocol, and the parent's cxr-grounding precedent reports it 'could not remove findings convincingly', so device removal must be piloted and measured, not assumed. The 1.76 GB U-Net plus VAE and text encoder is borderline for 6 GB fp32 at 512 px (estimate); run it on the RunPod 3090/4090 pod.
    code/data: https://huggingface.co/microsoft/radedit (gated; U-Net MSRLA, pipeline MIT); paper https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.12865

[7] XReal: anatomy- and pathology-controlled CXR generation (MBZUAI), 256 px, generation-only (2024; arXiv 2403.09240 (v2 Oct 2024)) verified=True
    url: https://github.com/BioMedIA-MBZUAI/XReal
    what: Hashmi et al. add an Anatomy Controller and a Pathology Controller (ControlNet-style spatial masks) to a pre-trained text-to-image diffusion model without fine-tuning it, generating 256x256 CXRs with a claimed 13%/10% anatomy/pathology realism gain 'based on expert radiologist evaluation'. The README releases code, configs and weights via a SharePoint link; it supports controlled generation of new images, not editing of an existing radiograph; README states no licence (the arXiv listing carries CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 for the paper).
    why: Useful only for a 'generated twin' arm (layout-matched synthetic CXR from an anatomy mask), the radiograph analogue of the plan's SD 1.5 + ControlNet twin; it cannot serve as the remover. 256 px is also below the resolution at which tubes and lines are legible, so a generated-twin arm on CXRs would need a resolution caveat.
    code/data: https://github.com/BioMedIA-MBZUAI/XReal (weights on SharePoint; code licence unspecified; paper CC BY-NC-ND 4.0)

[8] ianpan/cxr-lines-and-tubes: the proposed verification oracle is trained on RANZCR CLiP plus NIH pseudo-labels, with no fold file (2025; Hugging Face model release (MIT code, research-only weights)) verified=True
    url: https://huggingface.co/ianpan/cxr-lines-and-tubes
    what: Detects endotracheal tubes, nasogastric tubes, central venous catheters and Swan-Ganz catheters plus carina: a five-fold EfficientNet-B1 multilabel classifier (512 px; mean fold AUROC 0.9684), U-Net segmenters (1024 px), a DeepLabV3+ carina model and a FastViT presence screener (384 px). Training uses RANZCR CLiP (30,083 radiographs, 9,095 device-annotated) and the presence classifier additionally uses 31,448 NIH ChestX-ray14 radiographs with model-derived labels. The repo (728 MB) contains weights, runtime code and manifests but no CSV of fold assignments, so out-of-fold scoring on RANZCR is impossible.
    why: Confirms the leak: presence scores on RANZCR items are in-sample for 4 of 5 fold models and cannot be made out-of-fold. The model remains fine as a mask generator and as a selector, but removal verification must come from models whose training excludes the source images (item 9-10), which argues for sourcing the edited arm from CheXpert or MIMIC-CXR rather than RANZCR or NIH.
    code/data: https://huggingface.co/ianpan/cxr-lines-and-tubes (code MIT; weights research-only, non-commercial)

[9] CheXzero (MIMIC-CXR-only CLIP) as an independent 'Support Devices' verifier, and the negative finding that torchxrayvision has no device head (2022 (CheXzero); torchxrayvision current; Nature Biomedical Engineering 2022 (CheXzero); mlmed.org/torchxrayvision) verified=True
    url: https://github.com/rajpurkarlab/CheXzero
    what: CheXzero is trained exclusively on MIMIC-CXR image-report pairs; its zero_shot.py evaluates the 14 CheXpert labels including 'Support Devices' with the template pair ('{}', 'no {}') at 320x320, weights are on Google Drive and the licence is MIT (per-label Support-Devices AUC not verified this session; the Nature page is paywalled). torchxrayvision's pretrained DenseNet/ResNet heads output 18 pathologies with no 'Support Devices', its CheXpert baseline (xrv.baseline_models.chexpert) outputs only five labels, and the only device-adjacent asset is the ChestX-Det PSPNet that segments 14 anatomical structures, none of them devices.
    why: Corrects the plan: torchxrayvision cannot verify removal. CheXzero can, and because it never saw CheXpert images it is out-of-sample on a CheXpert-sourced edited arm (but in-sample on MIMIC-CXR). Use its 'support devices' vs 'no support devices' logit difference, calibrated on real device-negative images, as one of two independent removal checks.
    code/data: https://github.com/rajpurkarlab/CheXzero (MIT; weights via Google Drive link in README); https://github.com/mlmed/torchxrayvision

[10] MedSigLIP zero-shot device prompts and CheXagent-2-3b device tasks as second independent verifiers (2025 (MedSigLIP); 2024-2025 (CheXagent); Google HAI-DEF model card; Stanford AIMI model card / arXiv 2401.12208) verified=True
    url: https://huggingface.co/google/medsiglip-448
    what: MedSigLIP (400M vision + 400M text, 448 px) is trained on MIMIC-CXR, Slake-VQA, PMC-OA and proprietary radiology data (no RANZCR listed) and reports a zero-shot AUC of 0.852 for 'Support Devices' (mean 0.844 over 13 findings) on 518 ELIXR CXR evaluation examples; access is gated by login and the Health AI Developer Foundations terms, which permit research and commercial use with redistribution obligations. CheXagent-2-3b (MIT, 3B params, fp32 tensors, trust_remote_code) lists 'chest_tube_detection' and 'foreign_objects_detection' among its demo tasks; the CheXagent paper's CheXinstruct lists 32 sources including Object-CXR but not RANZCR CLiP or CANDID-PTX, and does not attribute the chest-tube task to a source.
    why: MedSigLIP is the cheapest RANZCR-independent presence check with a published device AUC; CheXagent adds a tube-specific detector that is also RANZCR-free. Agreement between MedSigLIP and CheXzero on 'device absent' after editing, plus a human read, is the leak-free verifier cascade the plan lacks. Treat CheXagent-2's training provenance as unverified beyond the v1 paper.
    code/data: https://huggingface.co/google/medsiglip-448 (gated, HAI-DEF terms); https://huggingface.co/StanfordAIMI/CheXagent-2-3b (MIT); https://github.com/Stanford-AIMI/CheXagent

[11] Reader-study protocols in 2025-2026 CXR editing papers: RoentMod, Spatially Gated Diffusion, RadiT (2025-2026; arXiv 2509.08640 (RoentMod); arXiv 2610.00805 (SGD); arXiv 2606.19460 (RadiT)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08640
    what: RoentMod (RoentGen weights dropped into SD image-to-image, no training; guidance 4, strength 0.4; <2 s/image on an RTX 4090 and <30 s on CPU) was audited by one radiology resident (4 years) and one board-certified radiologist (8 years), each reading 400 modified scans blinded to the prompt, scoring realism (745/800, 93%), prompted pathology present (70-99% by condition) and off-target findings (20/800); the authors concede 'we did not include real images in the reader studies as a negative control'; code and models 'will be available upon publication' and RoentGen weights are requested via a Google Form. Spatially Gated Diffusion (SDXL base, 1024 px; MIMIC-derived 2,400 requests; no code) used 'two readers blinded to method identity' of unstated qualification on 240 held-out requests scoring target completion, unintended non-target change, structural/artefact quality and joint validity, with adjudication and pre-adjudication Cohen's kappa 0.82; it reports 93.3% joint validity for itself, 82.1% for RadEdit, and 'device retention 476/480' (devices are kept, not removed). RadiT (1.3B rectified-flow transformer, 512 px, 1.2M radiographs; no release statement) had three pulmonologists judge 50 real vs 50 synthetic images twice, reaching near-chance accuracy with low kappa.
    why: The smallest protocol that passed review is SGD's: two blinded readers, four binary criteria, adjudication plus kappa, on about 240 items. A student version is two readers (one may be a resident), 100-150 edited images, the same four criteria, a 2AFC edited-vs-untouched detectability block (RoentMod's missing negative control), and kappa; this is also the format a KFUPM/JRCAI advisor can request from a hospital contact as a two-hour task. None of these three editors is usable on a 6 GB GPU (RoentMod needs RoentGen weights by request and unreleased code; SGD and RadiT have no code).
    code/data: RoentMod: 'upon publication' (RoentGen weights via form at https://stanfordmimi.github.io/RoentGen/); SGD: none found; RadiT: none found (project page https://radit-project.github.io)

[12] Data-use terms that constrain the reader audit, and the local reader routes verified so far (PhysioNet DUA, CANDID-PTX closure, IAU King Fahd Hospital of the University, JHAH) (2024-2026; PhysioNet Credentialed Health Data License; figshare; IAU and JHAH sites) verified=True
    url: https://physionet.org/content/mimic-cxr-jpg/view-license/2.1.0/
    what: The PhysioNet licence states 'The LICENSEE will not share access to PhysioNet restricted data with anyone else', so MIMIC-CXR images cannot be shown to a reader who is not independently credentialed. CANDID-PTX (19,237 CXRs at 1024 px with pneumothorax, rib-fracture and chest-tube segmentations, nominally CC BY 4.0) is 'not available for access by users outside Health New Zealand' since February 2024 (https://api.figshare.com/v2/articles/14173982), so the obvious RANZCR-free tube-mask source is closed. On routes: King Fahd Hospital of the University (IAU, Khobar; https://www.iau.edu.sa/en/university-hospitals/king-fahd-hospital-of-the-university) is a JCI-accredited academic medical centre with an Academic Affairs and Training section and SCFHS 'Gold Category' training status, and IAU's ethics process runs through its Deanship of Scientific Research and SCRELC (https://www.iau.edu.sa/en/scientific-research-innovation/research-ethics-on-living-creatures); JHAH's residency page (https://www.jhah.com/en/careers/residencies-and-training/) lists no radiology residency and no research-office contact, though it hosts Johns Hopkins Medicine on-site rotations. The KFUPM JRCAI site (jrcai.kfupm.edu.sa) did not resolve today and no SDAIA health partner could be verified.
    why: Determines where the edited images may come from if a reader is involved: CheXpert images require the reader to register with Stanford (terms not re-verified), MIMIC requires the reader to be credentialed, NIH ChestX-ray14 has no device labels but no sharing barrier (terms not re-verified this session). The most realistic route for a KFUPM undergraduate is an advisor-brokered introduction to a KFHU radiology resident or attending with the reading done inside a 2-hour blinded session on images the reader is entitled to view, and an ethics check with KFUPM/IAU for a study using clinicians as participants.
    code/data: PhysioNet licence text (fetched); CANDID-PTX figshare API record (fetched); IAU and JHAH pages (fetched); RANZCR Kaggle rules and CheXpert agreement pages not readable without login

-- gaps_and_opportunities --
  * No paper measures LaMa, MAT or SD-inpainting artefact detectability on radiographs; the only detectability number on CXRs is Sogancioglu 2018's 2AFC for three 2018 inpainters on 64x64 holes. A 2AFC on 100 edited crops with two raters would be a new, citable measurement on its own.
  * Every 2024-2026 CXR editor validates device edits by classifier flip (MedDiME, RadEdit, Latent Drifting) or keeps devices rather than removing them (SGD's 99.2% device retention, RadEdit's chest-drain keep mask); a removal arm audited by blinded readers against untouched negative controls is absent from the literature.
  * Edit-free alternative to inpainting: MIMIC-CXR and CheXpert contain multiple studies per patient, so same-patient pairs with a line or tube present in one study and absent in another give a device-absent 'twin' with no inpainting artefacts at all (RoentMod already uses real follow-ups as its anatomy-preservation comparator). Nobody has used such longitudinal pairs as the negative arm of a hallucination benchmark.
  * Verifier independence is achievable by construction: source edited images from CheXpert, make masks with the RANZCR-trained ianpan segmenter, and verify absence with CheXzero (MIMIC-only) and MedSigLIP (no RANZCR, no CheXpert listed); no published pipeline does this split.
  * Cheff's 256-px inpainting notebook is the only CXR-trained remover designed to run on a small GPU; a polite request to the author (issue #13) or a Zenodo re-host would unlock it, and the notebook's DDIM-100 latent masking could be re-created with RoentGen weights at 512 px if the form request succeeds.
  * The 'Rad-Phi4-Vision-CXR' and 'CCD toolkit 13-type device detector' named in the brief could not be located (HF search for rad-phi4 returns nothing; StanfordAIMI/RadPhi-2 is text-only); if they exist they are not public under those names.

-- recommended_for_this_project --
  * Drop the assumption that LaMa or Cheff carries the edited arm. Run a three-remover pilot on 40 device-positive CheXpert images on the RunPod pod: LaMa (IOPaint, grayscale replicated to 3 channels), SD 1.5 inpainting (fp32, attention slicing; prompt 'clear lung, no tube', negative 'tube, line, catheter'), and RadEdit at 512 px with edit_mask = dilated ianpan device mask and keep_mask = inverse. Keep the remover that passes the gate below; if none passes, switch the negative arm to real same-patient device-absent studies (edit-free).
  * Gate for edit validity (pre-register it): (1) both independent verifiers agree the device is absent (CheXzero 'support devices' logit and MedSigLIP zero-shot score both below the 90th percentile of real device-negative images), (2) a 2AFC edited-vs-untouched crop test by the student and one peer gives accuracy not significantly above 50% on the Sogancioglu design, (3) a two-reader blinded audit on 100-150 surviving images using SGD's four criteria (target removed, no unintended change, no structural artefact, joint validity) with adjudication and Cohen's kappa, including 30-50 untouched images as negative controls that RoentMod omitted.
  * Choose the image source for verifier independence and reader access: CheXpert (device label available; ianpan, CheXzero and MedSigLIP all out-of-sample) if the reader can register with Stanford; otherwise NIH ChestX-ray14 (no DUA barrier, but ianpan's presence head saw 31,448 NIH images, so use it only for masks and selection). Do not use RANZCR CLiP images for anything that ianpan scores, and do not show MIMIC-CXR images to any reader who is not personally credentialed.
  * Use ianpan/cxr-lines-and-tubes only as mask generator and item selector; report its in-sample status explicitly and never as the removal oracle. Replace the torchxrayvision 'Support Devices' head in the plan with CheXzero + MedSigLIP; torchxrayvision has no such head.
  * Reader route: ask Dr. Behzad to broker one radiology resident or attending at IAU's King Fahd Hospital of the University (Khobar, JCI academic medical centre, SCFHS training site) or JHAH for a single two-hour blinded session; prepare a web-based reading form, consent text and a one-page ethics note (IAU's SCRELC/DSR process exists; check KFUPM's own IRB for clinician participants). Avoid online second-opinion services: they are patient services, cost money, and would breach the PhysioNet non-sharing clause for MIMIC images.
  * Cite and position: Sogancioglu 2018 and Le 2020 as the CXR removal precedents, MaskMedPaint for LaMa on CXRs, MedDiME/Latent Drifting/RadEdit for classifier-only validation of removal, SGD for the two-reader protocol and the 82.1% RadEdit validity figure, RoentMod for the missing negative-control admission, and CANDID-PTX closure and the PhysioNet clause as the reasons a leak-free, reader-auditable edited arm has not been built.
  * Budget and schedule: the remover pilot (120 edits) and verifier runs fit in about 1-2 pod hours (<$1) plus LaMa locally; the reader session costs nothing but needs two weeks of lead time, so send the request in Week 1 and make the edited arm a gated extension with a kill date (end of Week 3) rather than a core dependency of the Nov 8 report.

-- cautions --
  * The shared WebSearch budget was exhausted at the start of this task; everything here comes from direct fetches of arXiv, GitHub, Hugging Face, PhysioNet, figshare and institutional pages. Items that could not be located without search are marked unverified: the cxr-grounding precedent quoted by the parent (not re-opened here), the 'CCD toolkit' 13-type device detector, 'Rad-Phi4-Vision-CXR', SDAIA health partners, and online second-opinion platforms.
  * VRAM statements are estimates, not measurements: RadEdit's 1.76 GB U-Net plus SDXL-VAE and BioViL-T at 512 px in fp32 is likely 4-6 GB and may not fit the GTX 1060; Cheff's 256-px inpainting would likely fit but its weights are 404 (checked 2026-10-12) with no mirror; SGD (SDXL 1024 px), RadiT (1.3B, 512 px) and MedDiME (A100 80GB reference) have no public code.
  * RoentMod's code and models are 'available upon publication' and RoentGen weights require a Google Form request; neither was confirmed obtainable. RoentMod is an adder (img2img), not a remover.
  * MedSigLIP's 0.852 'Support Devices' AUC is on 518 unnamed 'ELIXR CXR evaluation' examples; CheXzero's per-label Support-Devices AUC could not be read (Nature page paywalled); CheXagent-2-3b's training provenance beyond the CheXagent v1 paper is unknown, and the source of its chest-tube task is not attributed.
  * CheXpert's Stanford agreement, RANZCR CLiP's Kaggle rules and NIH ChestX-ray14's terms could not be fetched (login-gated or 403); the statements about reader access to those datasets are from general knowledge and must be checked before any image is shown to a reader.
  * LaMa's README names Places and CelebA-HQ as training data and RGB input; the Apache-2.0 licence attributed to it in the project digest was not re-verified in this session. IOPaint (the LaMa wrapper in the plan) was archived read-only on Aug 13, 2025.
  * The KFUPM JRCAI website did not resolve from this machine, and JHAH's research/IRB pages returned 404; the JHAH and IAU facts quoted are limited to what their public pages state.

-- confidence_notes -- High confidence (primary pages opened today, 2026-10-12): all arXiv abstracts and HTML full texts cited; Cheff README, notebook, issue #13 and the 404 status of two weight links; RadEdit model card, file tree and paper; ianpan model card and file tree; CheXzero zero_shot.py label list; torchxrayvision README and CheXpert baseline source; MedSigLIP card and HAI-DEF terms; CheXagent README and paper dataset list; PhysioNet licence clause; CANDID-PTX figshare record; IAU and JHAH pages; RoentMod full text via local PDF extraction. Medium confidence: XReal weight hosting (SharePoint link described in README, not opened); MaskMedPaint code availability (repo listing only). Low confidence or unverified: VRAM estimates, the cxr-grounding quote, the CCD toolkit and Rad-Phi4-Vision-CXR identities, dataset terms for CheXpert, RANZCR and NIH, any SDAIA hospital partnership, and second-opinion platform terms.


######## ANGLE: FILL: References in the advisor's own framework that nobody resolved: Kim et al. 2025 (Med-HALT), MedHallBench, 'CXR-VisHal', plus GEMeX and ReXErr-v1 ########
angle: Resolving the references inside the advisor's own framework (Kim et al./Med-HALT, MedHallBench, 'CXR-VisHal', Brooks & Anastasio, DREAM) and the two groundable CXR resources (GEMeX, ReXErr-v1) so a chest-X-ray device-hallucination report can cite them correctly and position itself against Alshahrani & Behzad 2026 (arXiv 2606.13211). Verification used direct primary-source fetches (arXiv abs/HTML, PhysioNet, Hugging Face and GitHub APIs, Crossref, Europe PMC) plus text extraction from the advisor's and Kim et al.'s PDFs, because the shared web-search budget was exhausted after the first query.

[1] Alshahrani & Behzad 2026, 'Hallucination in Medical Imaging AI: A Cross-Modality Analytical Framework for Taxonomy, Detection, and Mitigation under Regulatory Constraints' (the advisor's paper) (2026; arXiv cs.AI, submitted 11 Jun 2026; 12 pages, 45 references) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13211
    what: Literature synthesis across CT, MRI, PET/SPECT, ultrasound and digital pathology that unifies three taxonomies ([3] Brooks & Anastasio, [1] DREAM Report, [4] 'MediHall' = Med-HallMark) and builds a weighted benchmark composite. Verbatim from the PDF: abstract 'median hallucination-free rate 76.6% vs. 51.3%; p=0.012'; Results 'On the Med-HALT benchmark (February 2025), general-purpose models achieved a median hallucination-free rate of 76.6% compared to 51.3% ... (Mann-Whitney U=27.0, p=0.012). Gemini 2.5 Pro achieved 87.6%' (no inline marker; Fig. 3 caption cites [2] Kim et al.); Sec. 4.2 repeats it as 'p<0.001'. 'CXR-VisHal' appears four times, every time cited to [45] ('MedHallBench ... arXiv 2412.18947', listed with no authors and a title that does not match the arXiv record) and described as a localization/grounding benchmark; 'Med-HallMark (2024) ... over 20,000 QA pairs' is also cited to [45] instead of [4]; [1] carries PMCID PMC11844622, which resolves to an unrelated MRI motion-correction preprint.
    why: The student's report inherits the 76.6/51.3 claim and must state its true provenance (text-only LLMs on Med-HALT, 350 sampled items) and fix refs [1], [4]-attribution and [45] before co-authoring; the paper contains no object-level CXR experiment and no abstention scoring, which is exactly the gap a matched real/edited/generated device-existence study fills.
    code/data: none (survey); PDF 2.2 MB from arxiv.org/pdf/2606.13211

[2] Kim et al. 2025, 'Medical Hallucinations in Foundation Models and Their Impact on Healthcare' (source of the 76.6% vs 51.3% figure) (2025 (v1 26 Feb 2025; v2 2 Nov 2025); arXiv cs.CL (also medRxiv 10.1101/2025.02.28.25323115); no journal version found) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.05777
    what: v2 evaluates 11 text LLMs - general: Gemini-2.5 Pro, DeepSeek-R1, o3-mini, GPT-5, o1, GPT-4o, GPT-4o mini; medical: MedGemma, PMC-LLaMA, MedAlpaca-13B, AlpaCare-13B - on 350 items ('we sampled 50 examples from each of seven medical reasoning tasks (350 total cases)' of Med-HALT: False Confidence, None-of-the-Above, Fake Questions, Abstract-to-Link, PMID-to-Title, Title-to-Link, Link-to-Title), with CoT, search-augmented and system-prompt variants, a physician audit of NEJM cases and a 70-clinician survey. Verbatim: 'general-purpose models demonstrated substantially higher median baseline resistance than medical-purpose models (76.6% vs. 51.3%, average difference 25.2%, 95% CI: [18.7%, 31.3%], U=27.0, p=0.012, two-tailed Mann-Whitney test, rank-biserial r=-0.64)'. The same paragraph prints baselines gemini-2.5-pro 87.6, deepseek-r1 86.6, o3-mini 80.4, gpt-5 71.2, o1 64.0, gpt-4o 54.4, gpt-4o-mini 48.3 and medgemma 52.6, pmc-llama 40.8, medalpaca 32.0, alpacare 28.6, whose medians are 71.2 and 36.4, not 76.6/51.3 (U=27 of a maximum 28 is consistent with those values). No vision-language model and no image task is run; VLM benchmarks (Med-VH, HALT-MedVQA, Med-HallMark) appear only in its survey Table 3. The v1 abstract (Feb 2025) contains neither number.
    why: This is the only primary source of the advisor's headline claim and it is text-only, so the student's CXR object-existence experiment is the first image-grounded test of 'general beats medical'; its Med-HALT scoring (penalised Pointwise Score) is the precedent to contrast with confidence-target abstention scoring.
    code/data: paper only; underlying data = Med-HALT on Hugging Face (openlifescienceai/Med-HALT, Apache-2.0)

[3] Med-HALT: Medical Domain Hallucination Test for Large Language Models (Pal, Umapathi, Sankarasubbu) (2023; CoNLL 2023, pp. 314-334 (arXiv 2307.15343)) verified=True
    url: https://aclanthology.org/2023.conll-1.21/
    what: Text-only benchmark built from multinational medical-exam MCQs: Reasoning Hallucination Tests (False Confidence 18.9k rows, None-of-the-Above 18.9k, Fake Questions 1.86k) and Memory Hallucination Tests (four PubMed abstract/PMID/title/link mappings, 4.92k each); 59,254 rows in total. Scored with the Pointwise Score (+1 correct, -0.25 wrong, averaged) and accuracy; original models Text-Davinci, GPT-3.5, LLaMA-2, MPT, Falcon.
    why: The benchmark behind 76.6/51.3; its None-of-the-Above test and penalised score are the text-domain ancestors of abstention-aware scoring, so the report should cite it when introducing Effective Reliability and the Kalai confidence-target instruction, while noting it has no images.
    code/data: https://huggingface.co/datasets/openlifescienceai/Med-HALT (Apache-2.0); https://github.com/medhalt/medhalt

[4] MedHallBench: A New Benchmark for Assessing Hallucination in Medical Large Language Models (Zuo & Jiang) - the advisor's reference [45] (2024/2025 (v1 25 Dec 2024; v4 28 Mar 2025); AAAI-25 Bridge Program (arXiv comments field)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.18947
    what: Multimodal: textual case scenarios plus imaging with 'particular emphasis on chest X-ray interpretation' (sources MIMIC-CXR, MedQA/USMLE, MultiMedQA). Metric ACHMI = 'Assessment of Caption Hallucinations in Medical Imagery': ACHMI_I = proportion of hallucinated objects among all generated components, ACHMI_S = proportion of captions containing any hallucinated component; annotation combines expert review and an RL-assisted scheme. Nine LVLMs evaluated (BLIP2, InstructBLIP-7b/13b, LLaVA-1.5-7b/13b, mPLUG-Owl2, XrayGPT, MiniGPT4, RadFM, LLaVA-Med). No code, dataset, GitHub or Hugging Face release was found, and the string 'CXR-VisHal' does not appear anywhere in it.
    why: ACHMI_I is an object-level caption hallucination rate on CXR, the closest published metric to object-existence scoring, but with nothing released it can only be cited; the advisor's [45] mis-titles it, omits the authors, and wrongly carries the CXR-VisHal and Med-HallMark claims.
    code/data: none found

[5] MedHEval (Chang, Huang, Bhatia, Kass-Hout, Ma, Xiao) - the actual home of 'CXR-VisHal' (2025 (4 Mar 2025); arXiv cs.CV; no acceptance note found; code https://github.com/Aofei-Chang/MedHEval) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02157
    what: 15,976 close- and open-ended VQA pairs organised by root cause: visual misinterpretation (9,197 closed from SLAKE/VQA-RAD/IU-Xray/MIMIC-CXR + 490 open from MIMIC-CXR), knowledge deficiency (1,972 closed + 2,317 open, MIMIC-CXR) and context misalignment (2,000, MIMIC-CXR/MIMIC-IV). The visual set is split into MM-VisHal ('3,610 VQA pairs with 494 medical images', SLAKE + VQA-RAD) and CXR-VisHal ('focuses specifically on chest X-ray interpretation, using images and radiology reports from IU-Xray and MIMIC-CXR ... 5,587 VQA pairs across 790 chest X-ray images'); questions are GPT-4-generated and typed Anatomy/Measurement/Symptom/Radiology-feature, closed items scored by accuracy (Acc-A/M/S/R), open items by CHAIR. 11 (Med)-LVLMs (GPT-4o, LLaVA-NeXT 7B/13B, MiniGPT-4, LLaVA-Med 1/1.5, LLM-CXR, Med-Flamingo, RadFM, CheXagent, XrayGPT) and 7 mitigation methods (VCD, OPERA, DoLa, AVISC, M3ID, DAMRO, PAI) with 'limited effectiveness'. There is no bounding-box or localisation task.
    why: 'CXR-VisHal' is real but is a QA subset of MedHEval, so it must be cited as Chang et al. 2025 (not [45]) and not described as a grounding benchmark; its finding that decoding-time fixes barely help on CXR is the published reason to make grounding/retrieval the mitigation family, and its released code is reusable for VCD/OPERA baselines.
    code/data: https://github.com/Aofei-Chang/MedHEval (licence not stated); images from MIMIC-CXR-JPG (PhysioNet credentialed) and IU-Xray

[6] Brooks & Anastasio, 'A Taxonomy of Machine Hallucination in Radiology' (2026; Radiology: Artificial Intelligence 8(2):e250203, published 1 Mar 2026; PMID 41811081 (Crossref and Europe PMC records)) verified=True
    url: https://doi.org/10.1148/ryai.250203
    what: Short article from the University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign bioengineering group arguing that 'there are multiple widely used notions of what constitutes hallucination' and proposing a taxonomy to disambiguate them for clinicians, researchers and regulators (MeSH: Hallucinations/classification; Radiology; Generative AI). Full text is subscription-only (RSNA returned 403), so the advisor's paraphrase (an etiological axis separating data-related failures from model confabulation 'with no referent in the input image') could not be checked against the source.
    why: The advisor's first taxonomy; the student's attribution of removed-object 'yes' answers to residual image traces versus the language prior is naturally a data-versus-confabulation distinction and should be framed in this paper's vocabulary once read through KFUPM library access.
    code/data: none (paywalled; abstract via Europe PMC)

[7] Xia et al., 'On Hallucinations in AI-Generated Content for Nuclear Medicine Imaging (the DREAM Report)' (2025/2026; Journal of Nuclear Medicine 2026;67(2):166-174, online 6 Nov 2025, DOI 10.2967/jnumed.125.270653, PMID 41198241, PMCID PMC12866389; arXiv v1 16 Jun 2025, v3 10 Nov 2025) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13995
    what: Position statement (Yale, UC Davis, MGH/Harvard; NIH-funded) on hallucinations in AI-generated PET/SPECT image content, defined as 'AI-generated abnormalities or artifacts that appear visually realistic and highly plausible, yet are factually false when compared to reference scans'; it explicitly excludes lesion omission and uniform quantitative bias, and is organised as Definition, Representative Examples, Detection and Evaluation, Causes and Mitigation. It does not propose a formal type taxonomy (factual vs other).
    why: DREAM is about hallucinated pixels in generated images, not VLM answers, so it is the right citation for validating the generated/edited X-ray arm (a generator that paints in a plausible tube is a DREAM-type hallucination in the stimulus). The advisor's reference [1] gives a wrong PMCID (PMC11844622 is an unrelated MRI motion-correction preprint) and attributes SUV/uptake-pattern categories that the report does not contain.
    code/data: none

[8] GEMeX: A Large-Scale, Groundable, and Explainable Medical VQA Benchmark for Chest X-ray Diagnosis (Liu et al.) (2024/2025; ICCV 2025 (per project GitHub); arXiv v2 23 Mar 2025; project https://www.med-vqa.com/GEMeX) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.16778
    what: 151,025 chest X-rays taken from Chest ImaGenome (hence MIMIC-CXR) with 1,605,575 QA pairs in four formats (open-ended, closed-ended, single-choice, multi-choice) across 77 question categories (abnormality, disease and location >88%; also cause, size, severity, implication); every item has a textual reason and visual regions with bounding boxes; split train 149,535 images/1,588,975 QA, val 1,190/12,640, test 300/3,960. Built by OpenBioLLM-70B report re-grounding plus GPT-4o generation with radiologist-reviewed demonstrations; metrics A-score (accuracy) and V-score (mIoU with Hungarian matching); 12 baselines incl. LLaVA-Med, RadFM, XrayGPT, GPT-4o-mini, DeepSeek-VL, Qwen-VL-Chat, plus fine-tuned LLaVA-Med-GEMeX. Hugging Face viewer examples include endotracheal tube, nasogastric tube, IJ Cordis/central line, pacemaker, sternotomy wires and tracheostomy, so device questions exist although they are not a separate category.
    why: The only CXR VQA set with per-question boxes and device mentions: filter its closed-ended/choice questions by device terms to obtain real-image device questions with grounding targets, and reuse the boxes as LaMa removal masks for the edited arm; it has no abstention option, which is the student's addition.
    code/data: https://huggingface.co/datasets/BoKelvin/GEMeX-VQA (ungated JSONL: question, type, answer, reason, visual_regions, visual_locations, image_path; no licence tag; viewer shows a cast error on multi_choice 'choices'); https://github.com/Awenbocc/GEMeX-Project; images require PhysioNet credentialed MIMIC-CXR/Chest ImaGenome

[9] Chest ImaGenome v1.0.0 and MIMIC-CXR-JPG v2.1.0 (PhysioNet) - the credentialed image, box and device-label source behind GEMeX, MedHEval and ReXErr (2021 / 2024; PhysioNet; Wu et al. 2021; Johnson et al. 2024 (https://physionet.org/content/mimic-cxr-jpg/2.1.0/, DOI 10.13026/jsn5-t979)) verified=True
    url: https://physionet.org/content/chest-imagenome/1.0.0/
    what: Chest ImaGenome: 242,072 automatically built scene graphs for frontal MIMIC-CXR images with bounding boxes for 29 anatomical locations and attribute labels that include tubes/lines and devices, plus a 500-patient gold-standard set. MIMIC-CXR-JPG v2.1.0: 377,110 JPGs, 227,827 studies, CheXpert/NegBio 14-label set including 'Support Devices'. Both are under the PhysioNet Credentialed Health Data License 1.5.0 (credentialed account + CITI 'Data or Specimens Only Research' + signed DUA).
    why: Device attributes with anatomical boxes are the ground truth for device-existence questions and removal masks, and 'Support Devices' gives a coarse presence label for sampling device-free control images; credentialing is the single longest-lead item and must be filed on day one.
    code/data: PhysioNet credentialed access (CITI + DUA) for both

[10] ReXErr-v1: Clinically Meaningful Chest X-Ray Report Errors Derived from MIMIC-CXR (Rao, Zhang, Acosta, Adithan, Rajpurkar) (2024 (paper) / 2025 (data); PhysioNet 1.0.0, 19 Mar 2025, DOI 10.13026/9dns-vd94; paper arXiv 2409.10829 (17 Sep 2024; no venue found)) verified=True
    url: https://physionet.org/content/rexerr-v1/1.0.0/
    what: GPT-4o injects errors into MIMIC-CXR reports (AP/PA/lateral only) with Llama 3.1 screening; 12 categories: Add Medical Device, False Prediction, False Negation, Add Repetitions, Add Contradictions, Change Name of Device, Change Position of Device, Change Severity, Change Location, Change Measurement, Change to Homophone, Add Typo; report-level files (3 errors per report) and sentence-level paired original/error sentences across train/val/test; three board-certified radiologists shaped the error list and 83 of 100 modified reports were judged plausible.
    why: Its three device categories are ready-made templates for the student's distractor questions (absent device, wrong device name, wrong position) and a text-only language-prior control; it is Open Access (ODC-By 1.0, 82.7 MB zip, no login or DUA) so it can be used today, but joining it to images still needs MIMIC-CXR credentials.
    code/data: PhysioNet open access, Open Data Commons Attribution License v1.0

[11] Med-HallMark / MediHallDetector (Chen et al.), 'Detecting and Evaluating Medical Hallucinations in Large Vision Language Models' - the advisor's reference [4] ('MediHall') (2024; arXiv cs.CV (v2 Dec 2024; a later revision is listed Apr 2026); code https://github.com/ydk122024/Med-HallMark) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10185
    what: 6,341 samples (57.57% Med-VQA from SLAKE/VQA-RAD; 42.43% report generation from MIMIC-CXR/OpenI): 2,871 conventional, 1,350 confidence-weakening and 1,320 counterfactual questions plus 1,800 image-depiction samples. Five severity levels - catastrophic (0.0), critical (0.2), attribute (0.4), prompt-induced (0.6), minor (0.8), correct (1.0) - combine into the MediHall Score; MediHallDetector is a fine-tuned LVLM detector; baselines BLIP2, InstructBLIP, LLaVA-1.5, mPLUG-Owl2, XrayGPT, MiniGPT4, RadFM, LLaVA-Med.
    why: This is the severity framework the advisor unifies; a fabricated or missed tube sits at its attribute/catastrophic boundary, so the report can present MediHall-weighted scores beside abstention-aware scores. The advisor's 'over 20,000 QA pairs' and its citation to [45] are both wrong (the paper states 6,341 samples and is [4]).
    code/data: https://github.com/ydk122024/Med-HallMark (licence not checked)

[12] HALT-MedVQA: 'Hallucination Benchmark in Medical Visual Question Answering' (Wu, Kim, Wu) (2024; ICLR 2024 Tiny Papers (Notable); v2 3 Apr 2024) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.05827
    what: Medical VQA hallucination tests that, per Kim et al.'s survey Table 3, comprise FAKE Question, None-of-the-Above and Image SWAP tasks scored by accuracy, with prompting strategies compared; the source datasets, models and code were not confirmed from the primary page in this pass.
    why: The nearest prior to an abstention option (NOTA choice) and to blind/swap controls in medical VQA; it must be cited as precedent and distinguished from confidence-target abstention, Effective Reliability and per-item matched triplets.
    code/data: none found

-- gaps_and_opportunities --
  * No existing CXR benchmark isolates device, tube or line existence with matched edited and generated counterparts: GEMeX has device examples but only 300 test images, no device category and no abstention; MedHEval's CXR-VisHal has no device split and no grounding; MedHallBench is unreleased; ReXErr-v1 is text-only.
  * The advisor's headline claim (general-purpose beats medical-specialized, 76.6% vs 51.3%) rests entirely on text-only LLMs on 350 Med-HALT items; no published study tests whether the gap holds for small open VLMs on image-grounded object questions, which a general-vs-medical VLM pair in the model set would answer directly.
  * Abstention-aware scoring is absent from every CXR hallucination benchmark found (GEMeX, MedHEval, Med-HallMark, MedHallBench); only Med-HALT's -0.25 penalty and HALT-MedVQA's NOTA option exist as partial precedents, so Effective Reliability with Kalai confidence targets on CXR is new.
  * MedHEval already shows VCD, OPERA, DoLa, AVISC, M3ID, DAMRO and PAI have limited effect on CXR VQA, so grounding/retrieval mitigation (region-grounded prompting from Chest ImaGenome boxes, retrieval of device-placement rules or similar reports) is both unexplored and what the umbrella asks for.
  * Kim et al.'s stated medians (76.6/51.3) do not reproduce from the per-model baselines printed in the same paragraph (71.2/36.4); a one-paragraph replication note in the report is a legitimate small contribution and protects the advisor's claim from over-statement.
  * The DREAM definition (realistic but false content in generated images) has not been applied to synthetic benchmark stimuli; auditing the SD/ControlNet generated X-rays for generator-added devices against that definition is a novel validity check.

-- recommended_for_this_project --
  * Cite the 76.6% vs 51.3% figure as Kim et al. 2025, arXiv 2503.05777v2 (Nov 2025), evaluating 11 text LLMs on 350 sampled Med-HALT items (Pal et al., CoNLL 2023); never as a VLM or imaging result, and not to the Feb 2025 medRxiv version, whose v1 abstract lacks the numbers. Report that the direction is robust (U=27 of 28) but the medians do not reproduce from the printed baselines.
  * Correct the advisor's citations in the student's draft and flag them to Dr. Behzad before submission: [45] should read Zuo & Jiang, 'MedHallBench: A New Benchmark for Assessing Hallucination in Medical Large Language Models', arXiv 2412.18947, AAAI-25 Bridge; 'CXR-VisHal' is a subset of MedHEval (Chang et al. 2025, arXiv 2503.02157) and is QA-only, not grounding; Med-HallMark is [4] with 6,341 samples; DREAM is J Nucl Med 2026;67(2):166-174, DOI 10.2967/jnumed.125.270653, PMCID PMC12866389; Brooks & Anastasio is Radiol Artif Intell 2026;8(2):e250203.
  * Decide for chest X-rays: GEMeX (per-question boxes, device mentions, ungated QA JSONL), Chest ImaGenome (device attributes with anatomical boxes) and ReXErr-v1 (device error templates, open access) together supply real-image device questions, removal masks and distractors; no comparable groundable resource was found for education-domain diagrams in this pass.
  * File PhysioNet credentialing (CITI 'Data or Specimens Only Research' + DUA for MIMIC-CXR-JPG and Chest ImaGenome) on day one; meanwhile build the question and distractor pools from the ungated GEMeX-VQA JSONL (load with a JSONL reader, not the HF viewer) and the open ReXErr-v1 files, so the only credential-gated step is image download.
  * Build the real-image item pool by filtering GEMeX closed-ended and choice questions for device terms (endotracheal/ET tube, nasogastric/NG tube, central line/Cordis/PICC, pacemaker, sternotomy wires, tracheostomy, chest tube) and cross-checking against Chest ImaGenome tube/line/device attributes; use the GEMeX visual_locations boxes as the LaMa removal masks for the edited arm and ReXErr's 'Change Name of Device' / 'Change Position of Device' categories to write the hard distractors.
  * Add one medical-specialized small VLM beside the general models so the report directly tests the advisor's general-vs-medical claim at the VLM level (MedGemma is the natural choice because Kim et al. used its text version, but its multimodal model id, size and bf16 fit were not verified here and must be checked).
  * Position mitigation against MedHEval: reuse its released code for VCD/OPERA-style baselines on the same items, then contrast with grounding-based prompting (anatomical region boxes from Chest ImaGenome) and retrieval of device-placement rules, which is the umbrella's requested family and the gap MedHEval leaves.
  * Use three taxonomies as the advisor does, but accurately: Brooks & Anastasio for data-vs-confabulation attribution of removed-object 'yes' answers (read the full text via KFUPM library first), MediHall severity weights for clinical impact, and DREAM only for auditing generator-added devices in the synthetic arm.
  * Cite Med-HALT's penalised Pointwise Score and HALT-MedVQA's NOTA option as the prior abstention mechanisms, and state explicitly that confidence-target instructions with Effective Reliability on matched real/edited/generated CXR triplets have no precedent in the five CXR benchmarks reviewed.

-- cautions --
  * The shared WebSearch budget was exhausted after the first query of this task; every verification above came from direct fetches of arXiv, PhysioNet, Hugging Face and GitHub APIs, Crossref and Europe PMC, plus pypdf text extraction of the advisor's and Kim et al.'s PDFs. The statement that no standalone 'CXR-VisHal' benchmark exists rests on GitHub repository search (0 results), Hugging Face dataset search and arXiv full-field search, not on a general web search.
  * Verbatim-confirmed text: Kim et al.'s median sentence, baseline values, 350-item sampling and Med-HALT task list; the advisor's 76.6%, CXR-VisHal, Brooks/DREAM sentences and reference entries [1]-[5], [45]. Numbers taken from an automated page reader rather than verbatim extraction: GEMeX split sizes and 77 categories, MedHEval Table 2 counts, Med-HallMark counts and severity weights, ReXErr plausibility 83/100, Chest ImaGenome and MIMIC-CXR-JPG counts; re-check these against the papers before quoting.
  * Brooks & Anastasio: only Crossref/Europe PMC metadata and the first abstract sentence were verified; the full text is paywalled, so the advisor's description of its 'etiological axis' is unverified.
  * DREAM Report: the arXiv v1/v2 HTML was read; the JNM version may differ slightly in wording; the advisor's PMCID is wrong (PMC11844622 is an unrelated preprint), the correct one is PMC12866389.
  * Kim et al.: the paper's own medians (76.6/51.3) are inconsistent with the per-model baselines it prints (medians 71.2/36.4) and the reported rank-biserial r=-0.64 does not follow from U=27 with n=7 vs 4; treat the exact figures as reported-by-authors, not as reproducible.
  * GEMeX: no licence is stated on the Hugging Face card or in the paper excerpt; the QA files are ungated but the images are MIMIC-CXR via Chest ImaGenome and need PhysioNet credentialing (typically days to weeks); device questions exist in examples but there is no device category, so the usable device subset size is unknown until filtered.
  * MedHEval: 'CXR-VisHal = 5,587 pairs / 790 images' is the paper's sentence as extracted; the share from IU-Xray vs MIMIC-CXR and the licence of the released files were not determined; no conference acceptance was found.
  * HALT-MedVQA test types come from Kim et al.'s Table 3, not from the primary abstract; MedGemma's multimodal availability and fit on an RTX 3090/4090 pod were not verified in this pass.
  * medRxiv blocked fetching (403), so whether the Feb 2025 medRxiv version contains the 76.6/51.3 numbers is inferred from the arXiv v1 abstract (it does not) rather than observed.

-- confidence_notes -- High confidence on identities, ids, venues and access terms of all 12 items (each opened at a primary source) and on the exact wording of the advisor's and Kim et al.'s key sentences (PDF text extraction). Medium confidence on dataset counts and category lists that came through an automated page reader rather than verbatim quotes. Low confidence on the content of the Brooks & Anastasio taxonomy beyond its abstract, since the full text is paywalled.
